# Spec: waifu-companion

## Objective

An anime-style AI companion named **Cyrene** (renamable in `config.yaml`) that
listens through one USB mic on the owner's Windows PC, responds **only to the
owner's voice**, chats in Indonesian with Japanese phrases, and controls the
house: Tuya Wi-Fi lights and plugs, an IR blaster (Broadlink or Tuya) for the
Samsung AC, Wanbo Dali projector, and TV, and the PC itself.

Hardware budget is ≤ Rp1 juta, so there is no always-on hub: the PC runs
everything. Turning the PC *on* is out of scope for the voice loop (the owner
uses a smart plug + BIOS "power on AC restore" or Wake-on-LAN from a phone).

Since v1.4 she is also the owner's companion for the HoYoverse games they play
every day (Honkai: Star Rail, Genshin Impact, Zenless Zone Zero, Asia server):
live status, reminders by voice and phone push, the HoYoLAB daily check-in, and
pull history with pity (see "gacha buddy (v1.4)").

### User stories

1. "Cyrene, nyalakan lampu kamar" → light turns on, she answers in voice within ~3 s of the owner finishing speaking (local STT + LLM on the RTX 5060 Ti).
2. "Cyrene, AC dua puluh empat derajat dan matikan proyektor" → two device calls, one spoken summary.
3. Right after she answers, "terus nyalakan TV juga" works without the wake word (follow-up window).
4. A guest saying "Cyrene, matikan lampu" is ignored (speaker verification).
5. "Cyrene, matikan komputer" → she asks for confirmation first; on "iya" Windows schedules a 30 s shutdown that "batalkan" can cancel.
6. Without any hardware or GPU, `waifu chat --dry-run` lets the owner type and test the persona and tool calls against fake devices.

## Capability map

| Module id | Responsibility | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| config | Typed `config.yaml` (done: `src/waifu/config.py`) | — |
| devices | Device contract, registry, Tuya, Broadlink, PC, mock, IR code store | config |
| brain | Tool specs from the registry, persona prompt, Claude and Ollama backends | config, devices |
| voice | Mic/speaker I/O, utterance segmenter, speaker verification, STT, TTS | config |
| notify | Push notifications to the owner's phone through ntfy (v1.4) | config |
| gacha | HoYoLAB status and check-in, gacha history and pity, reminders, the game tools for the brain (v1.4) | config, notify, brain (`ToolSpec`, `ToolProvider`) |
| app | Wake gate, listen → verify → transcribe → think → speak loop, CLI | all |

Build order: config → devices, voice → brain → app. `devices` and `voice` are
independent; `brain` only needs `devices.registry`'s public surface. v1.4 adds
config → notify → gacha → app; `gacha` reaches the brain only through the
`ToolProvider` protocol in `brain/tools.py`, and the voice loop only through
`Assistant.announce`.

## Tech Stack

Python 3.14 venv at `.venv` (3.11+ supported). Core: `anthropic` 1.13, `ollama`
0.6, `pydantic` 2, `pyyaml`, `numpy` 2, `tinytuya` 1.20, `broadlink` 0.19,
`wakeonlan` 4, `edge-tts` 7.3.1+ (Microsoft's Oct 2026 token change refuses older
releases with HTTP 403), `soundfile`, `sounddevice`. Voice extra:
`faster-whisper` 1.2 (+ `ctranslate2`), `speechbrain` 1.1, `torch`/`torchaudio`
from the cu128 index (RTX 50-series needs CUDA 12.8+ builds). v1.4 adds `genshin`
(genshin.py) 1.7.31 to the core (unofficial HoYoLAB client, async on `aiohttp`,
which edge-tts already brings; only `tenacity` is new besides it).

## Commands

```
Install core:   .venv\Scripts\python -m pip install -e ".[dev]"
Install voice:  .venv\Scripts\python -m pip install torch torchaudio --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu128
                .venv\Scripts\python -m pip install -e ".[voice]"
Test:           .venv\Scripts\python -m pytest
Lint:           .venv\Scripts\python -m ruff check src tests
Format:         .venv\Scripts\python -m ruff format src tests
Run:            .venv\Scripts\waifu run [--config config.yaml] [--dry-run] [--no-speaker-check]
```

## Project Structure

See `CLAUDE.md` → Project Structure. One test module per source module:
`src/waifu/devices/registry.py` → `tests/test_devices_registry.py`.

## Module contracts

The abstract contracts are already committed in `devices/base.py`,
`brain/base.py`, and `voice/base.py`. The concrete public surface below is what
other modules may rely on.

### devices

- `devices/mock.py` — `MockDevice(device_id, name, aliases=None, commands=None, kind="mock")`. Accepts any listed command (default `["on", "off"]`); like `IrDevice`, a `value` with a listed `f"{command}_{value}"` resolves to that command. Records `calls: list[tuple[str, int | None]]` (resolved command, value), updates `state["power"]` for on/off and `state["last_command"]`, returns ok results. Unknown commands raise `DeviceError`. Used by tests and `--dry-run`.
- `devices/ir_codes.py` — `IrCodeStore(path)`: JSON file `{device_id: {command: base64}}`. `get(device_id, command) -> str | None`, `commands(device_id) -> list[str]` (sorted), `put(device_id, command, code)` writes atomically (temp file + `os.replace`), creates the parent folder. Command names must match `^[a-z0-9_]+$`.
- `devices/tuya.py` — `TuyaLight` (commands `on`, `off`, `toggle`, `brightness` with value 1–100), `TuyaPlug` (`on`, `off`, `toggle`), `TuyaIrBlaster` (send/learn base64 codes via `tinytuya.Contrib.IRRemoteControlDevice`). Each takes a `TuyaConnection` and an injectable factory for the tinytuya object; connections are created lazily on first command and use a short socket timeout (≤ 5 s). Tuya error payloads (dicts with `Error`/`Err`) become `DeviceError`.
- `devices/broadlink_ir.py` — `BroadlinkBlaster(host, *, factory=broadlink.hello)`: lazy `auth()`, `send(code_b64)`, `learn(timeout_s=20) -> str` (enter learning, poll `check_data()` until data or timeout). Reconnects once on a socket/auth error.
- `devices/ir_device.py` — `IrDevice(device_id, name, aliases, blaster, store)`: commands are the learned names in the store. `execute(command, value)`: if `value` is given and `f"{command}_{value}"` is learned, send that (so `temp` + 24 → `temp_24`); unknown commands raise `DeviceError` listing what is learned. Tracks `state["last_command"]`. Blaster protocol: `send(code_b64) -> None`, `learn(timeout_s) -> str`.
- `devices/pc.py` — `PcDevice(device_id, name, aliases, conn: PcConnection, *, run, send_wol, suspend, timer_factory)`. Commands: `wake` (needs `mac`), and when `conn.local`: `shutdown` (`shutdown /s /t <delay>`; Windows force-closes apps when the delay is > 0, and the reply says so), `restart` (`shutdown /r /t <delay>`), `cancel_shutdown` (`shutdown /a`, also cancels a pending sleep), `sleep` (PowrProf `SetSuspendState(0, 0, 0)` via ctypes — never rundll32, which cannot pass arguments and hibernates — delayed by a ~5 s daemon timer). **Power interlock:** when `WAIFU_DISABLE_POWER` is set to anything but `0`, every real power call refuses; the test suite sets it in `tests/conftest.py`, which also refuses `shutdown`/`rundll32`/PowrProf in every test.
- `devices/registry.py` — `DeviceRegistry(devices)`: `get(id) -> Device | None`, `all() -> list[Device]`, `describe_all() -> list[dict]`, `execute(device_id, command, value=None) -> DeviceResult` which **never raises** (unknown device, unknown command, `DeviceError`, timeouts, and unexpected exceptions all become `ok=False` with an Indonesian message; unexpected ones are logged with traceback). `build_registry(config: AppConfig, *, dry_run=False) -> DeviceRegistry`: one blaster instance per IR host/Tuya id shared by every IR device on it; `dry_run` swaps every device for a `MockDevice` with the same id, name, aliases, and commands, keeping the real `kind` (IR commands = learned codes ∪ the device's declared `commands`, PC commands from its type and `local` flag). No network or subprocess call happens while building, in either mode.
- For `ir` devices, `DeviceConfig.commands` declares the commands the owner *intends* to learn (e.g. `on`, `off`, `temp_16`…`temp_30`). Real mode only exposes learned ones; `waifu check` lists declared-but-unlearned commands and `waifu learn-ir <id> --missing` walks through them.

### brain

- `brain/tools.py` — `build_tool_specs(registry) -> list[ToolSpec]` with exactly two tools:
  - `control_device` `{device: enum[device ids], command: string, value?: integer}` — description lists each device's commands so the model picks valid ones.
  - `list_devices` `{}` — returns current `describe_all()`.
  Schemas use `additionalProperties: false`, no numeric `minimum`/`maximum` (unsupported by strict tool use). `run_tool(registry, name, arguments) -> tuple[str, bool]` returns a compact JSON string (`ensure_ascii=False`) and `is_error`; unknown tools and invalid arguments are errors, never exceptions. Every schema stays within strict tool use (the sections below call such specs and tools strict). `to_anthropic(spec)` sends `strict: True` only for a spec that requires an argument (every tool that acts on a device, a list, or an account does) and `strict: False` for the reads whose arguments may all be left out: one request takes at most 20 strict tools, 24 optional parameters, and 16 union-typed parameters across its strict schemas (beyond them every request is a 400), and every tool checks its own arguments anyway. A test sends the fully enabled tool set (Claude, the games, routines, every default feature) and checks those limits. `to_ollama(spec)` returns `{"type": "function", "function": {...}}`.
- `brain/persona.py` — `build_system_prompt(persona_text, assistant_name, registry) -> str`: persona with `{name}` filled, plus a stable device list (id, name, aliases, commands — no live state). `format_user_turn(text, now) -> str` prefixes `[<Indonesian weekday>, DD-MM-YYYY HH:MM]` so the model knows the time without editing the system prompt. `load_persona(path) -> str`.
- `brain/session.py` — `SessionClock(idle_minutes, max_turns, clock=time.monotonic)`: `should_reset() -> bool`, `record_turn()`, `reset()`. Shared by both backends.
- `brain/claude.py` — `ClaudeBrain(config: BrainConfig, registry, system_prompt, *, client=None, clock=...)`. Uses `client.beta.messages.create` with model `config.claude.model`, `max_tokens`, `output_config={"effort": ...}`, adaptive thinking by omission, strict tools, top-level `cache_control={"type": "ephemeral"}`, and when `config.claude.fallbacks` is on `betas=["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"]` + `fallbacks="default"`. Manual tool loop up to `max_tool_rounds`; all tool results of one round go back in a single user message. History is **append-only** within a session (preserved-thinking rule): always append the full `response.content`; never edit or trim earlier turns; when a session should end, start a new empty one. Before echoing a response that contains a `fallback` block, drop `thinking`/`redacted_thinking`/`tool_use` blocks that precede the last `fallback` block. A failed turn (API error) is rolled back by truncating to the length before the turn, then a short Indonesian apology is returned. `stop_reason == "refusal"` → apology, turn rolled back. `pause_turn` → resend. Text is the concatenation of `text` blocks of the final response (spacing as in the v1.18 web search section).
- `brain/ollama_brain.py` — `OllamaBrain(config: BrainConfig, registry, system_prompt, *, client=None, clock=...)`. `ollama.Client(host).chat(model, messages, tools=[to_ollama...], think=config.ollama.think, keep_alive=...)`; tool calls from `response.message.tool_calls`; tool results appended as `{"role": "tool", "content": ..., "tool_name": ...}`. Same session, rollback, and apology rules. Strips any `<think>...</think>` text from the final reply.
- `brain/factory.py` — `build_brain(config: AppConfig, registry, *, persona_text=None) -> Brain` (persona read from `assistant.persona_file` when not given). Since v1.6 also `providers` and `memory_section`, and `system_prompt` may be a callable read once per session (see long-term memory).

### voice

- `voice/audio.py` — `list_devices() -> str`, `resample(audio, src_rate, dst_rate) -> np.ndarray` (linear interpolation, float32), `MicStream(sample_rate, frame_ms, device=None)`: context manager; opens `sounddevice.InputStream` at the device's default rate and resamples to `sample_rate`; `frames()` yields fixed-size float32 frames; `pause()`/`resume()` drop audio while the assistant speaks (half-duplex, so she never hears herself). `Player(device=None)`: `play(audio, sample_rate)` blocking.
- `voice/segmenter.py` — `EnergySegmenter(sample_rate, vad: VadConfig, clock=time.monotonic)`: `feed(frame) -> Utterance | None`. Adaptive noise floor (slow EMA of non-speech frame dB); a frame is speech when its dB ≥ max(`threshold_db`, floor + `margin_db`). Keeps `pre_roll_ms` before onset, ends after `silence_ms` of non-speech, force-ends at `max_speech_s`, drops bursts shorter than `min_speech_ms`. `reset()` clears state.
- `voice/speaker.py` — `SpeakerVerifier(embedder: SpeakerEmbedder, voiceprint_path, threshold)`: `enrolled`, `enroll(samples, sample_rate)` (mean of L2-normalised embeddings, renormalised, saved with `np.save`), `score(audio, sr) -> float` (cosine), `verify(audio, sr) -> tuple[bool, float]`. `EcapaEmbedder(model_source, model_dir, device)`: lazy speechbrain `EncoderClassifier.from_hparams(..., run_opts={"device": ...}, local_strategy=LocalStrategy.COPY)` (Windows has no symlinks without admin), resamples to 16 kHz, returns a 1-D numpy embedding.
- `voice/stt.py` — `WhisperStt(config: SttConfig, assistant_name, *, model_factory=None)`: lazy `faster_whisper.WhisperModel(model, device, compute_type, download_root)`; before loading on Windows, register CUDA DLL folders from the `nvidia-*` pip packages and `torch/lib` with `os.add_dll_directory` when present. `transcribe` resamples to 16 kHz, runs with `vad_filter=True`, `condition_on_previous_text=False`, an `initial_prompt` containing the assistant name, auto language; if the detected language is not in `config.languages`, re-runs with `languages[0]`. Drops known Whisper hallucinations (e.g. "Terima kasih telah menonton", "ご視聴ありがとうございました", subtitle credits) and returns an empty transcript for them.
- `voice/tts.py` — `split_by_language(text, default="id") -> list[tuple[str, str]]`: runs of Japanese script (hiragana, katakana, CJK ideographs, Japanese punctuation, long-vowel mark) become `"ja"`, everything else the default; neighbouring punctuation/space stays with the current run; empty runs dropped. `clean_for_speech(text)` removes emoji, markdown, and `*actions*`. `EdgeTts(voice: EdgeVoiceConfig)` (async `edge_tts.Communicate(...).stream()` collected in a sync wrapper, MP3 decoded with `soundfile`). `GptSovitsTts(voice: GptSovitsVoiceConfig, *, http_get=...)` (GET `{url}/tts` with `text`, `text_lang`, `ref_audio_path`, `prompt_text`, `prompt_lang`, `speed_factor`, `media_type=wav`). `SpeechRouter(engines: dict[str, TextToSpeech], default_language)`: `synthesize(text) -> tuple[np.ndarray, int]` cleans, splits, synthesises each run with its language's engine (falling back to the default language's engine), resamples to the highest rate among the runs (linear interpolation has no anti-alias filter, so a run is only ever upsampled: a 16 kHz Windows voice never pulls a 24 kHz edge-tts run down), joins with 120 ms of silence. `build_speech_router(config: TtsConfig)`.

### app

- `gate.py` — `WakeGate(wake_words, followup_seconds, clock=time.monotonic)`: `check(text) -> GateResult(accepted, command)`. Normalises (lowercase, strip punctuation), matches a wake word anywhere with fuzzy tolerance (difflib ratio ≥ 0.8 per word, so "syrene"/"cyrene" match; wake words containing Japanese script match as substrings because Japanese has no spaces), removes it from `command`. Inside the follow-up window any text is accepted unchanged. `open_followup()` after each spoken reply; `close()`.
- `app.py` — `Assistant(config, *, brain, stt, speech, player, gate, verifier=None, speaker_check=True, now=datetime.now)`: `handle_utterance(utt) -> str | None` (verify owner → transcribe → gate → brain → speak → open follow-up; returns the reply text or None when ignored; a bare wake word gets a short acknowledgement like "はい, Master?"), `handle_text(text) -> BrainReply`, `run(mic, segmenter)` (loop with mic paused while speaking; Ctrl+C exits cleanly). Logs each decision (rejected speaker with score, gate reject, transcript, tool calls).
- `cli.py` — `main(argv=None) -> int`, subcommands: `run`, `chat [--speak]`, `enroll [--samples 6 --seconds 4]` (prompts Indonesian phrases to read aloud), `speaker-test` (live scores vs threshold), `devices`, `device <id> <command> [value]`, `learn-ir <device_id> <command>` or `learn-ir <device_id> --missing`, `say <text>`, `mics`, `mic [--seconds 3]` (v1.34.1), `check` (config valid, CUDA available, Ollama reachable or API key present, voiceprint present, IR codes per device). Shared `--config` (default `config.yaml`) and `--dry-run`. `python -m waifu` works via `__main__.py`.

### autostart (v1.1)

User story: the owner logs in to Windows and, without typing anything, Cyrene starts, loads her models, and says she is ready; closing her window stops her; she never runs twice.

- `single_instance.py` — `InstanceLock(path)`: context manager / `acquire() -> bool`, `release()`. Uses an OS byte-range lock on an open file (`msvcrt.locking` on Windows, `fcntl.flock` elsewhere) so a crashed or killed process never leaves a stale lock. Lock file `data/run.lock` (parent created). A second `acquire()` from another process returns False. `waifu run` takes it before loading anything, and `waifu enroll` and `waifu speaker-test` take it too (held while recording), so they never record while a running Cyrene also hears, and obeys, the owner. If taken, the command logs a WARNING, prints "Cyrene sudah berjalan (mungkin dari autostart). Tutup jendelanya dulu, atau jalankan 'waifu autostart status'." and exits 2.
- `autostart.py` — per-user Run key `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run`, value name `Cyrene` (`RUN_VALUE`). `RunKey` protocol: `get(name) -> str | None`, `set(name, command)`, `delete(name) -> bool`; `WindowsRunKey` implements it with `winreg` (REG_SZ, only HKCU, only that key). `build_command(project_dir, waifu_exe, *, delay_s=15, comspec=None) -> str`: `<comspec> /c start "Cyrene" /min /d "<project_dir>" "<waifu_exe>" run --startup-delay <n> --announce --log-file logs\waifu.log`, where `<comspec>` is the `comspec` argument, else `%ComSpec%` (e.g. `C:\WINDOWS\system32\cmd.exe`), else `cmd.exe`, quoted when it contains whitespace (paths quoted for spaces; validated by parsing it back with `subprocess.list2cmdline`-compatible rules in tests). `enable(run_key, command)`, `disable(run_key) -> bool`, `status(run_key, expected_command) -> AutostartStatus(enabled, command, matches_expected)`.
- CLI:
  - `waifu autostart enable [--delay 15] [--force]` refuses (exit 2) when `speaker.enabled` and no voiceprint, or `config.yaml` is missing, unless `--force`; prints the command and how to undo it (`waifu autostart disable`; Task Manager → Startup apps lists the entry as "Windows Command Processor", cmd.exe's description, and a disable made there is invisible to `enable` and `status`). `project_dir` = current working directory resolved; `waifu_exe` = `<sys.prefix>\Scripts\waifu.exe` (must exist).
  - `waifu autostart disable` removes the value (ok if absent).
  - `waifu autostart status` prints enabled/disabled, the stored command, whether it matches what `enable` would write from the current folder and venv (keeping the `--startup-delay` already stored, so only a moved project or venv counts as stale), whether an instance is running (lock held), and the log path with its last 5 lines when present. The lock and the log are checked in the folder the stored command starts in (its `start /d` folder, when it exists; else the current folder). Run from another folder without a `config.yaml`, it says to run it from that project folder instead of calling the command stale.
  - `waifu run --startup-delay N` (default 0) sleeps N seconds first (logged); `--announce` speaks "<name> siap, Master." after everything is loaded. The announcement is not a reply: it leaves the follow-up window closed, so only the wake word starts a conversation.
  - `run` preloads models before printing/speaking that it is ready: `WhisperStt.preload()` and the speaker embedder (`SpeakerVerifier.preload()` → `EcapaEmbedder` load), so the first command is not delayed ~30 s.
  - Global `--log-file PATH`: also log to a rotating file (1 MB × 3, UTF-8, parent created) in addition to the console. Every error that ends a command (config error, missing package, unexpected exception) is logged at ERROR as well as printed, with the traceback only under `-v`, so a closed autostart window leaves its reason in the log.
- Never touched by tests: the real Run key and `reg.exe`/`schtasks` (guarded in `tests/conftest.py`), the real lock file outside `tmp_path`.

### anime voice (v1.2)

User story: everything Cyrene says, Indonesian and Japanese, comes out in one anime character voice (つくよみちゃん, trained locally with RVC from the free Tsukuyomi-chan corpus, ~11 min); if the voice converter is unavailable she still speaks with the plain edge-tts voice.

Voice rights: the corpus terms allow private use without restriction when neither the voice nor audio made from it is published. The model, corpus, and converted audio stay under `C:\AI` (outside the repo, never committed or redistributed). README tells the owner to credit "つくよみちゃんコーパス（CV.夢前黎）" and read the terms before publishing any audio.

Architecture: Applio (MIT, its own Python 3.12 env at `tts.rvc.applio_dir`) does the conversion in a long-lived local HTTP server so the model loads once; Cyrene (Python 3.14) talks to it over `127.0.0.1`.

- `tools/rvc_server.py` — runs under Applio's python (`tts.rvc.python_path`), stdlib `http.server` only plus Applio. `python tools/rvc_server.py --applio-dir D --model M.pth [--index I.index] --host 127.0.0.1 --port 9881`. Applio is imported lazily inside `load_applio_converter(applio_dir)` (adds it to `sys.path`, `os.chdir` into it because Applio resolves its asset paths relative to the CWD) so the module imports without Applio for tests. `make_handler(converter, model_name)` builds the request handler: `GET /health` → 200 JSON `{"ok": true, "model": ...}`; `POST /convert?pitch=&index_rate=&protect=` with a WAV body (≤ 20 MB, else 413) → 200 `audio/wav`; bad query/body → 400 JSON error; converter failure → 500 JSON error (logged); a body shorter than its Content-Length → 400; a socket timeout (30 s) keeps a stalled client from blocking the server. Converter contract: `convert(wav_bytes, *, pitch, index_rate, protect) -> wav_bytes`; the Applio implementation writes temp files and calls `VoiceConverter.convert_audio(..., f0_method="rmvpe", embedder_model="contentvec")`, warming up once at start with 1 s of quiet harmonic tone (digital silence trips a CUDA device assert in the pitch embedding, which killed the server on the owner RTX 5060 Ti); all-silent input is returned unchanged without calling Applio. Applio replaces "trained" with "added" anywhere in the index path and silently converts without an index when that path does not exist, so `load_applio_converter` refuses (ValueError, before importing Applio) an index whose rewritten path is missing, and keeps a legacy `trained_*.index` whose `added_*.index` exists. Binds only to the given host (default loopback). One request at a time (`HTTPServer`, not threading).
- `src/waifu/voice/rvc.py` — `RvcError`, and its subclass `RvcUnavailableError` (the server could not be reached or did not answer in time). `RvcClient(config: RvcConfig, *, http_post=None, http_get=None)`: `served_model() -> str | None` (the `model` of a `GET /health` reply `{"ok": true, "model": ...}`; None when nothing answers or what answers is not the RVC server), `convert(audio, sample_rate) -> (audio, sample_rate)` (WAV via soundfile, POST with the configured pitch/index_rate/protect, timeout 5 s + 0.5 × clip length, at most 30 s, decode; transport errors, including `http.client.HTTPException`, → `RvcUnavailableError`). The default transport bypasses any proxy. `require_loopback_host(host)`: `RvcError` unless `tts.rvc.host` is an IPv4 loopback address (the server has no password; `localhost` costs ~2 s per request on Windows). `require_served_model(config, served, base_url)`: `RvcError` (Indonesian, "stop the old server") unless the served model file name equals `tts.rvc.model`'s. `RvcServerProcess(config: RvcConfig, *, popen=subprocess.Popen, client=None, clock=time.monotonic, sleep=time.sleep, log_path=Path("logs/rvc_server.log"), lock_path=Path("data/rvc_server.lock"), server_script=None, job_factory=None)`: `ensure_running() -> None` checks the host, then takes the start lock (`InstanceLock` on `lock_path`; when another command holds it, waits up to `startup_timeout_s` for that command's server instead of spawning a second one). Holding it, it reuses a server on the port that serves the configured model (another model → `RvcError`), else checks `python_path`/`model`/`index`/`tools/rvc_server.py` exist (`RvcError` with an Indonesian hint), spawns it (cwd `applio_dir`, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` when available, stdout/stderr appended to the log, environment plus `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` and `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`), puts it in a job from `job_factory` (default `KillOnCloseJob` on Windows: a job object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` whose only handle Cyrene holds, so the OS kills the server, and the processes it started, however Cyrene ends, including a closed console window, which ends Python without running `finally`; a job that cannot be made or joined is logged as a warning, not fatal), polls `served_model()` until `startup_timeout_s` (process exit or timeout → `RvcError` naming the log). A server that answers after its own child exited is treated as reused, not owned. `stop()` terminates the child it started (never a server it merely reused) and closes its job; context manager. The repo path of `tools/rvc_server.py` is resolved from the package location.
- `voice/tts.py` — `SpeechRouter(engines, default_language, *, converter=None, clock=time.monotonic)`: after joining the runs, `converter.convert(audio, sr)` when set; an `RvcError`/`OSError`/timeout is logged once per failure streak and the unconverted audio is returned (she must never go silent because of the voice converter). After an unreachable converter (`RvcUnavailableError` or `OSError`), replies skip it for 60 s (`CONVERSION_RETRY_S`) and then try it again; an error from a server that answered (HTTP 500) causes no pause. `build_speech_router(config: TtsConfig, *, converter=None)`.
- CLI: `run`, `say`, and `chat --speak` start the server via `RvcServerProcess` when `tts.rvc.enabled` (inside the instance lock for `run`, before "ready"/announce, as part of preloading) and stop it on exit; `say --raw` skips conversion for an A/B comparison; `say` without `--raw` prints a one-line warning when `tts.rvc.model` is set but `tts.rvc.enabled` is false. `check` reports RVC: disabled, or each path present, a host that is not IPv4 loopback (✗, no probe), and whether a server answers on the port and with which model (✗ when it serves another model than `tts.rvc.model`; no spawning). `--dry-run` does not affect voice.
- `tests/conftest.py` refuses spawning `rvc_server.py` for real; tests use fake popen/http/converter/job and a start lock under `tmp_path`. No test loads Applio or a model; the only real process is a throwaway `python -c "sleep"` child that a Windows-only test puts in a real `KillOnCloseJob` and ends by closing it.

### avatar (v1.3)

User story: while Cyrene runs, a 3D anime girl stands on the owner's desktop (transparent, always on top, draggable). She breathes, blinks, and looks at the owner when idle, looks thoughtful while thinking, moves her mouth in sync with every spoken reply, and shows the reply's emotion on her face. Closing the mascot does not stop Cyrene.

Model rights: the official Tsukuyomi-chan 3D model "Type A" (VRM 0.x, UniVRM 0.62, © Rei Yumesaki, https://tyc.rei-yumesaki.net/material/avatar/3d-a/) allows personal use; software that ships it must credit "つくよみちゃん（© Rei Yumesaki）". The VRM stays under `C:\AI\avatars\` (never committed); the page always shows a small credit line. Its presets: visemes `a i u e o`, `joy angry sorrow fun neutral`, `blink blink_l blink_r`, look `up down left right`, custom `Surprised`; no animations (pose and idle motion are procedural).

Architecture: Cyrene runs a loopback HTTP server (`avatar.host`/`avatar.port`, default 127.0.0.1:9882) that serves the viewer page, the VRM, and a Server-Sent Events stream of avatar events. The page renders with three.js 0.186 + @pixiv/three-vrm 3.5 (vendored, MIT, `src/waifu/avatar/web/vendor/`, see VENDOR.md, mapped by an import map). The mascot is an Electron 44 shell (`avatar-app/`) loading that page in a transparent frameless window; `window: app` uses an Edge app window instead; `window: none` only serves the page.

Event protocol (SSE `data:` lines, one JSON object each; `type` decides the rest):
- `{"type":"hello","model":"/model.vrm","name":"Cyrene","framing":"full","credit":"つくよみちゃん（© Rei Yumesaki）"}` — first message on every connection, followed by the last `state` and, while a reply is still playing, its `speak` event with `elapsed`.
- `{"type":"state","state":"idle"|"listening"|"thinking"|"speaking","followup_s":8}` — `followup_s` only with `listening` (after a reply, while the follow-up window is open; the page returns to idle by itself after it). With `assistant.followup_seconds: 0` there is no window, so a reply ends in `idle`; the page also treats `followup_s` ≤ 0 as idle at once and a missing one as listening until the next state.
- `{"type":"speak","emotion":"happy","fps":60,"duration":3.42,"visemes":[[aa,ih,ou,ee,oh],...]}` — published immediately before playback starts; weights 0..1 rounded to 2 decimals; the page plays the track from the moment it receives it and returns to a neutral mouth after `duration`. A viewer that subscribes while it plays gets it replayed with `"elapsed":0.75` (s already played, 3 decimals); the page starts the track that much earlier.
- `{"type":"emotion","emotion":"happy","hold_s":4}`.
Emotion names: `neutral happy sad angry surprised relaxed` (the page maps them to VRM expressions `neutral happy sad angry Surprised relaxed`; three-vrm maps the VRM 0.x presets joy/sorrow/fun to happy/sad/relaxed).

Python (`src/waifu/avatar/`):
- `emotion.py` — `EMOTIONS` (the six names). `split_emotion(text) -> tuple[str, str]`: a leading tag in square brackets (Indonesian `senang sedih marah kaget santai netral` or the English names, case-insensitive, optional spaces; the full-width brackets `［ ］` and `【 】` of Japanese text count too) selects the emotion and is removed; any other such emotion tag anywhere is removed too (it must never be spoken or printed); text without a tag → `("neutral", text)`; other bracketed text like `[1]` is left alone. `config/persona.md` asks the model to start every text it writes, also the text after a tool call (only the last one is spoken), with one of the Indonesian tags.
- `lipsync.py` — `viseme_track(audio, sample_rate, fps=60) -> list[tuple[float, float, float, float, float]]` (aa, ih, ou, ee, oh), one frame per 1/fps s (`ceil(duration*fps)` frames). Openness from frame RMS in dBFS mapped linearly from −50 dB (closed) to −15 dB (fully open), smoothed with a fast attack and a slower release; vowel shape from the frame's spectral centroid (low → ou/oh, mid → aa, high → ee/ih) blended with a baseline of aa so the mouth always opens; a frame's weights sum to at most its openness. Pure numpy.
- `bus.py` — `AvatarBus(name="Cyrene", framing="full", *, clock=time.monotonic)`: thread-safe fan-out. `subscribe() -> Subscription` (bounded queue of 64, the oldest event dropped when full, never blocks the publisher; the first items are hello + last state, then the last speak event with `elapsed` while `elapsed < duration` and no state other than `speaking` came after it), `unsubscribe(sub)`, `wait_for_viewer(timeout) -> bool` (whether a subscriber exists, waiting up to `timeout` s for one; False at once after `close()`), `publish(event)`, and helpers `state(name, followup_s=None)`, `speak(audio, sample_rate, emotion)` (builds the speak event with `viseme_track`), `emotion(name, hold_s=4)`, `close()` (wakes every subscriber so SSE handlers return).
- `server.py` — `AvatarError`. `AvatarServer(config: AvatarConfig, bus, *, web_root=<package>/avatar/web)`: `ThreadingHTTPServer` (daemon threads) bound to the configured host/port; `start()` serves in a daemon thread, `stop()` closes the bus subscriptions, shuts down, and closes the socket; `url` (`http://host:port/`); context manager. Every request whose `Host` is not `<host>:<port>` of the server or `localhost:<port>` gets 403 before routing (DNS rebinding; 421 before v1.5). Routes (GET only, others 405; v1.5 adds `POST /interact` and the Live2D routes, see "Live2D and interactions (v1.5)"): `/` → `index.html`; `/events` → SSE (`text/event-stream`, `Cache-Control: no-cache`, `retry: 2000`, a `: ping` comment every 15 s, ends cleanly on client disconnect); `/model.vrm` → the configured `avatar.model` file (404 when missing; `model/gltf-binary`); any other path → a file under `web_root` (the resolved path must stay inside `web_root`, else 404; no directory listing; content types for .html .js .mjs .css .json .png .svg .md; `Cache-Control: no-store` for .html). A port in use → `AvatarError` with an Indonesian hint.
- `window.py` — `AvatarWindow(config, url, *, popen=subprocess.Popen, edge_path=None, job_factory=None, project_root=<repo root>)`: `open()` for `mascot` runs `<electron_dir>/node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe <electron_dir> --url=<url>?mode=mascot --width=W --height=H --corner=C --always-on-top=1|0` (`electron_dir` relative to the project root; a missing exe → `AvatarError` telling the owner to run `npm install` and `node node_modules/electron/install.js` in `avatar-app`); for `app` runs Edge (`edge_path`, else `%ProgramFiles(x86)%` / `%ProgramFiles%` `Microsoft\Edge\Application\msedge.exe`) with `--app=<url>?mode=app --window-size=W,H --user-data-dir=<project>/data/avatar-edge-profile` (a separate profile so the window is its own process that can be closed); `none` does nothing. The child goes into a kill-on-close job (reuse `waifu.voice.rvc.KillOnCloseJob`; a failure is only logged) so it dies with Cyrene. `close()` terminates only what it started; idempotent; context manager; never raises.
- `app.py` integration — `Assistant(..., avatar: AvatarBus | None = None)`: `state("thinking")` once the gate accepts a command; replies go through `split_reply` (`split_emotion`, and a reply that was only a tag becomes `Sudah, Master.`, or a short apology when a tool failed or none ran) before speaking (also without an avatar, so tags are never spoken and she never goes silent); right before `player.play` → `avatar.speak(audio, sr, emotion)`; after speaking → `state("listening", followup_s=config.assistant.followup_seconds)`, or `state("idle")` when that is 0; a bare wake-word acknowledgement uses `happy`; avatar errors are logged and never stop the voice loop. `handle_text` (chat) returns the reply with the tag removed.
- CLI — when `avatar.enabled`: `run` (inside the lock, during preload, before "ready"), `say`, and `chat --speak` start `AvatarServer` + `AvatarWindow` and close them on exit; a failure prints an Indonesian warning and continues without the avatar. A speak event reaches only viewers already connected, so `say` (after synthesis) and `run --announce` (before the greeting) wait up to 5 s for the page to subscribe (`wait_for_viewer`), unless `avatar.window` is `none`; `chat --speak` and replies in `run` do not wait. New `waifu avatar [--demo]`: start server and window, print the URL, keep running until Ctrl+C; `--demo` cycles the six emotions and the states every few seconds so the owner can check the model without talking. `check` reports: avatar disabled, or the VRM exists, the window program exists (Electron exe for mascot, Edge for app), and whether the port is free (no spawning).

Web (`src/waifu/avatar/web/`):
- `index.html` — import map (three, three/addons/, @pixiv/three-vrm), full-window canvas, transparent page background when `?mode=mascot` (otherwise a soft gradient), the credit line in small text, a close button (×) visible on hover in mascot mode (calls `window.close()`), `-webkit-app-region: drag` on the page and `no-drag` on the button so the mascot can be dragged.
- `logic.js` — pure ESM functions, no DOM/three imports: `sampleVisemes(track, fps, elapsedS)` (linear interpolation, zeros outside the track), `nextBlinkDelay(random)` (2–6 s), `blinkWeight(phaseS)` (0→1→0 over ~0.15 s), `approach(current, target, dt, speed)` (exponential smoothing), `emotionTargets(emotion)` (VRM expression weights for one emotion, others 0), `breathOffset(timeS)`, `idleSway(timeS)`, `framingFor(name, headY)` (camera position/target for `full` and `upper`), `listeningEndS(followupS, nowS)`, `speechStartS(elapsedS, nowS)`, `avatarTitle(name)`. Tested with `node --test`.
- `avatar.js` — loads `/model.vrm` with GLTFLoader + VRMLoaderPlugin, `VRMUtils.rotateVRM0`, relaxed pose (upper arms down ~70°, slight elbow bend), camera framing from `logic.framingFor`, lights, render loop with `vrm.update(dt)`: breathing and sway on spine/chest, random blinks (suppressed while an emotion uses the eyes), look-at toward the camera with small drifts, state poses (thinking: head tilted and eyes up; listening: slight lean forward), emotion weights eased with `approach`, visemes from the active speak event. `EventSource('/events')` opens once the model has loaded (or failed), so a subscribed viewer can show the first words, and reconnects automatically; the page title is `Avatar <name>` (never just the name, the title of the autostart console that stops her); a missing model shows a readable error in the page.
- `tests/js/logic.test.mjs` — `node --test "tests/js/*.test.mjs"` (Node 22+ built-in runner; since Node 21 it takes files or globs, not folders).
- `tests/test_avatar_web.py` — resolves the page's static imports through the import map and asserts every module is a file under `web/` (one missing file stops the whole page), and that VENDOR.md lists every vendored file.

Electron shell (`avatar-app/`): `package.json` pins `electron` 44.7.0 (devDependency; `node_modules/` gitignored; npm 11 skipped its download script on this PC, so setup runs `node node_modules/electron/install.js` after `npm install`); `lib.js` (pure, CommonJS): `parseArgs(argv)` (defaults: width 360, height 640, corner bottom-right, always-on-top on; rejects a URL that is not plain http with a port on an IPv4 loopback address, 127.0.0.0/8, the same rule as `avatar.host`), `cornerPosition(workArea, width, height, corner, margin=16)`; `main.js`: single-instance lock, a transparent frameless non-resizable `BrowserWindow` without shadow, `alwaysOnTop` per arg, positioned with `cornerPosition` on the primary display's work area, `webPreferences: { contextIsolation: true, sandbox: true, nodeIntegration: false }`, navigation and `window.open` outside the page's origin blocked, the window title is `Avatar Cyrene`, the app quits when the window closes; `test/lib.test.js` runs with `node --test "avatar-app/test/*.test.js"`.

Safety: `tests/conftest.py` refuses spawning `electron.exe`/`msedge.exe`; tests use fake popen and jobs, bind servers to port 0, never open windows or load the real VRM. Only the owner's real run opens the mascot.

### notify (v1.4)

User story: whatever Cyrene tells the owner by herself (a game reminder, the daily check-in, a HoYoLAB problem) also reaches their phone, wherever they are, as long as the PC is on.

ntfy (open source, Android/iOS app, free public server `ntfy.sh`, no account): the owner subscribes in the app to a topic only they know and Cyrene publishes to it. On the public server anyone who knows a topic name can read it, so the topic must be long and random and is treated like a password.

- Config `notify`: `enabled` (false), `server` (`https://ntfy.sh`; must start with `https://`), `topic` (required when enabled; `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{16,64}$`, so a short guessable name is refused). The topic never appears in logs; `check` shows its first 4 characters and `…`.
- `notify.py` — `Notifier` protocol: `send(title: str, message: str, *, tags: Sequence[str] = ()) -> bool`. `NtfyNotifier(config: NotifyConfig, *, http_post=None, timeout_s=10.0)`: one `POST <server>/` with the JSON body `{"topic", "title", "message", "tags"}` (JSON publishing keeps UTF-8 titles and Japanese text intact; ntfy shows a tag such as `video_game` as an emoji); True on 2xx; any failure (HTTP status, timeout, `OSError`) is logged at WARNING without the topic and returns False; never raises. `build_notifier(config) -> Notifier | None` (None when disabled).
- CLI `waifu notify-test`: sends "Tes notifikasi dari Cyrene. Kalau ini muncul di HP, notifikasi sudah siap." and prints ✓, or ✗ with what to check (`notify.enabled`, the topic subscribed in the ntfy app, the internet connection).
- Tests never reach the internet: `tests/conftest.py` refuses `urllib.request` openers and `aiohttp` requests to any host that is not a loopback address (an assertion naming the host); `tests/test_safety_guard.py` covers it.

### gacha buddy (v1.4)

User stories (the owner plays Honkai: Star Rail, Genshin Impact, and Zenless Zone Zero on this PC, Asia server):

1. "Cyrene, Trailblaze Power-ku berapa?" → live HoYoLAB numbers: current/max and when it is full. "Cyrene, cek semua game" → a short summary of the three games.
2. While `waifu run` is on, she speaks up by herself, and pushes the same text to the phone, when stamina will be full within `stamina_minutes` (default 60) or already is; a game's dailies are not done at `daily_time` (default 20:00 local); on the last day before the weekly reset, weeklies are left; every expedition of a game has finished. Each occasion once, at any hour (the owner chose no quiet hours).
3. Once per HoYoLAB check-in day she claims the check-in reward of every game and says what she got in one sentence; when HoYoLAB asks for a captcha she tells the owner to check in by hand.
4. "Cyrene, aku barusan pull di HSR" → she refreshes the history and reacts like a friend: happy for a limited 5★ (50/50 won), comforting when the 50/50 was lost, otherwise the pity and how far hard pity is; she mentions that HoYoverse lists pulls about an hour late.
5. She never suggests spending money or topping up.

Non-goals: build or team advice (the brain already talks about it; no code; v1.16 and v1.23 add data about the owner's characters, see HSR data and Genshin and ZZZ data), reacting to pulls nobody mentioned, quiet hours, anything that plays the game (macros or auto-farming break HoYoverse's terms), servers other than the configured offset, Honkai Impact 3rd.

Data sources:
- HoYoLAB through genshin.py with the owner's browser cookie (needs `ltuid_v2` + `ltoken_v2`; the whole cookie string is recommended): real-time notes, daily check-in, game accounts. Notes are read with `autoauth=False`: Cyrene never changes HoYoLAB settings; disabled Real-Time Notes become an error that says how to turn them on.
- Pull history from the game's own history API (`getGachaLog`; `getLdGachaLog` for the HSR collab banners) with an authkey. The authkey comes from the game's web cache, written when the owner last opened the in-game history (valid ~24 h): the game log under `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\LocalLow` → the data folder it names → the `webCaches/*/Cache/Cache_Data/data_2` written last that holds such a URL → its last `getGachaLog`/`getLdGachaLog` URL. When that authkey is missing or expired and the cookie also has `cookie_token_v2` (with `account_id_v2`/`account_mid_v2`), genshin.py's `fetch_authkey(uid, game=...)` makes a fresh one. Checked on the owner's PC on 2026-10-12: HSR history read with the cached authkey (old and new HSR path, minimal query); older Genshin and ZZZ caches answered retcode -101 "authkey timeout" and retcode -1 "auth key time out".
- genshin.py's own history routes are not used: HSR and ZZZ moved (`public-operation-hkrpg-sg.hoyoverse.com/common/hkrpg_gacha_record/api/`, `public-operation-common-sg.hoyoverse.com/common/gacha_record/api/`). Requests use the base path of the cached URL, else these constants (Genshin: `public-operation-hk4e-sg.hoyoverse.com/gacha_info/api/`).

Modules (`src/waifu/gacha/`):

- `games.py` — per-game facts (frozen dataclasses) keyed by `hsr`, `genshin`, `zzz`: name and short name (HSR, Genshin, ZZZ), the genshin.py game, stamina name (Trailblaze Power, Original Resin, Battery Charge), daily name (Daily Training, Daily Commissions, Engagement), premium currency (Stellar Jade, Primogem, Polychrome; 160 per pull), top rarity (5, 5, and 4 for a ZZZ S-rank), the log files under `LocalLow` and the data-folder name, `game_biz`, the history API constant, and the banners in display order with hard pity and whether a 50/50 applies:
  - hsr: 11 Character Event Warp (90, 50/50), 12 Light Cone Event Warp (80), 1 Stellar Warp (90), 21 Collaboration Character Warp (90), 22 Collaboration Light Cone Warp (80); 21 and 22 use `getLdGachaLog`.
  - genshin: 301 Character Event Wish (90, 50/50; the API returns the second character banner, 400, inside 301), 302 Weapon Event Wish (80), 200 Wanderlust Invocation (90), 500 Chronicled Wish (90).
  - zzz: 2 Exclusive Channel (90, 50/50), 3 W-Engine Channel (80), 1 Stable Channel (90), 5 Bangboo Channel (80), 102 Exclusive Rescreening (90), 103 W-Engine Reverberation (80).
  Novice banners are not read. Every banner type keeps its own pity (HSR collab and ZZZ rescreening do not share it with the regular banners, per fan guides, 2026).
  Standard 5★ pools (what a lost 50/50 gives): genshin Dehya, Diluc, Jean, Keqing, Mona, Qiqi, Tighnari, Yumemizuki Mizuki; hsr Bailu, Bronya, Clara, Gepard, Himeko, Welt, Yanqing; zzz Grace, Koleda, Lycaon, Nekomata, Rina, Soldier 11 (also their full names Grace Howard, Koleda Belobog, Von Lycaon, Alexandrina Sebastiane). `gacha.off_banner[game]` adds names (since HSR 4.2 the owner picks the lost-50/50 character in Celestial Invitation).
  Time helpers (pure, aware datetimes, `offset` = `gacha.server_utc_offset`): `game_day(now, offset)` (changes at 04:00 server time), `checkin_day(now, offset)` (00:00 server time), `next_daily_reset(now, offset)`, `is_last_day_of_week(day)` (Sunday; the weekly reset is Monday 04:00 server time), `reminder_due(now, daily_time, offset)` (the first local `daily_time` at or after the start of the current game day has passed).
- `hoyolab.py` — `GachaError(kind, message)`: kind is `no_cookie`, `cookie`, `notes_off`, `captcha`, `account`, `rate_limit`, `network`, `authkey`, `locked`, or `other` (v1.16 adds `chronicle_private` for the Battle Chronicle data, see HSR data); the message is Indonesian for the owner. Values: `Account(game, uid, nickname, level, server_name)`, `WeeklyTask(name, current, max)`, `GameStatus(game, stamina, stamina_max, stamina_full_in_s, daily_current, daily_max, daily_done, weekly: tuple[WeeklyTask, ...], expeditions_total, expeditions_finished, checked_in: bool | None)`, `CheckinResult(game, claimed: bool, reward: str | None)` (`claimed=False`: already claimed today). `HoyolabClient(cookie: str | None, *, uids: Mapping[str, int] | None = None, client_factory=None, timeout_s=20.0)`:
  - `accounts() -> list[Account]` (the owner's accounts of the three games),
  - `status(games) -> dict[str, GameStatus | GachaError]` (each game's notes and check-in state, concurrently),
  - `checkin(game) -> CheckinResult`,
  - `authkey(game) -> str` (`fetch_authkey`).
  Each call is one `asyncio.run` around `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout_s)` with a new `genshin.Client(cookie, lang="en-us")` from `client_factory`, so nothing async is shared between the voice loop and the watcher thread. The uid is `uids[game]`, else the highest-level account of that game on the cookie (looked up once, under a lock). genshin.py is imported lazily. Notes → `GameStatus`: hsr `current_stamina`/`max_stamina`/`stamina_recover_time`, Daily Training `current_train_score`/`max_train_score`, weekly Echo of War (`max_weekly_discounts` − `remaining_weekly_discounts` of `max_weekly_discounts`) and Simulated Universe points (`current_rogue_score`/`max_rogue_score`), expeditions; genshin `current_resin`/`max_resin`/`remaining_resin_recovery_time`, `daily_task` completed/max (done also needs `claimed_commission_reward`), weekly bosses (`max_resin_discounts` − `remaining_resin_discounts`), expeditions; zzz `battery_charge` (`seconds_till_full`), `engagement`, the weekly points of `weekly_task` when present, no expeditions. `checked_in` comes from `get_reward_info` (None when only that call fails). Errors: `InvalidCookies` → cookie, retcode 10102 → notes_off, `GeetestError`/`DailyGeetestTriggered` → captcha, `AccountNotFound` → account, `TooManyRequests`/`VisitsTooFrequently` → rate_limit, timeout/`aiohttp.ClientError`/`OSError` → network, any other `GenshinException` → other (retcode logged); `AlreadyClaimed` is `CheckinResult(claimed=False)`. Without a cookie every call raises `GachaError("no_cookie")`. The cookie and authkeys never appear in a log line or an exception message.
- `history.py` — `GachaRecord(id, uid, banner, name, rarity, item_type, time)` (`time`: the API's server-time text `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS`; `banner`: the type it was asked for). `CachedGachaUrl(base, authkey)` (authkey left out of its repr); `find_cached_gacha_url(game, locallow) -> CachedGachaUrl | None` (`PermissionError` → `GachaError("locked")`: close the game and ask again). `GachaLogApi(*, http_get=get_json, sleep=time.sleep, clock=time.monotonic, request_gap_s=0.5, timeout_s=15.0)`: `fetch(base, game, banner, authkey, *, stop_at_id=0, max_pages=None) -> list[GachaRecord]` reads 20 records per page with `end_id` until a short page, a record with id ≤ `stop_at_id`, or `max_pages`; query `authkey_ver=1, sign_type=2, auth_appid=webview_gacha, authkey, lang=en, game_biz, gacha_type, real_gacha_type, size=20, end_id`; requests stay 0.5 s apart, across banners too (measured on the owner's PC: five back-to-back requests drew -110, twelve 0.5 s apart passed), and a -110 is retried after 1 s and 2 s before it becomes `rate_limit`; retcode -100/-101, or a message containing "auth" and "key", → `GachaError("authkey")`; transport and JSON errors → `network`; the URL query is never logged. `get_json(url, timeout_s)` sends a browser User-Agent. `GachaStore(directory)`: one JSON file per account, `<game>-<uid>.json` `{"version": 1, "game", "uid", "complete", "sequence", "records": [...]}` ordered by id, written atomically (`gacha/files.py`: temp file, fsync, `os.replace`); `load(game, uid=None) -> StoredHistory(uid, records, complete)` reads the account saved last (highest `sequence`: NTFS file times move in ~15 ms steps, too coarse to order saves), or the given account; a file it cannot read is skipped with a warning; `save(game, uid, records, *, complete)`. Pure `banner_stats(game, records, extra_standard) -> list[BannerStats]`, per banner in display order (banners without records left out): `pity` (pulls since the last top-rarity item), `to_hard_pity`, `total`, `guaranteed` (50/50 banners: walking the top-rarity items oldest first, a standard one sets it and any other clears it; None elsewhere), and `recent_tops`, the last five top-rarity items newest first, each with `pulls` (pulls it took on that banner) and `won_5050` (True/False on a 50/50 banner; None when it was guaranteed or the banner has no 50/50). `GachaHistory(store, api, *, locallow=None, hoyolab=None, extra_standard=None, server_utc_offset=8, now=...)`: `report(game, *, refresh=True, full=False) -> HistoryReport` reads every banner newest first down to the newest stored record of that banner (a banner without stored records stops after 5 pages; `full` reads everything) with the cached authkey, else on an authkey error or without a cache one from `hoyolab.authkey(game)` (base: the cached URL's, else the constant); records of another uid than the stored one mean another account, read again from that account's own saved history (empty when it has none). It saves only when something changed and the account is known (no record at all writes nothing) and returns `HistoryReport(game, updated, problem, complete, stats, new_pulls, new_count, latest_pull, uid)` plus `hint`: `uid` is the account read (after a failed refresh the stored one; None without any record), which need not be the one saved last, as switching back to an account with no new pull saves nothing; `new_pulls` are the new items of the top two rarities, newest first, with `pulls` and `won_5050` for a top-rarity one; `latest_pull` is in local time; `complete` stays False until every page has been read once (`hint` then names `waifu gacha history <game> --full`); after a `GachaError` nothing is saved, the saved records answer, and `problem` carries the message (no usable authkey: "Buka riwayat warp di HSR sekali, lalu tanya lagi."). It never raises `GachaError`. Verified on the owner's PC on 2026-10-12: a full HSR import read 1101 pulls over five banners in 36 s.
- `tools.py` — `GachaTools(hoyolab: HoyolabClient, history: GachaHistory, games)` implements the brain's `ToolProvider`:
  - `game_status` `{game?: enum[configured games]}` → `{"games": [{"game", "name", "stamina": {"name", "current", "max", "full", "full_in_minutes", "full_at"}, "daily": {"name", "current", "max", "done"}, "weekly": [{"name", "current", "max"}], "expeditions": {"finished", "total"} (games that have them), "checked_in", "daily_reset"}], "errors": [{"game", "message"}]}` (`full_at` local `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, None when full; `daily_reset` local `HH:MM`); `is_error` only when every requested game failed.
  - `gacha_history` `{game: enum[configured games]}` → the `HistoryReport` as JSON: `updated`, `note` (HoYoverse lists pulls about an hour late), `latest_pull`, `new_pulls_count`, `new_pulls` (banner, name, rarity `5★`/`4★` or `S`/`A` for ZZZ, and `pulls`/`won_5050` for a top-rarity one), `banners` (name, `pity`, `hard_pity`, `to_hard_pity`, `total`, `guaranteed` on a 50/50 banner, `recent_top_pulls` with `pulls`/`won_5050`/date), `currency_spent_estimate` (pulls × 160 with the currency name), and `problem`/`hint` when set; `is_error` only when there is no data at all. About 1.8 kB for the owner's HSR history.
  The descriptions say when to use each tool, that `won_5050` is judged from the standard pool list, and that she never suggests spending money.
- `reminders.py` — `Notice(game: str | None, kind, text, emotion)`, kinds `stamina`, `daily`, `weekly`, `expeditions`, `checkin`, `problem`. `ReminderState`: `fired` once-keys for dailies, weeklies, problems, and the check-in, kept in JSON `<data_dir>/reminders.json` (written atomically, keys older than 14 days dropped on save); `occasions` for stamina and expedition batches, kept only while the run lasts, because spending is only seen by a poll and a refill found after Cyrene was off is a new occasion. Pure `due_notices(statuses, now, state, reminders_config, offset) -> list[Notice]` (updates `state`):
  - stamina: once when `stamina_full_in_s ≤ stamina_minutes × 60` (or full), re-armed once it is above the window again or Cyrene restarts.
  - daily: once per game per game day after `reminder_due`, when not `daily_done`; the text gives the progress and the local reset time.
  - weekly (`reminders.weekly`): once per game per week, on the last game day after `reminder_due`, naming what is left (HSR Echo of War and Simulated Universe points, Genshin weekly bosses, ZZZ weekly points).
  - expeditions (`reminders.expeditions`): once when every expedition of a game has finished (at least one), re-armed when one is running again or Cyrene restarts.
  `problem_notice(game, error, now, state)`: for kinds cookie, notes_off, captcha, account, once per game day each (other kinds are only logged). `checkin_notice(results)`: one sentence naming the rewards she claimed (None when nothing new was claimed). Texts are Indonesian, in her voice, without emoji or markdown; emotions: check-in `happy`, reminders `surprised`, problems `sad`.
- `watcher.py` — `GachaWatcher(hoyolab, config: GachaConfig, *, notifier: Notifier | None, announce: Announce | None, title="Cyrene", now=..., state_path=None)`: `poll_once() -> list[Notice]`: the status of the configured games → `due_notices` and `problem_notice`s; when `gacha.checkin` is on and the current check-in day is not handled yet, `checkin` for every game, and the day counts as handled unless a game hit a `network` or `rate_limit` error; delivery is one push (each notice's text on its own line, tag `video_game`) and the texts joined into announcements, each with the strongest emotion of sad > surprised > happy: the reminders about a game's state (stamina, daily, weekly, expeditions) as one with `game_expires_at` = the poll's `now` + `poll_minutes` (v1.34, v1.34.1: held by a game past the next poll, play has likely changed what they tell, so they are dropped unsaid; a focus block alone holds them as long as it lasts, as in v1.30), then the problems and the check-in as one without (said together they sound as the one announcement before); the state is saved. `start()` polls at once and then every `poll_minutes` in a daemon thread; `stop()` wakes and joins it (≤ 5 s); a failing poll is logged and the loop goes on; context manager.

Voice loop: `Assistant.announce(text, emotion=NEUTRAL)` (thread safe) queues a text. In `run`, after a frame that did not complete an utterance and while the segmenter is not inside one (`Segmenter.busy`, a new read-only property of `EnergySegmenter`), the queued texts are spoken together with `followup=False` (mic paused, avatar mouthing, no follow-up window). Never during a reply.

Brain:
- `brain/tools.py` — `ToolProvider` protocol: `specs() -> list[ToolSpec]`, `run(name, arguments) -> tuple[str, bool]` (never raises). `build_tool_specs(registry, providers=())` is the two device tools plus each provider's specs (a duplicate name is a `ValueError`); `run_tool(registry, name, arguments, providers=())` dispatches to the device tools, then to the provider that declares `name` (an exception from it is logged and becomes an error result), else the unknown-tool error listing every tool. Specs stay byte-identical across turns (prompt cache).
- `ClaudeBrain`, `OllamaBrain`, and `build_brain` take `providers: Sequence[ToolProvider] = ()`.
- `config/persona.md` gains a short section: when Master talks about their HoYoverse games and the game tools exist, use them instead of guessing numbers, react to pulls like a friend, and never suggest spending money or topping up.

Config `gacha`: `enabled` (false), `cookie` (`SecretStr`, optional; when given it must hold `ltuid_v2` + `ltoken_v2` or `ltuid` + `ltoken`; the error names the missing cookie, never a value), `games` (default `[hsr, genshin, zzz]`, unique, at least one), `uids` (`{game: uid}`, optional), `server_utc_offset` (8, Asia), `poll_minutes` (15, 5..240), `checkin` (true), `reminders: {stamina_minutes: 60 (0..1440), daily_time: "20:00" (HH:MM), weekly: true, expeditions: true}`, `off_banner` (`{game: [names]}`), `data_dir` (`data/gacha`).

CLI (`waifu gacha <aksi>`, Indonesian output; these commands work whether or not `gacha.enabled` is on, which only adds the game tools and the watcher to `run`; `build_gacha(config) -> GachaParts(hoyolab, history)` reads the game logs under `GAME_LOGS_DIR`, `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\LocalLow`):
- `accounts` — every game account on the cookie (game, uid, nickname, level, server) and which one Cyrene uses.
- `status [game]` — the status of each configured game (or one), the same data as `game_status`.
- `checkin` — claim now; prints each game's reward or "sudah diambil".
- `history <game> [--full]` — refresh (`--full` reads every page: the first import) and print each banner's pity, guarantee, total, and last 5★.
- `run` with `gacha.enabled`: the game tools for the brain and, with a cookie, the watcher (started after "ready"/the greeting, stopped on exit), pushing through `notify` when it is enabled. `chat`: the tools only. `check`: gacha disabled, or the cookie's key names (never values) and whether `cookie_token_v2` is there, the games, `genshin` importable, and whether each game's cached history URL exists; notify disabled, or the server and the masked topic. No network call in `check`.

Safety: tests fake genshin.py's client (returning real genshin.py models built from sample payloads), the history API (`http_get`), the clock, the notifier, and the `LocalLow` folder (`tmp_path`); no test reads the real game caches, the real `config.yaml`, or the network (conftest guard). Logs and messages never contain the cookie, an authkey, or a history URL's query.

### Live2D and interactions (v1.5)

User story: Cyrene can wear a Live2D model instead of the VRM. With either engine she looks at the mouse cursor anywhere on the screen (mascot mode), reacts with a face and a short spoken line when the owner pats her head or pokes her body, starts listening on a double-click, looks bored after a while without activity, and greets the owner when they come back. The mascot moves by dragging her body; clicks on transparent pixels pass through to the desktop.

Model rights: the owner's Live2D model "Cyrene20251105" by MomokaSono (Cubism 5, moc3 v5, 75 parameters, 14 physics settings, no expression or motion files) states: copyright miHoYo; fan videos allowed; follow miHoYo's official fan-creation rules; no monetized livestreaming; no redistribution. Use is private and non-commercial; the model stays under `C:\AI\avatars\` and is never committed or shared; the page shows `avatar.credit`. No sexualized interaction exists: there are only head and body regions (the owner's request for "sensitive areas" was declined; the model's rules also forbid 18+ works). The head region ends above her chin, measured on the model (see `live2d.js`), so a touch on her chest is never a head pat: a pat line with her happy, blushing face must never answer it.

Live2D runtime: PixiJS 8.21.0 (`vendor/pixi/pixi.min.mjs`) and untitled-pixi-live2d-engine 1.4.0 (`vendor/live2d-engine/cubism.es.js`, MIT, bundles Live2D's Cubism Web Framework under the Live2D Open Software License), both vendored. Live2D's proprietary Cubism Core (`live2dcubismcore.min.js`) is never vendored: the owner downloads the official Cubism SDK for Web (accepting Live2D's licences at download); Cyrene serves that file unmodified from `avatar.live2d_core`. `.gitignore` excludes `live2dcubismcore*.js`. The engine was written for Core 5; the Cubism SDK for Web 5-r.5 ships Core 6 (06.00.0001), which moved the render orders from `drawables.renderOrders` to `model.getRenderOrders()` (drawables first, then offscreens). `logic.adaptCubismCore`, called before the model loads, gives every model a Core 6 makes that field back and refuses a model that uses Cubism 5.3 rendering, which the engine cannot draw: offscreens, or a drawable blend mode other than Normal/Over, AddCompatible or MultiplyCompatible (the official Framework 5-r.5's own test; the engine would draw them as Normal without an error); a Core 5 is left alone. Verified with Core 06.00.0001 and Cyrene20251105 (moc3 5.0, Normal/Over only).

Config (committed with its tests): `avatar.engine: vrm|live2d` (default vrm); `avatar.model` must be a `.vrm` for vrm and a `.model3.json` for live2d; `avatar.live2d_core` is required for live2d when enabled; `avatar.credit` (default the Tsukuyomi-chan credit); `avatar.interactions`: `enabled`, `cursor_follow`, `head_pat`, `body_touch`, `double_click_listen`, `idle` (all default true), `welcome_back_after_min` (default 10, 0 = never), `reaction_cooldown_s` (default 3), `lines` (`head_pat`, `body_touch`, `welcome_back`, `listen`; defaults provided, each non-empty).

Server (`avatar/server.py`, `avatar/bus.py`):
- Every request: the `Host` header must be `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, else 403 (blocks DNS rebinding).
- Every response (refusals and errors too) carries `PROTECTING_HEADERS`: `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: same-origin`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'`, so no other website can embed the Core, the model, or the page, or frame the page to turn clicks into pats.
- `file_under(root, relative) -> Path | None`: the file a relative `/`-separated path names inside `root`; an empty, absolute, drive, UNC or URL name, a backslash, or a NUL is refused before anything is resolved (resolving `\\host\share` makes Windows contact that host). Static files, `/model/<path>`, and `waifu check`'s model3.json references (backslashes read as `/`, as a URL does) all go through it.
- A random per-start token (`secrets.token_urlsafe(24)`).
- `GET /config.json` → `{"engine", "model", "core", "name", "framing", "credit", "token", "interactions": {switches..., "welcome_back_after_min"}}`; `model` is `/model.vrm` for vrm and `/model/<model3 file name>` for live2d; `core` is `/live2d/live2dcubismcore.min.js` for live2d, null for vrm. `hello` (SSE) carries the same fields except `token`.
- `GET /model/<path>` → a file inside the Live2D model's folder (the folder of `avatar.model`), traversal-protected like static files (404 otherwise, and always 404 for engine vrm); `.json` application/json, `.png` image/png, `.moc3` application/octet-stream.
- `GET /live2d/live2dcubismcore.min.js` → the `avatar.live2d_core` file unmodified (`text/javascript`); 404 for vrm or when missing.
- `POST /interact` — the only non-GET route (other methods 405): `Origin` must be `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` or `http://localhost:<port>` (else 403); `Content-Type: application/json`; body ≤ 4 KB `{"token": str, "kind": "head_pat"|"body_touch"|"double_click"|"welcome_back"}`; wrong token 403, bad body/kind 400, more than 10 requests per second 429; valid → 202 and `on_interact(kind)` (constructor argument, may be None) is called; its exceptions are logged.

Python interactions (`avatar/interactions.py`): `InteractionHandler(config: AvatarInteractionsConfig, *, speak: Callable[[str, str], None], listen: Callable[[], None], is_busy: Callable[[], bool], clock=time.monotonic, rng=random.Random())` with `start()`, `stop()` (context manager) and `handle(kind)`: `handle` only enqueues (it runs on server threads); a worker thread does the work. Ignored when `enabled` or the kind's switch is off, and every kind, `double_click` included, is ignored while `is_busy()` (the owner double-clicks again, or says the wake word, once she is free). `double_click` → `listen()`, then `speak(listen line, "happy")`; `head_pat` → `speak(line, "happy")`; `body_touch` → `speak(line, "surprised")`; `welcome_back` → `speak(line, "happy")`, only when `welcome_back_after_min > 0` and at most once per 30 minutes, and never while the optional `quiet` (v1.30: the focus timer's `in_block`) holds: the greeting is the one reaction she starts herself, so during a focus block it is dropped (DEBUG log), not saved for the break, and does not count against the 30 minutes; pats, pokes, and double-clicks are answered as always. Spoken lines are skipped within `reaction_cooldown_s` of the end of the previous one (a double-click inside it still listens, without a line); a line is picked at random, never the same as the previous line of that kind (when more than one exists). `Assistant` gains `busy` (true while handling an utterance, speaking, or while Master speaks), `speak_reaction(text, emotion)` (the normal speak path: mic paused, avatar speak event, follow-up window opened), and `listen_now()` (opens the follow-up window and publishes `state("listening", followup_s)`, saying nothing itself; it waits for a reply or announcement that started after the handler's busy check).

Web (`avatar/web/`):
- `index.html` fetches `/config.json`, then for live2d loads the Core with a classic `<script src=core>` and waits for it, then imports `live2d.js`; for vrm imports `avatar.js`; then opens `EventSource('/events')`. The import map adds `pixi.js` and `untitled-pixi-live2d-engine/cubism`. The page-wide `-webkit-app-region: drag` is removed (dragging is custom, see Electron). A `Content-Security-Policy` meta tag before every script keeps the page on Cyrene's own server (a downloaded model3.json may name `https://...` or `//host/...` files, which the engine would fetch as they are): `default-src 'self'`; scripts `'self' 'unsafe-inline' 'unsafe-eval'` (the inline scripts and import map; PixiJS compiles its uniform uploads and the Core is WebAssembly); images and fetches also `data:` and `blob:`, workers `blob:` (PixiJS's image workers); `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`. No source names another computer (`tests/test_avatar_web.py`).
- `logic.js` adds pure functions with `node --test` coverage: `live2dMouth([aa, ih, ou, ee, oh])` → `{open: 0..1, form: -1..1}` (open from all vowels, form wide for ee/ih and round for ou/oh); `live2dEmotionParams(emotion)` → target values for `ParamEyeLSmile/ParamEyeRSmile/ParamMouthForm/ParamCheek/ParamBrowLY/ParamBrowRY/ParamBrowLAngle/ParamBrowRAngle/ParamEyeLOpen/ParamEyeROpen/ParamEyeBallForm` on a normalized scale; `mapToRange(value, min, max)`; `cursorLook(cursor, faceCenter, size)` → `{angleX, angleY, bodyX, eyeX, eyeY}` in -1..1, saturating smoothly far away; `classifyPointer(samples)` → `click | double_click | rub | drag` from timed pointer samples (drag: moved > 6 px from a press outside the head, or > 24 px from a press on the head with the button held and leaving the head; rub: ≥ 3 direction changes, each after a stroke of ≥ 6 px, while pressed inside the head; double click: two clicks whose presses are ≤ 500 ms, Windows' default double-click time, and ≤ 12 px apart); `idlePhase(idleS)` → `active | bored` (bored after 180 s); `live2dHeadBox(drawables)` → her head `{left, top, width, height}` or null, from `[{vertices, head, mouth}]` at rest (y downward): the chin is the lowest point of the drawables that both turn with her head and move with her mouth; the box spans the head drawables' points down to 5 % of the head's height above the chin, so hair, ribbons and her chest below it are body; null when no head drawable moves with the mouth; `live2dRegionAt(x, y, {body, head}, isOpaqueAt)` → `"head"` inside the head box, `"body"` on an opaque pixel elsewhere inside her bounds, else null. Also `live2dPlacement(bounds, framing, width, height)` → `{scale, pivot}` (see live2d.js) and `adaptCubismCore(core)` (the Core 6 adapter, see Live2D runtime; tested with stand-in Cores shaped like Core 5 and 6).
- `live2d.js` (engine live2d): PixiJS `Application` with a transparent background in mascot mode, `extensions.add(Live2DPlugin)`, `Live2DModel.from(config.model)`, fitted to the window per `framing` by `logic.live2dPlacement(bounds, framing, width, height)` (full: the whole model fits; upper: the top half of her visible bounds fills the window's height, and whatever is wider than the window, like Cyrene's wings, runs past the sides, so she is shown bigger than in full). Each frame, after the engine's own update and before rendering (verify the hook in the vendored engine), set parameters through the core model clamped to each parameter's min/max: mouth from the active speak track via `live2dMouth`; emotion params eased toward `live2dEmotionParams`; cursor look (`ParamAngleX/Y/Z`, `ParamBodyAngleX`, `ParamEyeBallX/Y`) eased; the engine's EyeBlink and physics stay on (blink suppressed while the emotion closes the eyes); thinking: head tilt and eyes up; idle-bored: slow yawn cycle (mouth open, eyes half closed, head tilt) every 45–90 s. Exposes `hitTest(x, y) → "head" | "body" | null` and `isOpaqueAt(x, y)`. At load, after the first drawn frame, it measures her head on the model itself: which visible drawables move when `ParamAngleX`/`ParamAngleY` go to either end of their range (head) and when `ParamMouthOpenY` does (mouth), against the same rest pose, every parameter put back afterwards; `live2dHeadBox` turns that into the head region and `live2dRegionAt` answers `hitTest`. No fixed share of a full-body model's bounds tracks her head (the bounds include whatever hangs from her: on Cyrene20251105 the top 28 % reached over her chest). The cursor look aims from the head box's centre (without one, 14 % down her bounds).
- `avatar.js` (engine vrm) gains the same renderer interface: cursor look (head/neck bones and lookAt target toward the cursor), `hitTest` (head from the projected head bone, body from hips to neck), `isOpaqueAt` (renders only the pixel under the point into a 1x1 target and reads its alpha; a raycast through the skinned meshes cost 20-65 ms per call), idle-bored animation.
- `interaction.js` (shared, both engines): pointer handling with `classifyPointer`; click on head → `head_pat`, rub on head → `head_pat` at most every 1.5 s while rubbing, click on body → `body_touch`, double-click on the character → `double_click`, drag → `cyrene.dragBy(dx, dy)` in mascot mode; reports `isOpaqueAt` under the pointer to `cyrene.setClickThrough(!opaque)` (mascot only, throttled; a page element other than her canvas, like the close button, keeps its clicks even where she is transparent); cursor source = `cyrene.onCursor` in mascot mode, `pointermove` otherwise; idle tracking (cursor movement, presses, speak events) for the bored phase and `welcome_back` (sent once when the owner's activity resumes after `welcome_back_after_min`). Only the mascot greets: its feed sees the cursor anywhere on the screen, so a cursor move or a press there after the absence is Master coming back. In the app window the page sees the pointer only while it is over the page, so pointer activity there only ends the bored phase; a speak event and the mascot feed's first report after a pause (`resumed: true`, the window was minimized or hidden) count as activity without a greeting. `POST /interact` with the token from `/config.json`. Local feedback (a happy/surprised expression) is immediate, before the server answers.

Electron (`avatar-app/`): `preload.js` (sandbox-compatible, `contextBridge.exposeInMainWorld("cyrene", {onCursor(cb), dragBy(dx, dy), setClickThrough(flag)})` over `ipcRenderer`; `cb({x, y, resumed})`); `main.js` loads it, polls `screen.getCursorScreenPoint()` every 33 ms while the window is visible and not minimized and sends the cursor relative to the window's content bounds (it may be outside) when it changed, the first report after a pause with `resumed: true` (`lib.cursorReport`), moves the window on `drag-by` (finite numbers only, clamped to the display work area), and applies `setIgnoreMouseEvents(flag, {forward: true})` on `click-through`; `lib.js` adds pure helpers `relativeCursor(point, bounds)`, `clampToWorkArea(bounds, workArea)` and `cursorReport(feed, cursor, showing)` with `node --test` coverage.

CLI: `run` creates the `InteractionHandler` bound to the Assistant and passes `handler.handle` as the server's `on_interact`; `waifu avatar` binds it to printing the line plus an emotion and a short synthetic speak track (no microphone or TTS). `check` reports the engine, and for live2d the model folder files (model3.json, moc3, textures; a reference that is not a relative path inside the folder counts as missing and is never resolved, see `file_under`) and the Cubism Core file.

Safety: tests never load the real Live2D model or Core, never open windows, use port 0, and never send real interactions; `tests/conftest.py` guards stay untouched.

### long-term memory (v1.6)

User stories (the owner talks to Cyrene every day; today she forgets everything when a session ends):

1. "Cyrene, ingat ya, aku alergi udang." → she saves it and confirms in one sentence. Days later, "Cyrene, enaknya masak apa?" → she avoids shrimp without being reminded.
2. She also saves on her own the lasting things the owner mentions in passing: likes and dislikes, plans with a date ("Jumat ada ujian"), people and pets, routines, favourite games and characters. Small talk, device commands, and anything already saved are not saved.
3. "Cyrene, apa saja yang kamu ingat tentang aku?" → a short spoken summary, not a list read out.
4. "Cyrene, lupakan soal udang." → she deletes that note. When a fact changes ("sekarang aku sudah tidak kerja di sana") she updates the old note instead of keeping two that contradict.
5. Secrets are never kept: a note that holds a password, PIN, OTP, token, cookie, API key, or card number is refused in code, whatever the model asks for; a note that only names one without its value ("Master harus ganti PIN ATM") is kept.
6. The owner can read, add, delete, and wipe the notes from the terminal (`waifu memory`), or edit `data/memory.json` by hand.

Non-goals: a summary of every conversation (only facts the model chose to save), embeddings or semantic search (the whole list fits in the prompt), syncing between PCs, memories per person (there is one owner).

Privacy: the notes live only in `data/memory.json` on this PC (`data/` is gitignored and never committed). With the Claude backend they are sent to the Claude API as part of the system prompt of every session, like the rest of the conversation; with Ollama they stay local. README says so.

Config `memory` (committed with its tests before the parallel tasks): `enabled` (default true), `path` (default `data/memory.json`), `max_facts` (default 100, 1-500), `max_chars` (default 200 characters per note, 20-1000).

`memory.py`:
- `Fact` (frozen dataclass): `id: str` (`"m1"`, `"m2"`, … never reused), `text: str`, `created: datetime`, `updated: datetime` (local time; minute precision is enough).
- `MemoryError(Exception)`: its message is Indonesian and safe to show or speak.
- `looks_secret(text) -> bool`: true when a secret word is followed by what looks like its value, when the text contains a run of 20 or more characters from `[A-Za-z0-9_\-+/=]` that has both letters and digits, or when it contains 13-19 digits that pass the Luhn check, written together or in groups separated by single spaces or dashes (any stretch of neighbouring whole groups counts, so a card number said with its expiry or CVV, `4111 1111 1111 1111 12/28`, is caught; a group is never split). The secret words are these case-insensitive words or phrases (the words of a phrase joined by spaces, `_`, `-`, or nothing), as whole words except that the Indonesian suffixes `-nya`, `-ku`, `-mu` (with or without a hyphen) and a number may follow right away (`passwordnya`, `PIN-nya`, `PIN1234`): `password` and its short forms `passwd`, `pw`, `pwd`, `passcode`, `kata sandi`, `sandi` with one of those suffixes written on without a hyphen (`sandinya`) or before an account (`wifi`, `email`, `akun`, `hp`, `laptop`, `atm`, `bank`; a bare `sandi` is also a name or a code like sandi Morse), `pin` except before `lokasi`, `koleksi`, `genshin`, `honkai`, `hsr`, `zzz` (a map pin or a badge), `otp`, `cvv`, `api key`, `token` (also `token listrik` and `token PLN`: a prepaid token's 20 digits are a value), `seed phrase`. A secret word alone is no secret (`ganti PIN ATM`, `ingatkan ganti password wifi`, `beli token listrik` pass).
