In the same way, a phone turn sent through the camera (`camera`, or any photo) is marked in the shared `CameraTurn` (`begin()` right before the brain turn, `end()` in a `finally`), so only that turn is a camera turn; voice turns, text-chat turns, briefings, and phone messages sent without the viewfinder never are. `run` creates one `TurnImages` and one `CameraTurn` when the camera is offered and passes the first to the Claude brain, the Assistant, and (as `camera_max_side=vision.max_side`) the phone server, the second to the screen tool and the Assistant.

Page (`avatar/web/phone/`):
- A camera button next to send (hidden when `/config.json` says the camera is off). Tapping it opens the viewfinder: `getUserMedia({video: {facingMode: {ideal: "environment"}, width: {ideal: 1920}, height: {ideal: 1080}}, audio: false})` into a muted, `playsinline`, `autoplay` `<video>`; a flip button (environment ↔ user) and a close button. Errors in Indonesian: permission denied (how to allow it in Chrome's site settings), no camera, camera in use by another app.
- While it is open, sending text or releasing the talk button grabs one frame: drawn on a canvas sized by `fitWithin(width, height, maxSide)` (aspect kept, never upscaled) at that moment (for a talk, before the recording is stopped), `canvas.toBlob(..., "image/jpeg", 0.85)`; a blob over 3.5 MB is encoded again at 0.7, then 0.5, and dropped with a message if still too big. Every message sent while it is open goes as JSON with `"camera": true` (`/chat`, or `/talk` as `{audio, camera}`), with the photo as `"image"` when one was taken; a frame that could not be taken or was dropped still sends the message through the camera, without a photo, and says so. The photo shows as a thumbnail (an object URL, revoked when its bubble leaves the strip) in the owner's bubble. Send and talk stay disabled until the photo is encoded and the message has gone, so nothing else is sent meanwhile.
- The stream's tracks stop on close, on `visibilitychange` to hidden, on `pagehide`, and 2 minutes after the viewfinder opened, the last photo, or the last press of the talk button, whichever is later (so a long hold never loses the camera before its release); a viewfinder opened again has its own 2 minutes.
- The page reads `/config.json` only as it loads. When a message sent through the camera is refused with 400 or 413 (`waifu run` restarted with the camera off), it reads `/config.json` again: when the camera is now off, it closes the viewfinder, hides the camera button, and says that the camera was turned off on the PC and the message was not sent (typed text goes back to the text box); otherwise it shows the usual problem.
- Pure helpers in `phone/camera.js` with `node --test` coverage: `fitWithin`, `bytesToBase64` (chunked, for multi-megabyte input), the /chat and /talk request builders (`chatRequest(text, {camera, photo})`, `talkRequest(wav, {camera, photo})`) without the camera, through it with a photo, and through it without one, and the JPEG quality fallback order.

CLI: `check` adds `✓ Kamera HP: aktif`, `! Kamera HP: tidak tersedia dengan Ollama`, `! Kamera HP mati (vision.enabled: false)`, or `! Kamera HP mati (phone.camera: false)` when the phone is enabled. README (Indonesian), in "Cyrene di HP": using the camera, privacy, the camera permission, turning it off with `phone.camera: false`, and that "lihat layarku" from the phone still looks at the PC screen while with the viewfinder open only a word naming the screen does.

Safety: tests never open a camera: the page's browser check stubs `getUserMedia` with a canvas `captureStream()` showing a synthetic pattern; Python tests build tiny JPEG byte fixtures by hand (marker segments only; no image library); nothing reaches Claude.

### keeping her through fullscreen games (v1.15.1)

Bug report (2026-10-15): the owner plays Genshin Impact, Honkai: Star Rail, and Zenless Zone Zero in the games' Fullscreen mode on one of two monitors. With the mascot on the game's monitor, clicking the game puts her behind it (expected: nothing draws over an exclusive-fullscreen game). But she does not come back: not after Alt+Tab, not after being moved to the other monitor; only once the game is switched to windowed at a lower resolution does she appear again (then she is fine in front of the game or on the other monitor).

The owner's choice: she may stay hidden while a game covers her (the games' skill buttons sit in the bottom-right corner), but she must be visible again wherever nothing covers her (the other monitor, the game minimized or closed) without restarting Cyrene, and she comes back by herself if her window or its GPU process crashes.

Likely causes (the diagnostics below confirm which on the owner's next game session): Chromium's native window occlusion tracking (`CalculateNativeWinOcclusion`) and background throttling stop painting a window Chromium believes is covered, and a fullscreen game's display-mode switch can make it believe so on every monitor; the display-mode switch can also lose the page's WebGL context, after which the Live2D/VRM canvas stays empty (nothing handles `webglcontextlost`); a crash of Electron's GPU or renderer process leaves the transparent window with nothing in it; nothing restarts Electron if it exits.

Electron shell (`avatar-app/main.js`, pure helpers in `lib.js` with `node --test` coverage; `test/main.test.js` runs main.js against a fake `electron` module and a fake clock):
- Before `ready`: `app.commandLine.appendSwitch('disable-features', 'CalculateNativeWinOcclusion')`; the window's `webPreferences.backgroundThrottling: false`. So her page keeps painting whatever Chromium thinks covers her. Also `app.disableDomainBlockingFor3DAPIs()` (allowed only before `ready`): after GPU resets or GPU process crashes Chromium refuses WebGL, for a while, to the domain of a page that had a context then (Cyrene's 127.0.0.1; a review of Chromium 152's source found one Windows GPU reset can already add the two entries that block a domain), so the reload meant to bring her back would come back without WebGL.
- Recovery: on `webContents` `render-process-gone` and on `app` `child-process-gone` with `type === 'GPU'`, the page is reloaded after 1 s; at most 5 reloads in 10 minutes (`lib.reloadAllowed(history, now)`, pure), after which it only logs. `unresponsive`/`responsive` are logged. When the page reports `webgl-unavailable` (its renderer got no WebGL context), the page is reloaded the same way while `app.getGPUFeatureStatus().webgl` is enabled (`lib.isWebglEnabled(status)`: `enabled` or `enabled_*`); otherwise (Chromium gave up on the GPU after too many GPU process crashes, so no page of this Electron gets WebGL again) the mascot logs `exiting webgl=<status>: ...` and exits with code 1, so `window.py` opens a fresh Electron within its 3-in-10-minutes limit.
- Displays: on `screen` `display-metrics-changed`, `display-added`, and `display-removed` (a fullscreen game changing the monitor's resolution moves windows), `lib.placementAfterDisplayChange(bounds, workAreas, primaryWorkArea, size, corner, placed)` (pure) decides where she goes: when her bounds are no longer inside any display's work area (`lib.isOffScreen`) she is placed in her corner of the primary display (`lib.cornerPosition`) and the spot she was taken from is remembered (`placed = {from, at}`); while she still stands at `at` (nobody moved her since), she goes back to `from` once a display shows it again (the game gave the display its resolution back, a sleeping monitor woke up), else her corner follows the primary work area. Always-on-top is applied again at the same `'floating'` level (she still hides behind a fullscreen game, by the owner's choice) at once, again 1.5 s after the last display change (one timer, reset by every change, cleared when the window closes), and on the window's `show` and `restore`; those re-pins are logged as `re-pinned after <reason>`. Why more than once: on Windows, Electron puts a window at the `'floating'` level right behind the taskbar (`SetWindowPos` after `Shell_TrayWnd`), which keeps it topmost only while the taskbar is topmost at that moment, and Explorer takes the taskbar out of the topmost band while a fullscreen app runs on the primary display.
- Diagnostics: one line per event on stdout, `Cyrene mascot: <event> <details>`, for page visibility changes, WebGL context lost/restored, a renderer without WebGL (`webgl-unavailable`), page reloads, GPU/renderer process gone (reason, exit code), the exit for a fresh mascot, unresponsive/responsive, display changes, where she was placed (`placed-back`), and re-pins. The page sends its events through the preload's `diagnostic(kind)` (a fixed list of kinds; anything else is ignored).

Page (`avatar/web/index.html`, both renderers; helpers in `recovery.js` with `node --test` coverage):
- On the canvas's `webglcontextlost`: `preventDefault()`, report it; on `webglcontextrestored`, or 3 s after a loss without one, reload the page (a fresh renderer; the SSE stream reconnects by itself). At most one pending reload.
- `visibilitychange` is reported (diagnostics only).
- When the window gives a renderer no WebGL context, `createRenderer` rejects with `recovery.WebGLUnavailableError` (after its Indonesian message, `cause` the library's error), and the page reports `webgl-unavailable` (`recovery.reportRendererFailure`; a model that cannot load reports nothing), which main.js answers (see Recovery).
- In `mode=mascot` the renderers draw at most 30 frames per second (Live2D motions are authored at 30 fps), so painting while a game covers her costs little.
- The renderer interface (top of `live2d.js`) gains `destroy()`: stops drawing and lets go of the model and the GPU, after which `createRenderer` may draw her again on a fresh `#stage` canvas (`avatar.js` holds one renderer at a time).

Phone page (`avatar/web/phone/`, which imports the same renderers): a lost WebGL context (Android Chrome drops it for a page in the background; a GPU reset drops any) does not reload the page, which would lose the chat. `recovery.keepRenderer` (watchContextLoss, then once the page is visible) destroys the renderer, swaps its canvas for a fresh copy, and calls `createRenderer` again; the renderer is null meanwhile, and a new renderer that does not start leaves the avatar empty with the chat working. The already loaded Cubism Core is reused (it is loaded once per page). `recovery.js` comes from the PC like the renderers (the service worker keeps only the page's own files).

`avatar/window.py`:
- The mascot's stdout and stderr are piped and read by a daemon thread: lines starting with `Cyrene mascot:` are logged at INFO (WARNING when a word ends in gone, lost, unresponsive, crashed, unavailable, or skipped: a skipped reload and a page without WebGL leave her gone), other lines (Chromium's own noise) at DEBUG; the thread ends when the pipe closes.
- Supervision (mascot only; Edge's app window is left alone): a daemon thread waits on the process; when it exits while not being closed by `close()` with a non-zero exit code, it is opened again after 3 s, at most 3 times in 10 minutes (then a warning: restart Cyrene); exit code 0 (the owner closed her with × or from the taskbar) is respected. `close()` stops the supervision before ending the process. Tests use fake popen/processes and an injected clock and sleep; no real Electron.

README (Indonesian) troubleshooting "Cyrene hilang saat main game": she hides while a fullscreen game covers her and comes back by herself; if she does not, the `Cyrene mascot:` lines in the `waifu run` window say why (send them); once the automatic recovery gave up (a skipped reload, `WebGL tidak tersedia…` on the mascot, the mascot left closed), restarting `waifu run` brings her back now (closing the mascot with × or Alt+F4 does not); windowed/borderless game modes let her stay visible on another monitor without any of this.

Safety: tests never spawn Electron (tests/conftest.py refuses it); JS tests cover `lib.js`, `main.js` (a fake `electron` module in a VM context, never the real one), and the page helpers with fakes.

### HSR data (v1.16)

User stories (the owner plays Honkai: Star Rail on the Asia server; the gacha buddy's cookie is set):

1. "Cyrene, Acheron-ku Eidolon berapa?", "Firefly pakai Light Cone apa?", "karakter Nihility-ku siapa aja?" → she answers from the owner's own HoYoLAB Battle Chronicle: owned characters with rarity, level, Eidolon, Path, element, and Light Cone (level and Superimposition).
2. "Stat Acheron-ku gimana?" → the final stats, relic main stats, and memosprite of that one character, as data; she may comment, but no code gives build advice.
3. "Memory of Chaos-ku udah berapa bintang?", "Pure Fiction berakhir kapan?" → this season's Memory of Chaos, Pure Fiction, Apocalyptic Shadow, and Anomaly Arbitration: stars, deepest floor, battles, the best floors' cycles or score, and when the season ends (local time).
4. "Banner apa sekarang?", "event apa yang mau habis?" → the event calendar: current and upcoming warps with their featured 5★ and 4★, running events with the owner's progress, and the endgame windows, each with when it ends. (Since v1.23, with more than one game in `gacha.games`, the question names the game: "Banner HSR apa sekarang?".)

Non-goals (v1.16): build or team advice in code (the tools give data; the model may comment), relic substats, relic set names, and traces (later, after checking real data), endgame teams and previous seasons, Simulated Universe detail (its weekly points are already in `game_status`), Genshin and ZZZ equivalents (added in v1.23, see Genshin and ZZZ data), reminders about endgame seasons or banners ending (banner reminders read as a nudge to pull: never without the owner asking), mentioning HSR in the morning briefing, changing any HoYoLAB setting. This revises the v1.4 non-goal "build or team advice": data about the owner's characters is in scope, advice code is not.

Privacy: the data is read with the owner's cookie on request only (no polling), kept in memory for minutes (below), never written to disk or logged (log lines name only "HSR" and the error type or retcode), except that `-v` logs every tool result at DEBUG, as for every other tool (`Assistant` and the Ollama brain), to the console and to `--log-file`; the INFO log never holds it. The uid is in no log, even with `-v`: genshin.py logs every request URL, the uid in its query, at DEBUG, so the CLI holds the `genshin.client` logger at INFO (not `genshin`, which every new `genshin.Client` sets back to NOTSET). Tool results never hold the uid, nickname, ids, icons, wiki links, or descriptions; she does not save it with `remember` unless the owner asks. When HoYoLAB answers that the data is not public (genshin.py `DataNotPublic`, retcode 10102) she says so and where the setting is; she never changes it (no `update_settings`, no `autoauth`).

`gacha/starrail.py` (new, no genshin import; readers take the genshin.py models as `Any`):
- `ENDGAME_MODES = {"memory_of_chaos": "Memory of Chaos", "pure_fiction": "Pure Fiction", "apocalyptic_shadow": "Apocalyptic Shadow", "anomaly_arbitration": "Anomaly Arbitration"}` (display order).
- Frozen dataclasses (every datetime aware): `HsrLightCone(name, level, superimposition, rarity)`; `HsrRelic(piece, main_stat, value, level)`; `HsrCharacter(name, rarity, level, eidolon, path, element, light_cone: HsrLightCone | None, stats: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...], relics: tuple[HsrRelic, ...], memosprite: str | None)`; `HsrFloor(name, stars, cycles: int | None, score: int | None)`; `HsrEndgame(mode, season: str | None, ends_at: datetime | None, has_data: bool, stars: int, extra_stars: int, deepest_floor: str | None, battles: int | None, floors: tuple[HsrFloor, ...])`; `HsrWarp(kind: "character" | "light_cone", name, featured: tuple[str, ...], four_stars: tuple[str, ...], starts_at: datetime | None, ends_at: datetime | None)`; `HsrEvent(name, starts_at: datetime | None, ends_at: datetime | None, current: int | None, total: int | None, done: bool)`; `HsrChallengeWindow(mode, name, starts_at: datetime | None, ends_at: datetime | None, current: int | None, total: int | None)`; `HsrCalendar(version: str | None, warps, events, challenges)`.
- Readers: `read_character(model) -> HsrCharacter` (from a detailed `StarRailDetailCharacter`: `rank` is the Eidolon, `equip` may be None and its `rank` is the Superimposition, the Path from `StarRailPath(...).name` title-cased with spaces, final stats from `properties` (`info.name`, `final`), relics and ornaments with `main_property`); `read_endgame(mode, model) -> HsrEndgame` (season name and end from `seasons[0]`/`groups[0]`, already aware from the uid's server; since v1.27.1 an end on minute 59, HoYoLAB's 03:59:59 that genshin.py's `PartialTime` keeps only to the minute, is the next whole minute, so a season ends at the 04:00 reset as its calendar window does; floors in API order, at most 3: `round_num` as cycles for Memory of Chaos, `score` for Pure Fiction and Apocalyptic Shadow; Anomaly Arbitration from `records[0]`: stars = boss + mini-boss stars, deepest floor = the boss's name, no floors); `read_calendar(model, fetched_at) -> HsrCalendar`. genshin.py 1.7.31 parses the calendar's times as UTC and relabels them +08:00 without converting (`TZDateTime`), so every calendar time is anchored on the calendar's own `now`: absolute = `item_time + round(fetched_at - calendar.now)`, the difference rounded to the nearest 15 minutes (any offset of the API's clock is whole quarter hours, while request latency and the PC clock's skew are seconds and would otherwise show a whole-minute end one minute early), labelled with `fetched_at`'s zone; correct whichever clock the API uses, and HoYoLAB's own minute (since v1.27.1 a time on that minute's last second, such as a warp's 11:59:59 server time, is the next whole minute, through `game_data.on_the_minute`). Whatever is over at `fetched_at` is dropped; upcoming warps, events, and endgame windows are kept with their start, so a cached calendar still has what starts after it was read (a new Memory of Chaos season, a new version's events). Warps split their characters and Light Cones by rarity (5 → featured, 4 → four_stars); unknown challenge types are skipped.

`gacha/hoyolab.py` (additive; existing methods unchanged):
- Error kind `chronicle_private` (`DataNotPublic` from these HSR endpoints only; notes keep `notes_off`), message `Data HSR ini tidak bisa dibaca karena Battle Chronicle di HoYoLAB tidak publik. Pengaturannya ada di HoYoLAB, di Battle Chronicle; Cyrene tidak mengubahnya.` (since v1.23 the message names the game it is about, see Genshin and ZZZ data). Not a watcher or reminder kind.
- `HoyolabClient(..., now: Callable[[], datetime] = <aware UTC now>)`: the anchor for calendar times.
- `hsr_characters() -> tuple[HsrCharacter, ...]` (one `get_starrail_characters(uid)`: detailed, not `simple`); `hsr_endgame(modes: Iterable[str]) -> dict[str, HsrEndgame | GachaError]` (like `status`: `get_starrail_challenge`, `get_starrail_pure_fiction`, `get_starrail_apc_shadow`, `get_anomaly_arbitration(uid, schedule_type=1)`, gathered with `return_exceptions` behind a semaphore of 2, one failing mode never hides the others; a failure of the whole call gives every mode that error); `hsr_calendar() -> HsrCalendar` (`get_starrail_event_calendar(uid)`, `fetched_at` read before the request). Each is one `_run` with the 20 s timeout and a fresh client, the uid from `_resolve_uids` (a missing HSR account is `account`), never `raw`, `previous`, or a language other than en-us. A model that fails to parse (a new Path, a missing field) is `other`.

`gacha/hsr_tools.py` — since v1.23 `gacha/game_data_tools.py`, whose `game_characters`, `game_endgame`, and `game_events` (with an optional `game`) replace the three tools below and give the same HSR results (see Genshin and ZZZ data). `HsrTools(source: HsrSource, *, now=<local now>)`, a `ToolProvider` (`HsrSource` is the Protocol of the three HoyolabClient methods). Specs are static English (the prompt cache), strict, with optional arguments not listed in `required`:
- `hsr_characters {name?: string}` (free text, at most 60 characters, never an enum of owned characters). Without `name`: `{"as_of", "count", "columns": ["name", "rarity", "level", "eidolon", "path", "element", "light_cone", "lc_level", "superimposition"], "rows": [[...], ...]}`, rows sorted by rarity, then level (both descending), then name; at most 120 rows (`"truncated": true` beyond); an 80-character roster fits in 8 kB. With `name`: matched after NFKD + casefold with punctuation and spaces removed, exact first (the name, or the name with its Path in either order: "Trailblazer Harmony" is that one character), then substring of the name, at most 3 characters (both March 7th Paths and the Trailblazer's Paths come back together, told apart by `path`): `{"as_of", "query", "characters": [{"name", "rarity", "level", "eidolon", "path", "element", "light_cone"?: {"name", "level", "superimposition", "rarity"}, "stats": {...at most 14}, "relics": [{"piece", "main", "value", "level"} ...at most 6], "memosprite"?}], "more"?: [{"name", "path"} ...at most 6]}`, each character at most 1.5 kB; `more` names the matches beyond the 3 (a fourth Trailblazer Path) so she can ask for one by name and Path; no match: `"characters": []` and `"close"`: up to 3 `difflib.get_close_matches` names (not an error: a name misheard by the STT still resolves).
- `hsr_endgame {mode?: enum[ENDGAME_MODES]}` (none = every mode): `{"as_of", "modes": [{"mode", "name", "season"?, "ends"?, "ends_in_hours"?, "has_data", "stars", "extra_stars", "deepest_floor"?, "battles"?, "floors": [{"name", "stars", "cycles"? | "score"?}]}], "errors"?: [{"mode", "message"}]}`; keys without a value are left out; no maximum-stars field (no source confirms one, so she never says how many stars are missing); an error result only when every requested mode failed; four modes fit in 2 kB.
- `hsr_events {}`: `{"as_of", "version"?, "warps": [{"type", "name"? (HoYoLAB often gives none), "featured", "four_stars", "starts"?, "starts_in_hours"?, "ends"?, "ends_in_hours"?}], "events": [{"name", "ends"?, "ends_in_hours"?, "current"?, "total"?, "done"}], "endgame": [{"mode", "name", "ends"?, "ends_in_hours"?, "current"?, "total"?}]}`; `starts` only for a warp that has not started; only the events and endgame windows running (started and not over), which carry no start; at most 8 warps, 15 events (soonest end first, names cut to 60 characters), 4 endgame windows, counted after leaving out whatever ended and the events and windows yet to start; rewards, descriptions, and icons never; a typical calendar (4 warps, 10 events, 4 windows) fits in 3 kB, one at these caps with every event name at 60 characters in 5 kB.
- Times are local (`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM`, `as_of` the fetch time), `ends_in_hours`/`starts_in_hours` rounded up and never below 0, computed when answering so a cached result stays right; for the same reason a cached calendar leaves out the warps, events, and endgame windows that ended since it was read, and lists the events and endgame windows that started since. Compact JSON, `ensure_ascii=False`. A `GachaError` becomes `{"ok": false, "message"}` with is_error; invalid arguments and unknown names as in `GachaTools`; `run` never raises.
- An in-memory cache per process, keyed `characters`, `endgame:<mode>`, `calendar`, with TTLs of 15, 10, and 60 minutes, holding only successes; asking for every mode reuses the fresh modes and fetches only the stale ones. A lock guards the cache, never held during a fetch (voice and phone turns run on different threads).
- Descriptions say: call it only when Master asks about Honkai: Star Rail, never in her own greeting or the morning briefing (that turn offers every tool); use it instead of guessing; answer only what was asked in one or two short sentences, at most about three names, no lists or parentheses (her TTS drops parenthesized asides); keep the official English terms (Eidolon, Light Cone, Superimposition, Memory of Chaos); comment only from the returned data and say so when a character or Light Cone is new to her; never claim how many stars are missing; present banners only as information; never suggest pulling, spending money, or topping up; do not save the data with `remember` unless Master asks.

CLI: `run` and `chat` add `HsrTools(gacha.hoyolab)` after `GachaTools` when `gacha.enabled` and `hsr` is in `gacha.games` (since v1.23 `GameDataTools` for every configured game, see Genshin and ZZZ data). `waifu gacha hsr characters|endgame|events` (works with `gacha.enabled` off, like the other `gacha` commands; no cache, always fresh): one line per character with its Path, which tells apart the characters HoYoLAB names alike (`Acheron ★5 Lv80 E2 (Nihility), Along the Passing Shore Lv80 S1`, or `tanpa Light Cone`), one line per mode (stars, deepest floor, `berakhir <local time> (<duration>)`), or the warps (an upcoming one with `mulai <local time>`) and the running events and endgame windows; a `GachaError` prints the failure and exits 1. `check` is unchanged (offline). Logging also holds the `genshin.models` logger at ERROR, even with `-v` (v1.21.1), so genshin.py's own "Unknown <enum>: ..., please report to developer" parsing notices (for example `HSREventStatus` `DoubleRewardActStatusFinish` on every HSR calendar read) stay off the console and the log file; the HoYoLAB errors Cyrene logs herself (`waifu.gacha.*`) are not affected. `config/persona.md`'s game section names the three tools (v1.23 renames them) and the questions they answer, and uses them only when Master asks about HSR, never for a greeting or the morning briefing. README (Indonesian): what she can tell, examples, privacy (with the `-v` exception) and the Battle Chronicle setting, and manual checklist items that compare the CLI and her answers with the game (warp and season ends, stars, Eidolon and Superimposition).

Safety: tests fake genshin.py's client with real genshin.py models built from deep-copied sample dicts (its validators change their input), assert that `update_settings` is never called and that the cookie, uid, and nickname appear in no result or log; nothing reaches HoYoLAB.

### PC health (v1.17)

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, PC-ku gimana?", "GPU-ku panas nggak?", "RAM kepake berapa?", "disk C sisa berapa?" → she reads the PC right then: CPU load, RAM, each fixed drive's free space, the NVIDIA GPU's temperature, load, VRAM, and power draw, and how long since Windows last restarted (sleep and a Fast Startup shutdown do not reset it, so it is not the time since Master turned the PC on), and answers in a sentence or two.
2. While she runs, she speaks up by herself (and pushes the same text to the phone when notifications are on) when a fixed drive has less than `pc_health.disk_warn_gb` free (once per drive per day, local date), or the GPU has stayed at or above `pc_health.gpu_warn_c` for `pc_health.gpu_warn_minutes` (once per hour at most).
3. `waifu pc` prints the same report.

Non-goals (v1.17): CPU temperature (Windows exposes none without a kernel driver such as LibreHardwareMonitor's), fans, per-process lists (process names would go to the model), ending processes, cleaning disks, overclocking, non-NVIDIA GPU details (the GPU part is then simply absent), history graphs.

Privacy: only numbers, drive letters, and the GPU's model name; no process, file, or user names; nothing is written to disk (the once-per-day/hour notice memory lives in the running process, so a restart may repeat a notice once).

Config `pc_health` (committed with its tests before the parallel tasks): `enabled` (true), `notices` (true: the proactive part; false keeps only the tool and `waifu pc`), `poll_seconds` (60, 15–3600), `disk_warn_gb` (10, 1–2000), `gpu_warn_c` (85, 60–100), `gpu_warn_minutes` (3, 1–60).

`pc_health.py` (no new dependency; Windows calls through `ctypes.WinDLL` with full `argtypes`/`restype`; every collaborator injected; nothing here raises to the caller — a part that cannot be read is None and is logged once at WARNING):
- Values (frozen dataclasses, committed with the config): `Drive(letter: str, free_gb: float, total_gb: float)`, `GpuStatus(name: str, temp_c: int | None, load_percent: int | None, vram_used_gb: float | None, vram_total_gb: float | None, power_w: float | None)`, `PcSnapshot(cpu_percent: float | None, ram_used_gb: float | None, ram_total_gb: float | None, drives: tuple[Drive, ...], gpu: GpuStatus | None, uptime_s: int | None)`. Gigabytes are GiB (2**30 bytes), as Windows Explorer shows them.
- `CpuLoad(*, system_times=<kernel32.GetSystemTimes>, sleep=time.sleep, clock=time.monotonic)`: `percent()` is the busy share ((kernel + user − idle) / (kernel + user) of the deltas; kernel time includes idle) since the previous call when that sample is at most 10 s old, else (and at the first call, or when no timer tick has passed) over a fresh 0.5 s, so the load is the one of the last few seconds, never an average over minutes or hours; never below 0 or above 100.
- `memory()` via `GlobalMemoryStatusEx` (used = total − available); `fixed_drives(*, ..., dos_device=<kernel32.QueryDosDeviceW>)` via `GetLogicalDrives` + `GetDriveTypeW` (DRIVE_FIXED only; removable, network, and optical drives are skipped) + `shutil.disk_usage`, leaving out a SUBST letter (`QueryDosDeviceW("S:")` gives `\??\<folder>`; it would report its drive's type and space a second time; a letter whose target cannot be read is kept, and the folder is never logged); `uptime_s()` via `GetTickCount64` (since Windows last restarted; sleep and a Fast Startup shutdown do not reset it).
- `gpu_status(*, run=subprocess.run, nvidia_smi=<shutil.which or %SystemRoot%\System32\nvidia-smi.exe>, thread=threading.Thread)`: `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,temperature.gpu,utilization.gpu,memory.used,memory.total,power.draw --format=csv,noheader,nounits`, timeout 5 s, `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`, the first GPU's line; `[N/A]`/`[Not Supported]` fields are None; no nvidia-smi, a failure, or a timeout is None. It runs in a daemon thread given up on after 7 s (None), since after its timeout `subprocess.run` kills nvidia-smi and then waits without a limit for it to exit, which one stuck in the driver may not do; until that one has exited, a call is None at once and starts no other nvidia-smi (a new one would get stuck too, a process and threads more at every poll).
- `read_snapshot(*, cpu: CpuLoad, memory=..., drives=..., gpu=..., uptime=...) -> PcSnapshot`.

`brain/pc_tools.py` — `PcTools(read: Callable[[], PcSnapshot], *, owner_call)` (`ToolProvider`, a strict tool `pc_health {}`, static English description): `{"cpu_percent", "ram": {"used_gb", "total_gb", "percent"}, "drives": [{"drive": "C:", "free_gb", "total_gb", "free_percent"}], "gpu"?: {"name", "temp_c", "load_percent", "vram_used_gb", "vram_total_gb", "power_w"}, "uptime_hours"}`, numbers rounded (one decimal for GB and watts, whole percents), parts that are None left out. The description: `uptime_hours` is the time since Windows last restarted, not since Master turned the PC on (sleep and a Fast Startup shutdown do not reset it); use it when Master asks how the PC is doing, about temperature, load, memory, or disk space; never guess; answer in one or two short sentences with rounded numbers; there is no CPU temperature (say so when asked); never suggest buying hardware; she may suggest closing heavy programs or cleaning the disk.

`pc_watch.py` — `PcWatcher(read, config: PcHealthConfig, *, announce: Announce | None, notifier: Notifier | None, owner_call, title="Cyrene", clock=time.monotonic, now=datetime.now, sleep_event=threading.Event)`: `poll_once() -> list[str]` (the notices it gave) and `start()`/`stop()` (a daemon thread polling every `poll_seconds`, stopped promptly by `stop()`):
- Disk: a fixed drive below `disk_warn_gb` free → `"{owner}, disk C: tinggal 8,4 GB. Mungkin perlu dibersihkan sedikit."` (the free space rounded down to one decimal, so a drive just under the line never reads as the line: 9.96 is `9,9`) once per drive per local date (the drive going back above the line and dipping again the same day stays quiet).
- GPU: `temp_c ≥ gpu_warn_c` in every poll for at least `gpu_warn_minutes` (by `clock`; a poll below the line or with no reading restarts the streak) → `"{owner}, GPU-nya sudah {t}°C beberapa menit. Istirahat sebentar atau cek pendinginnya, ya."` at most once per hour; announced with `game_expires_at` an hour later on `now` (v1.34, v1.34.1: one a game held unsaid until then is dropped, since the next could be told by then; a long hot game never piles them up; a focus block alone holds it as long as it lasts, as in v1.30). The disk notice never expires.
- Each notice goes to `announce(text, "sad", game_expires_at=...)` (None for the disk notice; rain_watch's `Announce`; for a worried face, the closest the avatar has, as for the gacha watcher's problems; she says it between utterances, as the gacha watcher's notices) and, when given, to the notifier (push failures logged, never raised); a failing `read` is logged and the thread goes on.

CLI: `run` and `chat` add `PcTools` when `pc_health.enabled` (both brain backends); `run` starts `PcWatcher` after "ready" when `pc_health.enabled` and `pc_health.notices`, stopped on exit. `waifu pc` prints the report (Indonesian, one line per part, the uptime as `Sejak restart: 5 jam 12 menit`; `GPU: tidak terbaca (bukan NVIDIA atau nvidia-smi tidak ada)` when absent) and works whether or not `pc_health.enabled`. `check` adds `✓ Kesehatan PC: aktif, GPU <name>` / `! Kesehatan PC: aktif, GPU tidak terbaca` / `! Kesehatan PC mati`. README (Indonesian) section "Kesehatan PC": examples, the notices and their settings, what she cannot read (CPU temperature), privacy.

Safety: tests never call the real Windows functions or nvidia-smi (fakes for every collaborator; the watcher's clock, date, and waits are injected); no audio, no push, no network.

### web search and free offers (v1.18)

User stories (the owner, 2026-10-16: "web search for free offers" means both a general web search and free offers; offers are free games and also prestigious AI models' programs, such as Claude for Startups, that can be claimed free or applied for):

1. "Cyrene, cari berita terbaru soal ...", "harga RTX 5070 sekarang berapa?", "kapan HSR 4.7 rilis?" → she searches the web (Claude's server-side web search) and answers in a sentence or two, naming where she read it.
2. "Ada game gratis apa sekarang?" → the free games running now and coming soon on Epic Games Store (from Epic itself) and on Steam, GOG, and others (from GamerPower.com), with when each ends.
3. "Ada promo AI gratis nggak?" → AI programs she found in her last scan: free credits, free tiers, startup or student programs of well-known AI providers, each marked as claimable now or needing an application, with eligibility and deadline.
4. While she runs, she speaks up by herself (and pushes to the phone with the link to open) when a new free game appears, and after each AI scan (weekly by default) when it found new offers. "Kirim linknya ke HP" pushes an offer's link to the phone. She never claims, applies, signs in, or buys anything.

Non-goals (v1.18): claiming or applying on the owner's behalf, accounts or sign-ins, paid discounts (only free-to-keep games and free/apply-for AI programs), loot/DLC/beta giveaways, HoYoverse redeem codes (a later version), reading pages with web fetch (search only), web search with the Ollama backend (Claude's server tool only), opening links on the PC.

Privacy and safety: the web search runs on Anthropic's servers with only the query the model writes; the AI scan sends no personal data beyond the country (Indonesia). Search results and scanned pages are untrusted data: the system prompt says so, and the scan runs in a request that has no device, PC, memory, or schedule tools at all. Links are never read aloud; they go to the phone only when the owner asks or with a notice.

Config (committed with its tests before the parallel tasks):
- `web_search`: `enabled` (true), `max_uses` (3 searches per request, 1–10; one turn may take up to `max_tool_rounds` + 1 requests), `country` ("ID", two uppercase letters), `timezone` ("Asia/Jakarta", IANA), `city` (none: then `briefing.city`; a non-empty string otherwise).
- `free_offers`: `enabled` (true), `games` (true), `game_platforms` (default `[epic, steam, gog]`, from `epic`, `steam`, `gog`, `itchio`, `pc`), `games_poll_hours` (6, 1–48), `ai` (true), `ai_scan_days` (7, 1–30), `ai_max_searches` (10 per request, 1–20; a scan may take up to 6 requests), `notices` (true; off also stops the weekly AI scan, which only the watcher runs), `state_path` (`data/free_offers.json`).
- `usage.web_search_per_1000_usd` (10.0, ≥ 0): search fees in the cost monitor.

Values (`offers/values.py`, frozen dataclasses, committed with the config): `GameGiveaway(id: str, title: str, platform: str, source: str, worth_usd: float | None, starts_at: datetime | None, ends_at: datetime | None, ends_on: date | None, url: str)` (exact aware times from Epic; GamerPower gives only `ends_on`, a date: its times are not precise; `starts_at` in the future means upcoming); `AiOffer(id: str, title: str, provider: str, kind: str, access: str, eligibility: str, deadline: date | None, url: str, summary: str, first_seen: datetime)` with `kind` one of `free_credits`, `free_tier`, `startup_program`, `student_program`, `trial`, `other` and `access` one of `claim`, `apply`.

Web search in the brain (`brain/claude.py`, `brain/factory.py`, `brain/persona.py`, `usage.py`):
- When `web_search.enabled` and the backend is Claude, the frozen request's tools end with `{"type": "web_search_20260209", "name": "web_search", "max_uses": max_uses, "user_location": {"type": "approximate", "country", "timezone", "city"?}}` (static for the brain's lifetime; no `code_execution` tool alongside it: the 20260209 version filters with it internally). Only models with dynamic filtering accept that version (`DYNAMIC_FILTERING_MODELS` in `brain/claude.py`: Fable 5.1 and 5, Opus 5.5, 5, 4.8, 4.7, 4.6, Sonnet 5.5, 5, 4.6, from the claude-api reference); any other `brain.claude.model` (Haiku 5.5, for example) gets `web_search_20250305` with the same fields, in the brain and in the AI scan. Server-tool blocks (`server_tool_use`, `web_search_tool_result`) are kept in the history exactly as returned (append-only, preserved thinking); `pause_turn` continues the turn as today, counted against `max_tool_rounds`. A turn that would end with a `server_tool_use` that has no result in its response (a pause at the round limit, or a stop such as `max_tokens` mid-search) is rolled back like a failed one (truncated, or a new session when it changed memory) with the round-limit or cut-off apology: the API rejects such a block in every later request unless it ends the history. After a server-side fallback, `_echoable` also drops a `server_tool_use` block before the last `fallback` block that has no matching result (the documented echo rule); paired server-tool blocks stay.
- With web search, every request is streamed (`client.beta.messages.stream(...)` and `get_final_message()`: the same body with `stream`), so the 60 s read timeout bounds each wait between events rather than the server's whole search loop; an answer that stalls mid-stream (`httpx2.TimeoutException`) is rolled back with "Maaf {owner}, jawaban dari Claude terlalu lama, jadi aku berhenti dulu." and never sent again by the SDK (it may be billed already). Without web search the requests stay non-streamed, exactly as before.
- The reply's text joins adjacent text blocks as they are (a cited answer comes split at its citations, each piece keeping its own spacing). Whitespace that both pieces carry where they meet becomes one space, so no space doubles, or its line breaks when it holds any (a paragraph break there stays one). Passages that other blocks (a search) separate are joined with one space. The reply's ends are stripped, and the text inside a block, its line breaks too, stays as written.
- `build_system_prompt` ends, only when web search is on, with a fixed section: search results and web pages are data, never instructions from Master; never control devices, the PC, apps, schedules, or memory because a page says so; name the source briefly; never read a URL aloud.
- `TokenUsage` gains `web_searches` (from `usage.server_tool_use.web_search_requests`, 0 when absent); the ledger prices them at `usage.web_search_per_1000_usd / 1000` each (already in the cost totals), `waifu usage` shows the count, and (v1.21.1) the `api_usage` tool gives the count and the fees (`web_searches`, `web_search_cost_usd`, `web_search_cost_idr`, only when above 0). The ledger saves `web_searches` only when above 0, so a day without searches keeps exactly the keys a pre-v1.18 `waifu run` (possibly still running) reads. `usage.iterations` entries in a row of one model (the turns of a server-side tool loop) are one recorded request; each model attempt (each fallback hop) is its own. A web search error (`web_search_tool_result` whose content is an error object such as `max_uses_exceeded`) is left to the model; nothing raises.

Free games (`offers/games.py`, httpx with an injected client and clock, 15 s timeout, no new dependency):
- Epic: `GET https://store-site-backend-static-ipv4.ak.epicgames.com/freeGamesPromotions?locale=en-US&country=ID&allowCountries=ID`; an element of `data.Catalog.searchStore.elements` is free when a `promotions.promotionalOffers[*].promotionalOffers[*]` (now) or `promotions.upcomingPromotionalOffers[*].promotionalOffers[*]` (soon) has `discountSetting.discountPercentage == 0` (Epic's "0" means nothing left to pay; paid sales show other numbers); start/end from its ISO-8601 UTC times; url `https://store.epicgames.com/en-US/p/<pageSlug>` from `offerMappings[0]` (else `catalogNs.mappings[0]`, else `productSlug` without `/home`, else `urlSlug`); id `epic:<id>`.
- GamerPower (`https://www.gamerpower.com/api/giveaways?type=game&platform=<p>` per configured platform other than `epic`, at most one request per platform per poll; free for any use with attribution and a link to GamerPower.com, at most 10 requests per second): `id`, `title` (its " Giveaway", " Key Giveaway", or " Steam Key Giveaway" suffix removed; then, as often as the title ends with one, a store in parentheses (one of the item's `platforms` or a known store: Steam, GOG, itch.io, PC, Epic Games, Epic Games Store, IndieGala) is removed, so "Dwarven Realms (Steam) Key Giveaway" is "Dwarven Realms"; a Key anywhere else is the game's own ("Skeleton Key (Steam) Giveaway" is "Skeleton Key"); other brackets (an edition, a year, "Portal 2 (Soundtrack)") and words such as "DLC" stay; a title that would then be empty keeps GamerPower's own title; a title that is blank from GamerPower is skipped as unreadable), `worth` ("$19.99" or "N/A"), `end_date` ("YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS" read as a date only, or "N/A"), `open_giveaway_url` (else `gamerpower_url`), `status == "Active"` only; items also listed for "Epic Games Store" are skipped when Epic itself is read; id `gamerpower:<id>`.
- `read_free_games(platforms, *, http, now) -> tuple[GameGiveaway, ...] | OffersError` (each source fails alone: one source's error is logged and the other's games still come back; both failing is an `OffersError` with an Indonesian message); games that ended are dropped.

AI offers scan (`offers/ai_scan.py`): `scan_ai_offers(*, client_factory, model, fallbacks, max_searches, now, on_usage, known: Sequence[AiOffer]) -> list[AiOffer] | OffersError`: one Claude conversation (the configured Claude model and effort `medium`, the server-side fallback as in `ClaudeBrain` only when `brain.claude.fallbacks` is on, top-level `cache_control` so each search-loop turn and continuation reads the results so far from the cache) with only two tools: `web_search_20260209` (`web_search_20250305` for a model without dynamic filtering, as in the brain; `max_uses = ai_max_searches` per request, country ID) and a strict client tool `report_offers {offers: [{title, provider, kind, access, eligibility, deadline: "YYYY-MM-DD" | null, url, summary}]}`. The prompt (English, static): find offers currently open of well-known AI providers and models (searched family by family: Anthropic's Claude for Startups and education credits, OpenAI, Google for Startups and Gemini, Microsoft for Startups, AWS Activate, the GitHub Student Developer Pack, xAI, Mistral, Meta's Llama, Hugging Face, then anything else notable) that give free credits, a free tier, or a startup, research, or student program, free to claim or by application, available to someone in Indonesia; only from the provider's own pages or reputable reporting; no referral schemes, giveaways that need payment, or offers that ended; the already-known titles are listed (marked as data from earlier scans) so it reports the new ones and again each known one still open, with its title as listed (an open offer stays kept, and one unreported for 90 days has ended); finish by calling `report_offers` once. The loop handles `pause_turn` (at most 5 continuations) and ends when `report_offers` is called (its input validated in code; invalid entries dropped and logged); a turn that ends without it, a refusal, or an API error is an `OffersError`, `billed` once any response arrived (`OffersError(message, *, billed=False)`). Every response's usage goes to `on_usage` (the cost monitor). URLs must pass `notify.is_https_link` (https, a plain host with no user name or port, no backslash, space, or non-printable character: these could hide the real host); title and provider at most 120 characters and eligibility and summary at most 300 (`offers/checks.py` `MAX_CHARS`: page text reaches her conversation and voice), a longer entry dropped; ids are a hash of the normalized title and provider; `first_seen` is `now`.

Links (`offers/checks.py`): `official_link(url)` is true for a link a notification may open (`notify.is_https_link`) whose host is one of `OFFICIAL_HOSTS` or is, or is under, one of `OFFICIAL_DOMAINS`: sites where nobody but one of the named providers publishes. Domains, with every host under them: anthropic.com, claude.com, startup.google.com, edu.google.com, blog.google, deepmind.google, x.ai, ai.meta.com, llama.com, aws.amazon.com, azure.microsoft.com, startups.microsoft.com, education.github.com, docs.github.com. Single hosts (anyone can publish on other hosts of their domain): openai.com, www.openai.com, ai.google.dev, cloud.google.com, mistral.ai, console.mistral.ai, docs.mistral.ai, microsoft.com, www.microsoft.com. Hosts where anyone can publish never count, even on a provider's domain: github.com and gist.github.com (anyone's repositories), huggingface.co (anyone's Spaces and repositories), claude.ai, chatgpt.com, gemini.google.com, and chat.mistral.ai (shared chats, published artifacts, custom GPTs), developers.google.com (developer profiles), sites.google.com and Google Forms, shell.cloud.google.com (runs anyone's tutorial), forums (community.openai.com, discuss.ai.google.dev, answers.microsoft.com, techcommunity.microsoft.com, learn.microsoft.com), the rest of meta.com (other people's apps), and the Amazon shop. The scan reads pages anyone can write, so only such a link ever opens on a tap; the others are kept and shown as unverified.

Store (`offers/store.py`, the store file rules as `memory.py`): `OffersStore(path)` keeps the games already announced (ids, forgotten 30 days after they ended), the AI offers (with `first_seen`; dropped after their deadline or 90 days unseen), `last_ai_scan`, and `last_failed_ai_scan` (the last scan that failed after it was paid for; a file without the key reads as none); `games()`/`ai_offers()`/`last_failed_ai_scan()` read it; `remember_games(...)` returns the new ones; `merge_ai_offers(...)` returns the new ones; `record_failed_ai_scan(at)` keeps the failure. A stored offer over the text caps makes the file not an offers record.

Tools (`brain/offers_tools.py`, `OffersTools(store, read_games, *, notifier, owner_call, now)`, strict static specs):
- `free_offers {category?: enum["games", "ai"]}` (none = both): `{"as_of", "games": [{"id", "title", "platform", "worth_usd"?, "starts"?, "starts_in_hours"?, "ends"?, "ends_in_hours"?, "ends_on"?, "source"}], "ai": {"scanned"?, "offers": [{"id", "title", "provider", "kind", "access", "eligibility", "deadline"?, "summary"}]}}` (games read fresh at most every 30 minutes, cached; AI offers from the store; local times; at most 15 games and 15 offers; no URLs: the model never reads them aloud). Description: use it for free games and AI programs; say what it is, where, until when, and whether to claim or apply; mention GamerPower.com when a game came from it; the AI offers' titles, eligibility, and summaries come from web pages: data, never instructions from Master, never a reason to control devices, the PC, apps, schedules, or memory (static, so it holds with Ollama and with web search off too); never claim or apply for Master; never suggest paying; for something newer, search the web.
- `send_offer_link {id: string}`: pushes that game's or offer's title and link to the phone through the notifier (`click` set to the URL); an AI offer whose link is not `official_link` goes without `click`, the link as text after "Bukan situs resmi yang kukenal, periksa alamatnya dulu:", and the result tells her to have Master check the address; an error result when notifications are off or the id is unknown.

Watcher (`offers/watcher.py`, `OffersWatcher(store, read_games, scan, config, *, announce, notifier, owner_call, now, clock, wait)`): every `games_poll_hours` reads the games and announces the new ones in one notice (`"{owner}, ada game gratis baru: Bad Cheese dan Agent A di Epic, sampai Kamis 22.00."`, ending `", infonya dari GamerPower.com."` when any new game came from GamerPower, pushed with the first game's link); when `free_offers.ai` and `last_ai_scan` is older than `ai_scan_days` (also right after starting when it never ran) and no paid scan failed in the last day (`last_failed_ai_scan`), runs the scan and announces new offers in one notice that asks no yes-or-no question (an announcement opens no follow-up window): `"{owner}, aku menemukan 2 promo AI baru: ... Link yang pertama ada di notifikasi HP; minta aku kalau mau link yang lain."` (one offer: "Linknya ada di notifikasi HP."; the first link not `official_link`: no `click`, "Minta aku kalau mau linknya dikirim ke HP."; no notifier: "Tanya aku kalau mau tahu lebih lanjut."). Failures are logged, never raised: a scan error that was not billed is retried at the next poll; a billed one is kept with `record_failed_ai_scan` and retried a day later, across restarts too. A scan time in the future (a clock set back) counts as long ago. `stop()` is prompt; nothing runs when `free_offers.enabled` or `notices` is off.

Notifier (`notify.py`): `send(title, message, *, tags=(), click: str | None = None)`; ntfy gets the `Click` header only for a link that passes `is_https_link(url)`: https, a host of only ASCII letters, digits, dots, and hyphens (so no user name or port), and no backslash, space, or non-printable character anywhere (a browser reads `https://evil.example\.anthropic.com/` as evil.example; Python as a host under anthropic.com). Any other link is left out and logged; the message still goes.

CLI: `run`/`chat` add `OffersTools` when `free_offers.enabled` (both backends; the AI part reads the store, so it works with Ollama too) and start `OffersWatcher` in `run` after "ready" when `notices`; `waifu offers [games|ai]` prints them with their links (and "Sumber: GamerPower.com" when used; an AI offer's link that is not `official_link` marked "(belum terverifikasi: bukan situs resmi penyedia yang dikenal)"); `waifu offers ai --scan` runs a scan now and prints its cost from the usage of its responses; one AI scan runs at a time across processes (`InstanceLock` on `<state_path>.scan.lock`, taken in `_ai_scan`): one that finds it held is an unbilled `OffersError` ("Pencarian promo AI lain sedang berjalan, misalnya oleh 'waifu run'. Coba lagi setelah selesai."); `check` adds lines for web search (on with Claude with `max_uses` per request and `max_uses × (max_tool_rounds + 1)` per question, unavailable with Ollama, off) and free offers (the last scan time; with `notices` off and `ai` on, "tanpa pemberitahuan dan tanpa pencarian promo AI mingguan"). README (Indonesian): web search (what it costs: $10 per 1000 searches plus tokens), free games with the GamerPower credit, AI promos (weekly scan, about the cost of a few searches and tokens), privacy, that she never claims anything, and that a `waifu run` from before v1.18 is restarted before `chat` with Claude or `--scan`.

Safety: tests never reach Epic, GamerPower, or Claude (injected HTTP clients and fake Anthropic clients; conftest also refuses network beyond loopback), never push (fake notifier), and use tmp state paths.

### redeem codes (v1.19)

User stories (the owner plays HSR, Genshin, and ZZZ on the Asia server; the gacha buddy's cookie holds `cookie_token_v2`, `account_id_v2`, and `account_mid_v2`, which redemption needs):

1. While she runs, a new redeem code for one of the owner's games shows up → she says it and pushes it to the phone: `"{owner}, ada 2 kode HSR baru: 60 Stellar Jade, dan 30 Stellar Jade plus Traveler's Guide. Mau kutukarkan?"`.
2. "Iya, tukarkan" / "redeem kode HSR yang baru" / "pakai kode ABC123 di Genshin" → she redeems them on HoYoLAB for the owner's account and says what happened (redeemed, already used, expired, invalid, region-locked, level too low); the rewards arrive in the in-game mail.
3. "Ada kode baru nggak?" → the codes known for each game, which are still to redeem, and what they give.

Non-goals (v1.19): codes for other games, codes that need the game client (web event codes), redeeming without the owner asking unless `auto_redeem` is on, scraping fan sites directly, telling which codes are region-specific beyond what HoYoLAB answers.

Source: the community API behind Hoyo Buddy's auto-redemption, `GET https://hoyo-codes.seria.moe/codes?game=<hkrpg|genshin|nap>` → `{"codes": [{"id", "code", "status", "game", "rewards"}], "game"}` (the codes are checked against HoYoLAB with an Asia account; GPL-3.0 project, no published terms for others, so it is asked once soon after each `waifu run` start, then at most once per game every `poll_hours`, plus each `waifu gacha codes`); only `status == "OK"` (the source drops a code once it finds it no longer works); a code is trimmed, uppercased, and kept only when it matches `^[A-Z0-9]{5,24}$`; rewards text at most 200 characters (longer is cut).

Config `redeem_codes` (committed with its tests before the parallel tasks): `enabled` (true), `poll_hours` (6, 1–48), `auto_redeem` (false), `notices` (true), `state_path` (`data/redeem_codes.json`). The games are `gacha.games`; listing codes needs no cookie, redeeming needs `gacha.cookie`.

Values (`gacha/codes.py` top, committed with the config): `RedeemCode(game: GameId, code: str, rewards: str, first_seen: datetime)`; `REDEEM_OUTCOMES = ("redeemed", "claimed", "expired", "invalid", "region", "level", "cooldown", "cookie", "error")`; `RedeemResult(game: GameId, code: str, outcome: str, message: str)` (the message Indonesian, for her to say).

Modules:
- `gacha/codes.py`: `read_codes(games, *, http) -> dict[GameId, list[tuple[str, str]]] | CodesError` (one request per game, 15 s timeout, a game failing alone is logged and left out; all failing is a `CodesError` with an Indonesian message).
- `gacha/code_store.py` — `CodeStore(path)` (the store file rules as `memory.py`): per game the codes seen (`first_seen`, `last_seen`, rewards, whether the latest read listed it, whether Master named it), each code's last outcome, and which were announced; `add(game, codes, now) -> list[RedeemCode]` (a successful read of that game: the new ones; a kept code it leaves out is no longer listed); `add_named(game, codes, now)` (codes Master named: kept as Master's and announced, since Master knows them); `unredeemed(game)` (no final outcome, and listed by the latest read or named by Master; final = redeemed, claimed, expired, invalid, region, level; newest first by `first_seen`, the source's order within a read); `unannounced(game)` (the same, not announced); `record(results)`; `mark_announced(codes)`; a code gone from the source for 60 days is forgotten (until then it is remembered, so it is not new when listed again).
- `gacha/hoyolab.py` (additive): `redeem_codes(game, codes) -> list[RedeemResult]`: the uid from `_resolve_uids`; codes redeemed one at a time with `client.redeem_code(code, uid, game=...)` and 6 s between attempts (HoYoLAB's cooldown is about 5 s; a `RedemptionCooldown` waits 6 s and tries once more); at most 10 codes per call; each attempt has its own 20 s timeout and the call as a whole a bound of 10 × 26 s; genshin.py errors map to outcomes: `RedemptionClaimed` → claimed, `RedemptionInvalid` with retcode -2001 → expired, other `RedemptionInvalid` → invalid, `RedemptionRegionLock` → region, `RedeemGameLevelTooLow` → level, `RedemptionCooldown` twice → cooldown, `InvalidCookies` → cookie (the message says the cookie needs `cookie_token_v2` and may have expired: copy it again from HoYoLAB), anything else → error (the message of a cooldown or error is `NOT_REDEEMED` with the code, then the problem); a cookie outcome, or an error because HoYoLAB is unreachable (a timeout or connection failure), rate-limiting (`TooManyRequests`, `VisitsTooFrequently`), or asks for a captcha, stops the rest (each remaining code gets the same outcome, untried); an optional `on_result` gets each result as soon as it is known; an optional `should_stop`, asked before each attempt and each gap, ends the run once it says so (the attempt HoYoLAB is answering is kept, a cooldown is not tried again, and the codes not tried yet are left out of the results); never logs the cookie or the uid; the waits are injected (`sleep`) for tests.
- `gacha/code_tools.py` — `CodeTools(store, hoyolab, games, *, now)`, a strict static provider: `redeem_codes_list {game?: enum[configured games]}` → `{"games": [{"game", "name", "to_redeem": [{"code", "rewards", "first_seen"}], "recent": [{"code", "rewards", "outcome"}] (the last 5)}]}`; `redeem_codes {game: enum[configured games], codes?: [string] (at most 10)}` → redeems the given codes (format-checked; kept with `add_named`) or, without `codes`, the first 10 unredeemed codes of that game (newest first; `left` counts the rest), records the results (a store that cannot be written is logged: HoYoLAB has answered, so the results still come back), and returns them `{"results": [{"code", "outcome", "message"}]}`; without a cookie, an error result telling to set `gacha.cookie`. Descriptions: redeem only when Master asks or agrees in this conversation; never invent a code; say the rewards arrive in the in-game mail; say an expired or used code plainly; after a cooldown, cookie, or error result, not again in the same turn; never suggest spending money.
- `gacha/code_watcher.py` — `CodeWatcher(store, read_codes, hoyolab, config, games, *, announce, notifier, owner_call, now, wait)`: every `poll_hours` (and once soon after start) reads the codes and adds them, so her answers stay current; with `notices` off it stops there (never tells or auto-redeems); when there are new ones: with `auto_redeem` (and a cookie) redeems them (each outcome recorded as soon as it is known, through `on_result`) and announces the outcome (`"{owner}, aku sudah menukarkan 2 kode HSR baru: 60 Stellar Jade... Hadiahnya ada di surat dalam game."`; a cooldown or error line in the push names its problem, and the notice says the problem when the codes left share one), otherwise announces them and asks (story 1); the push carries the codes and rewards in text; announced codes are marked so a restart never repeats them; a failing read, or a redemption call that raises, is logged and retried at the next poll; codes HoYoLAB could not redeem then (cooldown, cookie, error) are told once and wait for Master to ask; a store that cannot be written after redeeming, or a failing announcement or push, is logged and the notice still goes; `stop()` is prompt, and a poll in flight then redeems, says, and pushes nothing more (it passes `should_stop`, so redeeming ends before the next code also after `stop()` gave up waiting; the codes HoYoLAB already answered keep their outcomes, shown only in `redeem_codes_list`'s `recent` and `waifu gacha codes`, and are never told; the codes left untried wait for the next start); a daemon thread.

CLI: `run` and `chat` add `CodeTools` when `gacha.enabled` and `redeem_codes.enabled`; `run` starts `CodeWatcher` after "ready" (with `notices` off it only keeps the codes; `chat` alone knows the codes the last `run` or `waifu gacha codes` read); `waifu gacha codes [game] [--redeem]` prints each game's codes to redeem and recent outcomes (reading the source fresh and adding new codes), and with `--redeem` redeems that game's first 10 unredeemed codes, newest first (a game is required), and prints each result (then a warning when they could not be saved); `check` adds a line (on/off, how often, notices, auto-redeem, whether the cookie has `cookie_token_v2`, by name only; without a cookie, what that means for the plan: only told, not auto-redeemed, or redeeming fails). README (Indonesian): what she does, the source and its credit, that redeeming acts on the owner's account only when asked unless `auto_redeem`, the cookie fields it needs, the outcomes.

Safety: tests fake the source (injected HTTP client), genshin.py (a fake client recording `redeem_code` calls; it is never called with a real cookie), the waits, the clock, and the notifier; conftest refuses the network; nothing is redeemed by a test.

### to-do list (v1.20)

User stories (one of the owner's wishes in Cyrene's memory: "calendar/tasks"; schedules and reminders exist since v1.7, so this adds the tasks without a set time):

1. "Cyrene, tambah tugas: beli susu", "catat tugas kerjakan laporan, tenggatnya Jumat" → a task on her list, with a due date when one was said (she turns "Jumat" or "besok" into a date: the user turn starts with today's date and weekday).
2. "Tugasku apa aja?", "ada yang telat nggak?" → the open tasks, overdue first, then by due date, then the rest, briefly.
3. "Beli susu sudah", "tugas laporan selesai", "hapus tugas X", "ganti tenggat laporan jadi Senin" → done, removed, or changed.
4. Her morning greeting (v1.12) and "hari ini ada apa?" also mention the tasks due today and the overdue ones.
5. `waifu todo [add|done|remove|list]` from the terminal.

Non-goals (v1.20): times of day and reminders for tasks (a timed reminder stays a schedule entry; she can make one when asked), recurring tasks, sub-tasks, sharing, syncing with Google Calendar or other services, priorities beyond the order above.

Privacy: the list stays in `data/todo.json`; like memory notes, a task that holds a secret (`memory.looks_secret`: a password, PIN, OTP, token, or card number with its value) is refused, while a task that only names one ("ganti PIN ATM", "ingatkan ganti password wifi", "beli token listrik") is saved.

Config `todo` (committed with its tests before the parallel tasks): `enabled` (true), `path` (`data/todo.json`), `max_tasks` (100, 1–500 open tasks), `max_chars` (200, 20–1000), `keep_done_days` (7, 0–90: done tasks are kept that long for "what did I finish?", then forgotten).

Values (`todo.py` top, committed with the config): `Task(id: str, text: str, due: date | None, created: datetime, done: datetime | None)`; ids are "t1", "t2", ... never reused (like memory notes).

`todo.py` — `TodoStore(path, *, max_tasks, max_chars, keep_done_days, now=<aware local now>)` (the store file rules as `memory.py`: re-read per call, process-wide lock, atomic replace, never created by reading, a corrupt file moved aside): `add(text, due=None) -> Task` (`TodoError` with an Indonesian message when the text is empty, too long, looks like a secret, or the list is full; text whitespace-collapsed), `open_tasks() -> list[Task]` (overdue first, then by due date, then undated by creation), `done_tasks() -> list[Task]` (newest first), `complete(id) -> Task | None`, `reopen(id) -> Task | None`, `remove(id) -> Task | None`, `edit(id, *, text=None, due=<keep>|None) -> Task | None` (a sentinel tells "clear the due date" from "keep it"), `due_or_overdue(today) -> list[Task]`; done tasks older than `keep_done_days` are dropped on write.

`brain/todo_tools.py` — `TodoTools(store, *, now)`, strict static specs (static English descriptions; dates as "YYYY-MM-DD"):
- `add_task {text: string, due?: string}`, `list_tasks {include_done?: boolean}` → `{"today", "open": [{"id", "text", "due"?, "overdue"?}], "done"?: [{"id", "text", "done"}]}`, `complete_task {id}`, `reopen_task {id}`, `remove_task {id}`, `edit_task {id, text?: string, due?: string, clear_due?: boolean}` (only `clear_due` true removes the due date; with `due` too it is an error result).
- A null argument counts as left out, as local models send null for the optional arguments they do not use: `edit_task {id, text, due: null}` changes the text and keeps the due date.
- Descriptions: add a task only when Master asks to note something to do; a task with a time of day belongs in a schedule reminder instead (say so or make both when asked); pick the task by its text from `list_tasks` when Master names it; confirm briefly, saying the weekday and date when a due date is set; never read every task aloud unless asked: say how many and the most urgent.
- Results are compact JSON; an unknown id or a `TodoError` is an error result with its Indonesian message; `run` never raises.

Briefing (`briefing.py`): `BriefingTools(..., todo: TodoStore | None = None)`: `daily_briefing` gains `"tasks": {"due_today": [...], "overdue": [...], "open_count": n}` when a store is given (a store failure fills a `tasks_problem` field instead); `BRIEFING_PROMPT` adds the tasks due today and overdue to what she sums up (one more clause, still 2–4 sentences).

CLI: `run` and `chat` add `TodoTools` when `todo.enabled` (both backends) and pass the store to `BriefingTools`; `waifu todo` (= `list`), `waifu todo add <text> [--due YYYY-MM-DD]`, `waifu todo done <id>`, `waifu todo remove <id>`, `waifu todo list --done` (Indonesian output, works whether or not `todo.enabled`); `check` adds `✓ Daftar tugas: N terbuka` or `! Daftar tugas mati`. README (Indonesian): examples, the difference from schedules and reminders, privacy.

Safety: tests use tmp paths and injected clocks; nothing else is touched.

### songs on YouTube (v1.21)

User stories (one of the owner's wishes in Cyrene's memory: Spotify/YouTube songs; Spotify is not installed on the PC):

1. "Cyrene, putar lagu Hope Is the Thing With Feathers", "puterin playlist lofi buat belajar", "play Hoyo-Mix Cyrene theme" → she finds the song's YouTube page (with Claude, through her web search) and opens it in the default browser, where it plays; with Ollama or with web search off (she is never offered a link then), or when no exact link was found, she opens YouTube's search page for it instead and says so.
2. "Pause", "lagu berikutnya", "kecilin volumenya", "pindah lagu" → the existing `media` device (v1.8): Chrome and Edge answer the media keys. Such words never open a page, also without a media device; only a play verb in the same words asks for a song ("matiin musik ini, puterin lofi aja"), and never with a next or previous word ("putar lagu berikutnya").
3. With `music.service: youtube_music`, a song opens on YouTube Music instead.

Non-goals (v1.21): Spotify (not installed), downloading audio, playing through her own voice output, choosing among results by herself without a link, controlling the browser beyond opening a page, any YouTube API key.

Safety: a page opens only when Master's own words in that turn pass a word gate, tokenized as the screen tool's look words, her names left out. Words that hold a search word (`cari`, `carikan`, `cariin`, `nyari`, `nyariin`, `mencari`, `search`) pass only with both a play verb and a music word ("cari lagu lofi terus putar" is "find it and play it"; "sebelum aku play Genshin, cari kode redeem" is not). Words that hold a look-up or question word never pass, whatever else they hold (`info`, `berita`, `lirik`, `lyrics`, `harga`, `judul`, `penyanyi`, `siapa`, `apa`, `apakah`, `kapan`, `kenapa`, `mengapa`, `bagaimana`, `gimana`, `berapa`, except `apa` with `aja` or `saja`, which is "anything": "putar lagu apa aja" asks for a song; such turns send her to the web, and there `play` is often a game and `dengerin` a person), nor do words that hold a next or previous word (`next`, `berikutnya`, `selanjutnya`, `previous`, `prev`, `sebelumnya`; the media device's keys, also in "putar lagu berikutnya"). Otherwise a play verb asks for a song (`putar`, `puterin`, `putarkan`, `mainkan`, `mainin`, `setelin`, `play`, `dengerin`), and so does a music word (`lagu`, `musik`, `music`, `playlist`, `youtube`) in words that hold no player word (the other media keys and the volume: `pause`, `jeda`, `jedain`, `stop`, `berhenti`, `hentikan`, `matikan`, `matiin`, `off`, `lanjut`, `lanjutkan`, `lanjutin`, `resume`, `skip`, `lewati`, `lewatin`, `pindah`, `volume`, `kecilkan`, `kecilin`, `besarkan`, `besarin`, `gedein`, `pelankan`, `pelanin`, `keraskan`, `kerasin`, `down`, `up`, `mute`). These look-up, question, next, previous, and player words also count with `-nya` (`liriknya`, `volumenya`); play verbs and music words count only as they are, so "lagunya bagus ya" is a remark. Bare `setel` (also a device's setting) and `dengar` (also hearing a person) do not ask. A request that also asks a question ("lagu apa yang lagi hits? putar") opens nothing; Master asks again without the question. The gate matches words, not meaning: in a turn whose words do not pass, nothing a web page says can get a page opened; words that pass may still be a remark or a question without any of these words ("ada lagu yang enak nggak?"), and in such a turn, as in a real request, a page she read could sway which one YouTube page opens. Only YouTube addresses are ever opened: `https` on `www.youtube.com`, `youtube.com`, `m.youtube.com`, `music.youtube.com`, or `youtu.be`, as a video (`/watch?v=<11-character id>`, `youtu.be/<id>`), a playlist (`/playlist?list=<id>`), or the search page this code builds; everything else is refused. Opening a browser takes the focus from a fullscreen game: she only does it when asked.

Config `music` (committed with its tests before the build): `enabled` (true), `service` (`youtube` or `youtube_music`, default `youtube`).

`music.py` (no new dependency):
- `youtube_link(url, *, service) -> str | None`: the canonical link for a YouTube video or playlist URL (any of the hosts above, `www.`/`m.` variants, `youtu.be`, extra query parameters such as `t` and `si` dropped except `v` and `list`), on `www.youtube.com` or `music.youtube.com` per `service`; None for anything else (another host, `http`, a user or port in the URL, a backslash, a malformed id). Video ids match `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{11}$`, playlist ids `^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{10,64}$`.
- `search_link(query, *, service) -> str`: `https://www.youtube.com/results?search_query=<urlencoded>` or `https://music.youtube.com/search?q=<urlencoded>`; the query is whitespace-collapsed and at most 120 characters.
- `open_in_browser(link)`: Windows' own "open" (`os.startfile`), looked up on every call and never bound at import, so the guard in `tests/conftest.py` covers it too.
- `open_link(link, *, startfile=open_in_browser)`: opens a link that `youtube_link` or `search_link` made, in the default browser; an `OSError` is raised to the caller.

`brain/music_tools.py` — `MusicTools(*, service, names, links, startfile=open_in_browser)`, a `ToolProvider` with one strict tool `play_youtube {query: string, url?: string}` (`url` only with `links`, a brain that searches the web) and the `begin_turn(user_text)` hook (the gate above):
- When this turn's words do not ask for a song: an error result (Master did not ask for music in this message). At most one page per turn: a later call in a turn whose page opened opens nothing and is a success, `{"ok": true, "opened": "already", "message"}` (the local model often sends the same call twice, and its empty-reply fallback speaks any error as a failure); after a failed try it is an error result.
- With `links` and a `url` that `youtube_link` accepts: that video or playlist opens. Otherwise (no `url`, a `url` that is not a YouTube video or playlist, or any `url` without `links`): the search page for `query` opens, as a success, so the model never retries after an error.
- Result `{"ok": true, "opened": "video" | "playlist" | "search", "service"}` (no URL read aloud); an `OSError` is an Indonesian error result.
- Description (static English, per `links`): use it when Master asks to play a song or music; with `links`, first search the web for the song's YouTube page and pass its youtube.com or youtu.be link as `url`, never made up, otherwise only `query` (any other link opens the search page too); without `links`, only `query` (the search page opens and Master picks); say what you opened in one short sentence; never read a link aloud; never use it to pause, skip, go back, stop, or change the volume, even without a media device; it takes the focus from a game, so only when asked.

CLI: `run` and `chat` add `MusicTools(service=music.service, names=[assistant.name, *wake_words], links=brain.backend == "claude" and web_search.enabled)` when `music.enabled` (both backends); `waifu youtube <url or words>` opens a link (when `youtube_link` accepts it) or the search page, from the terminal; `check` adds `✓ Lagu YouTube: aktif (YouTube|YouTube Music)` or `! Lagu YouTube mati`. README (Indonesian): examples, that with Claude she finds the exact song (a web search: $0.01 plus its result tokens, usually a few cents) and with Ollama or web search off the search page opens, the media keys, and the game-focus note.

Safety: tests never open anything (`startfile` injected; conftest refuses `os.startfile`), never reach the network.

### Genshin and ZZZ data (v1.23)

User stories (the owner plays Genshin Impact and Zenless Zone Zero on the Asia server too; the gacha buddy's cookie is set; v1.16's HSR stories keep working, except that with more than one game in `gacha.games` a calendar question names its game, "Banner HSR apa sekarang?", since `game_events` without `game` is then invalid, see Game resolution below):

1. "Cyrene, Furina-ku C berapa, pakai weapon apa?", "karakter Hydro-ku siapa aja?" → her answer from the owner's own HoYoLAB Battle Chronicle: the owned Genshin characters with rarity, level, Constellation, element, weapon type, and the weapon with its level and Refinement.
2. "Ellen-ku Mindscape berapa?", "agent Ice-ku siapa aja?", "Ellen pakai W-Engine apa?" → the owned ZZZ agents with rank (S or A), level, Mindscape Cinema, element, and specialty; for an agent asked for by name also her W-Engine with its level and Refinement.
3. "Stat Furina-ku gimana?", "Drive Disc Ellen apa aja?" → the final stats, the weapon's or W-Engine's stats, the artifact or Drive Disc main stats, and the sets worn, of up to three characters matching the name; data only, no advice code.
4. "Abyss-ku lantai 12 berapa bintang?", "Imaginarium Theater-ku sampai act berapa?", "Stygian Onslaught-ku difficulty berapa?", "Shiyu Defense-ku rating apa, berakhir kapan?", "skor Deadly Assault-ku berapa?" → this season of each endgame mode: stars, the deepest floor, act, or frontier, difficulty, rating, score, clear time, and when the season ends (local time).
5. "Banner Genshin apa sekarang?", "event ZZZ apa yang mau habis?" → that game's event calendar: the current and upcoming banners with their featured 5-star or S-rank and 4-star or A-rank names, the running events, and for Genshin the Spiral Abyss and Imaginarium Theater windows, each with when it ends.

The three HSR tools become `game_characters`, `game_endgame`, and `game_events`, each with an optional `game`; the HSR results stay byte-for-byte as v1.16's (until v1.27.1 reads HSR calendar times and endgame season ends on the minute, see `on_the_minute`). The old names get no aliases (`run_tool` dispatches by the names in the specs): a persona that still names `hsr_*` costs one "tidak dikenal" round each time, so CHANGELOG and README tell the owner to update a custom persona.

Non-goals (v1.23): Genshin talent levels and ZZZ skill levels (genshin.py gives `CharacterSkill.skill_type` no documented meaning and does not say whether a level includes the Constellation or Mindscape +3: later, after one real read, as v1.16 did for traces); artifact and Drive Disc substats and rolls, and HSR relic substats and set names (still deferred from v1.16); ZZZ Annihilation Simulacrum, Threshold Simulation, and Lost Void (where they stand in the game is unverified; Hollow Zero's bounty is already in `game_status`); Bangboos, the `get_genshin_user`/`get_zzz_user` index summaries, Envisaged Echoes, TCG, and old activities; ZZZ gacha info (currencies and pity: close to spending, and pity already comes from her own history) and the ZZZ upgrade-guide API (it needs a badge login with `cookie_token`/`stoken`, returns build advice, and holds the HoYoLAB ids); a W-Engine column in the ZZZ roster and questions such as "who uses an S-rank W-Engine" (one request per agent); a `game_*` call without `game` that covers every configured game at once, and searching a name across games (up to 9 endgame and 4 calendar requests in one call, 10-15 kB results; the model can call once per game); previous seasons, endgame teams, Imaginarium Theater act detail (`need_detail=True` needs genshin.py's character-name list), Stygian Onslaught multiplayer, and the rank percentages of Shiyu Defense and Deadly Assault; a `--name` detail view in the CLI (the manual checklist checks detail answers through `waifu chat -v`); saying how many stars are missing anywhere but a Spiral Abyss floor (genshin.py states only `Floor.max_stars` 9 and `Chamber.max_stars` 3); tolerant per-item parsing (one unknown enum value fails a whole roster or calendar, which is `other`); reminders, briefing mentions, and aliases for the `hsr_*` names. v1.16's other non-goals hold for every game (build or team advice in code, changing any HoYoLAB setting).

Privacy: v1.16's rules (HSR data → Privacy) hold for every game. The data is read on request only, kept in memory for minutes, never written to disk or logged; log lines name only the game's short name (HSR, Genshin, ZZZ) and the error type or retcode, and `-v` logs tool results at DEBUG as for every tool. Tool results and the CLI never hold the uid, a nickname, an avatar or icon, ids, wiki links, or descriptions: a character's `ref` (the game's id, needed to ask for its detail) lives only in the dataclasses. The readers never read ZZZ Shiyu Defense V2's `player_nickname`/`player_avatar` (genshin.py copies `nick_name` and `icon` into that model) or Deadly Assault's `nickname`/`player_avatar`. Never `update_settings`, `autoauth`, `raw`, `previous`, `return_raw_data`, or a language other than en-us.
