Production `now` is naive (`datetime.now`) while the journal's expiry is aware WIB, so both are compared as aware times, a naive one read as the PC's local time: the two never raise `TypeError`.
- `prompt_timer.end_of_day(moment) -> datetime`: the midnight that ends `moment`'s day, in its own time zone (naive stays naive, WIB stays WIB). `PromptTimer(..., expires: Callable[[datetime], datetime], request: Callable[[str, datetime], None])` calls `request(prompt, expires(now))` at the moment of asking and logs "Asking for the <what> of <day> (said until <YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM>)".
- Each timer's expiry: `BriefingTimer` at `briefing.until` on its day, the end of the window it is asked in; `WeeklyReportTimer` at `end_of_day`, midnight after the report day; `JournalTimer` at `end_of_day` in WIB, the end of its own day. Not earlier for the journal: an answer to a question asked just before midnight goes on the question's day (below).
- CLI: the three requests become `lambda prompt, expires_at: assistant.ask_aloud(prompt, ..., expires_at=expires_at)`.

The answer's day:
- `JournalTools(store, *, now=wib_now, answer_window=timedelta(minutes=10))`: her question's turn (`begin_turn(JOURNAL_PROMPT)`), once it has read the journal successfully, remembers the question's WIB day and the moment the turn began (a second question replaces them); a question turn without a successful `journal_read` (the brain failed and apologised) is not remembered, as `ask_aloud` with `required_tool` says nothing then, so a note dictated just after a failed question goes on the new day; a later turn that begins within `answer_window` of the question keeps them, one that begins later forgets them. While they are kept, a `journal_write` without `date` (or with null) writes on the question's day: an answer just after midnight, and an answer repeated after a turn rolled back by an API error, too. An explicit `date` is used as given. When the question's day is not today, INFO, once it is written, "An undated journal note went on <YYYY-MM-DD>, the day of her nightly question" (without the note). The question's own turn still refuses to write. `answer_window` is no tool parameter: the budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters.
- `journal_write`'s description keeps the date rule of v1.31 first and names the answer in it: after midnight about the day that just ended, also in an answer to her nightly journal question asked before midnight, `date` is that day. It never tells her to leave `date` out for an answer: the window is only the safety net for one she writes without it, and an answer after the window, dated by her as the rule asks, still goes on the day she asked about.
- CLI: `answer_window` is `assistant.session_idle_minutes`, as long as the question's session lasts without a word.

Known limits: a dropped request is not asked again that day (the day was saved before the request); the greeting expires at `until` however late in the window it was asked, so a greeting asked at 11:59 and held for two minutes is skipped; the answer window counts from her question, not from Master's last words, so a note told without a date more than `session_idle_minutes` after the question goes on today even while the talk goes on (the description asks her to date an answer after midnight, so this needs her to leave the date out against it).

Docs: README (Indonesian) adds a sentence to "Sapaan pagi dan ringkasan harian" (a greeting held past `until` is skipped), "Laporan game mingguan" (not after midnight), and "Jurnal harian" (a question held until midnight is skipped; an answer just after midnight goes on the question's day); CHANGELOG v1.31.1.

Safety: unchanged; every clock in the tests is a fake.

### mode tidur (v1.32)

The owner sleeps with the AC on and wants it off a few hours later. Until now every step of a routine ran at once, so "mode tidur" could turn the lamp off and set the AC to 26 but not turn the AC off at 01:00: that needed a second request ("matikan AC jam 1"). A routine step may now wait.

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, mode tidur." at 22:00 runs the routine `tidur` the owner wrote: the lamp goes off and the AC to 26 at once, and its step `{device: ac, command: off, after_minutes: 180}` goes into the schedule as a one-shot device entry for 01:00. She confirms in one short reply that mentions it ("Lampu sudah mati, AC di dua puluh enam derajat, dan AC akan mati jam satu nanti. おやすみなさい, Master.").
2. "Cyrene, batalkan AC mati nanti." → she cancels that entry with `cancel_schedule` (its id is in the routine's result, and `list_schedules` shows it as coming from the routine). `waifu schedule list` shows it and `waifu schedule cancel j12` cancels it too.
3. At 01:00 the AC gets `off` without a word, like any timed device command (only a failure is spoken and pushed). A restart of `waifu run` before then keeps it: it lives in `data/schedule.json`.
4. "Mode tidur" said again at 23:00 moves the AC off to 02:00: a run cancels the delayed steps its routine left in the schedule that have not fired yet, so the AC is never turned off twice or earlier than the last request.
5. `waifu routine list` and `waifu check` show `tidur: Mode tidur (lampu_kamar off, ac temp 26, ac off setelah 3 jam)`; `waifu routine run tidur` runs the steps of now and schedules the delayed one (with `--dry-run` in a temporary schedule).

Non-goals (v1.32): delays written as text ("3h", "90m": every duration in the config is a whole number with its unit in the key, like `late_grace_minutes`); a delay counted from the step before; a delay said by voice (the `schedule` tool already turns "matikan AC 3 jam lagi" into a clock time); conditions ("if it is still hot"); keeping the PC awake until a delayed step, or putting it to sleep or off from a routine (voice only, as before); delayed steps that repeat on their own (each run schedules them once); a new tool, tool parameter, config section, or dependency.

Config:
- `RoutineStep.after_minutes: int | None` (default None: the step runs at once): how many minutes after the routine starts the step runs, a whole number from 1 to 1440 (24 hours). A boolean, text ("3h", or "180" in quotes), a fraction, 0, or more than 1440 is a ConfigError naming the step: "after_minutes must be a whole number of minutes from 1 to 1440 (24 hours), like 180 for 3 hours" (a YAML `true` or `"90"` is never read as a number).
- Besides the v1.7 rules, which already refuse a delayed step on an unknown device, the PC (`type: pc`), or a plug marked `powers_pc`, the config refuses at load: a delayed step while `schedule.enabled` is false ("routine 'tidur' step 3 has after_minutes, but delayed steps run from the schedule: set schedule.enabled: true or remove after_minutes"), and a delayed `play_pause` or `mute` on a `type: media` device ("routine 'tidur' step 3 may not delay 'mute' on 'musik': it toggles, so at night it could start the music or unmute the PC; delay stop or volume instead"), the rule `schedule` already applies. `config.MEDIA_TOGGLES` holds the two commands, and `ScheduleTools` uses it too.

`schedule.py`:
- `DeviceAction(device, command, value=None, routine: str | None = None)`: `routine` names the routine whose delayed step it is; None for a command scheduled by voice or by hand. In the file the device action gains the key `routine` only when it is set (`{"type": "device", "device", "command", "value", "routine"}`), so a file without delayed steps is written exactly as before and `FILE_VERSION` stays 1. Reading accepts the device action with or without `routine`, which must be a string when present; `add` refuses a `routine` that is empty or not text (ScheduleError).
- A Cyrene older than v1.32 that reads a file holding a routine's pending delayed step sets the file aside as corrupt (the CHANGELOG says so).

`routines.py`:
- `run_routine(registry, routine, *, name, schedule: ScheduleStore | None = None, now=datetime.now) -> list[DeviceResult]`, still one result per step, in order. `now` is read once, as the routine starts. A step without `after_minutes` runs at once, as before. A delayed step never runs then: when the `waifu check` rule names a problem (an unknown device, the PC or a `powers_pc` plug, a command or value the device lacks), the problem is its result (`ok=False`) and nothing is scheduled; otherwise it is added to `schedule` as a one-shot `DeviceAction(device, command, value, routine=name)` at the routine's start plus `after_minutes`, cut to the minute, and its result is `DeviceResult(ok=True, "Dijadwalkan jam 01.00 dini hari nanti.", state={"id": "j12", "at": "2026-10-18 01:00"})` for a start at 22:00. The message is what the model copies into her reply, so it says the time as she would say it at the start and never holds the id (read aloud as "j dua belas"; `state` has it): `jam 16.00 nanti` later the same day; a time from 00:00 to 04:59 adds `dini hari` and, after a start from 05:00 on, stays `... nanti` on the next date too, since "besok jam satu" said at 22:00 sounds like 13:00 tomorrow; any other time on the next date is `besok jam 08.00` (from a start in the small hours, `besok jam 03.00 dini hari`). Two steps with the same delay land on the same minute. A ScheduleError (a full schedule: "Jadwal sudah penuh, 50 entri. Batalkan jadwal lama dulu.") makes that step `ok=False` with its message, and so does a schedule file that cannot be opened ("File jadwal tidak bisa dibuka, jadi langkah ini tidak dijadwalkan.", logged); the steps of now have run all the same. Without a `schedule` a delayed step fails ("Langkah yang ditunda butuh jadwal, tapi jadwal mati (schedule.enabled: false)."); the config refuses that combination, so this only guards a caller that passes none.
- Before the steps, when the routine has a delayed step and a schedule is given, every one-shot device entry whose `routine` is `name` is cancelled (logged at INFO with the ids): the latest run wins. Entries Master scheduled by voice or by hand and other routines' are never touched. A routine without delayed steps never reads or creates the schedule file.
- `describe_step(step) -> str` (public, was `_describe_step`): `ac temp 26`, `ac off setelah 3 jam` (`setelah 1 jam 30 menit`, `setelah 45 menit`), in `describe_routine`, `routine_problems`, the scheduler's failure text, and `waifu routine run`.

`scheduler.py`: a scheduled routine runs with the store, its name, and the scheduler's `now`, so its delayed steps count from the moment it fired. Scheduling them is silent; a delayed step that could not be scheduled is a failed step in the usual failure text (`Langkah 3 (ac off setelah 3 jam): Jadwal sudah penuh, ...`). When a delayed step fires, it is a device entry like any other: refused again when `registry.powers_pc` names its device, and one missed by more than `late_grace_minutes` (the PC off or asleep, `waifu run` closed) is removed with a WARNING log only. Its failure text names the routine: `Master, jadwal ac off dari rutinitas tidur jam 01.00 gagal: ...`.

`brain/schedule_tools.py`: `ScheduleTools(..., now=datetime.now)` (no tool parameter; tests pass the store's clock) runs a routine with the store, its name, and `now`. A delayed step in the result: `{"device", "command", ["value",] "after_minutes": 180, "ok": true, "message": "Dijadwalkan jam 01.00 dini hari nanti.", "scheduled": {"id": "j12", "at": "2026-10-18 01:00"}}` (`scheduled` only when it was). `run_routine`'s static description adds: a step listed with "setelah ..." is not run now but scheduled once for that long after the routine starts; mention it briefly with its clock time ("AC akan mati jam satu nanti"); Master can cancel it with `cancel_schedule` and the id in its result; running the routine again replaces its delayed steps that have not run. `list_schedules` describes a delayed step as `perangkat: ac off (rutinitas tidur)`. The budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters.

CLI:
- `waifu routine list` and the routine check line use `describe_routine`, delays included. `waifu routine run <nama>` runs with the schedule of the session (`_session_schedule`: the real one, or with `--dry-run` a temporary one deleted at the end; None with the schedule off, which a routine with a delayed step cannot have) and prints a delayed step as `✓ Langkah 3 (ac off setelah 3 jam): Dijadwalkan besok jam 01.00 (j12).`, built from the result's `state` and the start it passed as `now` (`besok` on the date after the start, the id for `waifu schedule cancel`): written, a 24-hour time cannot be misheard; when a delayed step was scheduled a last line says who runs it: `DELAYED_STEPS_IN_RUN` ("Langkah yang ditunda dijalankan pada jamnya oleh Cyrene yang sedang berjalan ('waifu run'): biarkan PC dan 'waifu run' menyala sampai saat itu. Batalkan dengan 'waifu schedule cancel <id>'."), or in a dry run `DRY_RUN_DELAYED_STEPS` ("Mode simulasi: langkah yang ditunda hanya dicoba di jadwal sementara yang hilang saat ditutup. Jadwal asli (data\schedule.json) tidak disentuh."). `ROUTINE_DESCRIPTION` (the help) names `after_minutes`.
- `waifu schedule list` and `cancel` show a delayed step as `perangkat ac off (rutinitas tidur)`.
- `config.example.yaml`: the commented `tidur` routine gains `{device: ac, command: off, after_minutes: 180}`, and its comment explains `after_minutes`.

Known limits: a delayed step runs only while `waifu run` runs and the PC is awake: "mode tidur" does not keep the PC awake (Windows sleep set to "Never", or longer than the delay), and one missed by more than `late_grace_minutes` is only logged. A device removed or renamed in config.yaml before its delayed step fires fails then, spoken and pushed like any failed timed command. A toggling IR command (a projector's `power`) cannot be told from an `off` at load, so the README says never to delay one. Each run adds as many entries as the routine has delayed steps toward `schedule.max_entries` (the run's own earlier ones are cancelled first).

Docs: README (Indonesian) subsection "Mode tidur" in "Jadwal, pengingat, dan rutinitas", after "Menulis rutinitas di config.yaml": a full routine (lampu off, AC 26, AC off 3 jam lagi), the conversation, cancelling, running it again, what `waifu routine run` and `waifu schedule list` print, that the PC and `waifu run` must stay on until the delayed step, and what may not be delayed; the routine bullets name `after_minutes`; CHANGELOG v1.32.

Safety: tests never touch `data/schedule.json` (every store under tmp_path, or a dry run's temporary folder), never run real devices, and never sleep (injected clocks).

### pendapatan gacha (v1.33)

The owner plans pulls around what a month brings in. HoYoLAB keeps a monthly diary of each game: Genshin's Traveler's Diary, HSR's Trailblaze Monthly Calendar, and ZZZ's Inter-Knot monthly report. Cyrene reads them, read-only, through genshin.py 1.7.31.

User stories (the gacha buddy's cookie is set):

1. "Cyrene, bulan ini aku dapat berapa Primogem?" → "Bulan ini Master sudah dapat sekitar lima ribu empat ratus Primogem, kira-kira tiga puluh tiga pull, paling banyak dari event." Information for planning pulls, never a push to pull, spend, or top up; only when asked, never in a greeting, the morning briefing, or anything she starts herself.
2. "Stellar Jade bulan lalu berapa?" → last month's figure; "pendapatan gacha bulan ini?" → every configured game in one answer.
3. Her weekly game report (v1.27) also tells this month's income so far per game, in one short part.
4. `waifu gacha income [game] [--month YYYY-MM]` prints the same without the brain, also with `gacha.enabled: false`.

Non-goals (v1.33): what was spent, top-ups, Genesis Crystals, Oneiric Shards, or Monochrome (the diaries do not count them); a pull plan or advice; months HoYoLAB no longer keeps (it gives this month and the two before); the per-action logs (`genshin_diary_log`, `starrail_diary_log`, `get_zzz_diary_detail`); a push or reminder of its own; keeping the figures on disk; a `waifu check` line (the cookie line already says HoYoLAB needs the cookie); a new config key, strict tool, or dependency.

What each diary gives (genshin.py 1.7.31's models):

| Game | `earned` | `others` | `last_month`, `change_percent` | `today`, `yesterday` | `sources` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Genshin | Primogem (`month_data.current_primogems`) | Mora | `last_primogems`, `primogem_rate` | today's `day_data.current_primogems` | the Primogem categories (`group_by`), with HoYoLAB's English names |
| HSR | Stellar Jade (`current_hcoin`) | Star Rail Pass (`current_rails_pass`; HoYoLAB does not say whether Special or regular) | `last_hcoin`, `hcoin_rate` | `day_data.current_hcoin`, yesterday `day_data.last_hcoin` | the Stellar Jade categories, with HoYoLAB's names |
| ZZZ | Polychrome (`PolychromesData`) | Master Tape and Boopon entries, each with HoYoLAB's `data_name` | none | none | `income_components`, named in code |

Months:
- This month is the month of `checkin_day(now, gacha.server_utc_offset)` (midnight server time, HoYoLAB's day). `income_months(now, offset)` gives this month and the two before, newest first, each as its 1st: HoYoLAB's `optional_month` usually lists those three (genshin.py drops that field for Genshin and HSR; what HoYoLAB answers outside them is unknown), so any other month is refused in code before HoYoLAB is asked.
- The month is always passed: genshin.py's default would use the PC's clock. Requests: Genshin `get_genshin_diary(uid, month=<1-12>)`, HSR `get_starrail_diary(uid, month="YYYYMM")`, ZZZ `get_zzz_diary(uid, month="")` for this month (genshin.py's current month) and `month="YYYYMM"` for an earlier one (assumed: genshin.py does not state ZZZ's format, so the answer is checked). Never the deprecated `get_diary`.
- An answer for another month than the one asked (Genshin's `month` 1-12, HSR's `month` YYYYMM, the digits of ZZZ's `data_month`) is refused as GachaError other `HoYoLAB menjawab bulan lain untuk pendapatan <short>, jadi angkanya tidak disebut; coba lagi nanti.`, so a month is never told under another's name. Only a month inside `income_months` is ever asked, so the message never blames the window.

`gacha/income.py` (no genshin import; nothing holds the uid, nickname, avatar, or region):
- `IncomeCategory(name, amount, percent)`, `IncomeCurrency(name, amount)`, and `GameIncome(game, month: date, earned: int, others: tuple[IncomeCurrency, ...], last_month: int | None, change_percent: int | None, sources: tuple[IncomeCategory, ...], today: int | None, yesterday: int | None)` (frozen), with `pulls = earned // PULL_COST` (160): passes and tapes are listed under `others`, never added to `pulls`.
- `read_genshin_income(model, month, *, this_month)`, `read_hsr_income(...)`, `read_zzz_income(...)`: `month` is the one asked, never the model's (Genshin says 10, HSR 202610); sources most first, without the empty ones; `today` and `yesterday` only with `this_month`. ZZZ's sources are named in code: `daily_activity_rewards` Daily Activity, `growth_rewards` Growth, `shiyu_rewards` Shiyu Defense, `mail_rewards` Mail, `hollow_rewards` Hollow Zero, `event_rewards` Events, `other_rewards` Other. A ZZZ answer without a Polychrome entry cannot be read (ValueError, then other). genshin.py's ZZZ model refuses an action or currency type it does not know: the ZZZ read is then other ("Data HoYoLAB ZZZ tidak bisa dibaca saat ini."), the other games unaffected.
- `answers_month(game_id, model, month) -> bool`, `income_months(now, offset) -> tuple[date, ...]`, `month_text(month) -> "YYYY-MM"`, `parse_month(text) -> date` (exactly `YYYY-MM`, else ValueError), and `IncomeSource`, the protocol `income(games, month, *, this_month) -> dict[str, GameIncome | GachaError]`.

`HoyolabClient.income(games, month, *, this_month) -> dict[str, GameIncome | GachaError]`: one `asyncio.run` with one client for every game asked, as `status` (the uids resolved once, each request with its game's explicit uid), each request through `_chronicle` (a hidden diary is chronicle_private, never Real-Time Notes' notes_off), the whole bounded by the client's timeout (20 s). A failing game does not hide the others; a failure of the whole call (no cookie, the timeout) is every game's error, without a cookie before HoYoLAB is asked. Never `update_settings`.

Tool `gacha_income` (`gacha/income_tools.py`: `IncomeTools(source, games, *, server_utc_offset=8, now=<local now>)`, a provider of its own, so the three game data tools stay as they are): non-strict, `{game?: enum[the configured games], month?: string}`, `additionalProperties: false`, every argument checked in code, a null argument counting as left out (local models send null for the optional arguments they do not use). `game` left out is every configured game, one failing game under `errors` while the others are still told; `month` left out is this month; a `month` that is not `YYYY-MM`, or not one of `income_months`, is invalid (`'month' harus YYYY-MM, salah satu dari: 2026-10, 2026-09, 2026-08.`) and nothing is asked; an unknown key is invalid. Reads are kept per process for 15 minutes under `income:<game>:<YYYY-MM>` (this month's key adds the server day, so `today` never outlives midnight); the stale games are asked in one call. Result: `{"as_of", "month": "YYYY-MM", "this_month": bool, "games": [{"game", "name", "currency", "earned", "pulls", "today"?, "yesterday"?, "last_month"?, "change_percent"?, "sources": [{"name", "amount", "percent"}] (at most 8), "others": [{"name", "amount"}]}], "errors"?: [{"game", "message"}]}`; every game failing is an error result with each distinct message once. The static English description, built once from the configured games: what each game's diary gives; `pulls` counts only the premium currency, at 160 a pull; `last_month` is the month before as HoYoLAB counts it (a month still running is mostly below it); HoYoLAB's diary may lag behind the game; information for planning pulls: never suggest pulling, spending money, or topping up, and never judge the amount; only when Master asks what they earned, never in her own greeting, the morning briefing, or anything she starts herself; one or two short sentences with the numbers rounded, no lists or parentheses; never saved with `remember` unless Master asks. `run` never raises.

Persona: a line under "Game HoYoverse Master" (in `persona.md` and `persona_hsr.md`, whose rules stay word for word the same) names `gacha_income` for the Primogem, Stellar Jade, or Polychrome Master earned this month or an earlier month, with the game in its `game` argument, only when Master asks (not for a greeting or the morning briefing), as information for planning pulls, never an invitation to pull, spend, or top up.

Weekly report (`gacha/weekly.py`):
- `gather_weekly_report(..., income: IncomeSource | None = None)`: with a source, one more lane (`weekly-income`) makes one `income(games, this month, this_month=True)` call within the same 30 s budget (late: "waktunya habis"); `GameWeek.income: GameIncome | None` and `income_problem` (`Pendapatan <short> bulan ini tidak terbaca: <why>`), both None without a source; `problems` holds it; `readable` ignores it, as it does play time.
- `report_json`: per game `"income_this_month": {"month", "currency", "earned", "pulls", "today"?} | null` and `"income_problem"`, only with a source. `WeeklyReportTools(..., income=None)`'s description then names this month's premium currency earned so far and its worth in pulls, and its information-only rule covers that income too ("only as information for planning: never suggest pulling, spending money, or topping up, and never judge the amount earned"), since asking how the week went has no `WEEKLY_REPORT_PROMPT` around it; `WEEKLY_REPORT_PROMPT` names "pendapatan bulan ini" once in its list (still no look word, play verb, or music word). Its facts now hold what was earned, never what was spent.
- `waifu gacha report`: `Pendapatan bulan ini: 5.400 Primogem (33 pull), hari ini 120`, or `✗ <problem>`.

CLI:
- `run` and `chat` add `IncomeTools` right after `GameDataTools` when `gacha.enabled` (both backends); `_weekly_report_tools` passes `income=gacha.hoyolab`.
- `waifu gacha income [game] [--month YYYY-MM]` (every configured game and this month by default; also with `gacha.enabled: false`): a header `Pendapatan gacha Oktober 2026, sampai sekarang:` (an earlier month: `Pendapatan gacha September 2026:`), then per game `HSR (Honkai: Star Rail)` with `Stellar Jade: 4.800 (30 pull), hari ini 60, kemarin 120` (today and yesterday only for this month), `Bulan sebelumnya: 10.000; bulan ini -52% dibanding itu` when given (`+12%`, `0%`; an earlier month names itself, `September -52% dibanding itu`, since HoYoLAB's change is the month shown against the one before; without a change only `Bulan sebelumnya: 10.000`), `Sumber: Daily Activity 1.800 (38%), ...` when given, `Lainnya: Star Rail Pass 5` when given, or `✗ <message>`; then `INCOME_NOTE` ("Catatan: pull dihitung 160 per pull dari mata uang premium saja, tanpa pass atau tape; catatan bulanan HoYoLAB bisa tertinggal dari game."). Numbers have a dot per thousand. Exit 1 when a game could not be read; a month outside the window prints `✗ Pendapatan hanya ada untuk bulan ini dan dua bulan sebelumnya: 2026-10, 2026-09, 2026-08.` and exits 2 without asking HoYoLAB. `GACHA_DESCRIPTION` names it.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Pendapatan gacha bulanan" in "Teman main gacha" after "Laporan game mingguan" (examples, what each game gives, the months, the command, privacy), a command row, a line in "Laporan game mingguan", checklist items; CHANGELOG v1.33.

Privacy: the figures are read only when asked (or for the weekly report), kept in memory for 15 minutes, never written to disk or the INFO log; with the Claude backend they go to Anthropic as a tool result, and the weekly report's spoken sentence, which may name them, goes to ntfy as that report always does. No result or log holds the uid, nickname, avatar, or region.

Safety: tests fake genshin.py's client with real genshin.py models (`Diary`, `StarRailDiary`, `ZZZDiary`) built from deep-copied sample dicts, assert that `update_settings` is never called and that the cookie, uid, and nickname are in no result or log, and never reach HoYoLAB (`tests/conftest.py`). The strict budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters.

### mode game (v1.34)

The owner plays HSR, Genshin, and ZZZ on this PC every evening, often in voice chat, and her notices ("Master, resin penuh", the rain alert) talk over the game. The play time watcher (v1.29) already knows when a tracked game runs; game mode uses it to keep her quiet while he plays, the way a focus block (v1.30) does, and to run a routine he wrote when he starts and stops playing.

User stories:

1. With `game_mode.announcements: hold`, while a tracked game is open, what she would say by herself (the rain alert, the version notice, the gacha, redeem code, PC, and free offers notices, the budget notice) waits until the last game has closed and then comes out briefly, merged into one announcement after a short lead-in ("Tadi selama main ada kabar."), a notice held twice said once. The turns she starts herself (the morning greeting, the weekly game report, the nightly journal question) wait too, and one held past its expiry (v1.31.1) is dropped. The reminders and schedules Master set still speak on time, the bedtime nudge too, and Master can still talk to her (wake word, double-click, the phone). The avatar's welcome-back greeting is skipped.
2. With `phone`, while a game is open a notice that already goes to the phone (notifications on; for the rain alert and the version notice also their `push`) is not spoken at all; one that does not is held as with `hold`, and so are her own turns (never pushed instead).
3. With `on_start: redup` (a routine Master wrote: the lamp dimmed), the routine runs once, without a word, when the first game opens; with `on_stop: terang`, when the last game has closed. Alt-tab, a launcher restart, or a game restarted within a poll never runs them again, a restart of `waifu run` mid-game does not run `on_start` again, and stopping `waifu run` never runs `on_stop`.
4. `waifu check` says which game mode is on.

Non-goals (v1.34): games other than the tracked ones (`play_time.games`); telling a game in the foreground or full screen from one open behind other windows (open counts as playing, minimized or idle too, as for play time); pushing a notice that is not pushed already (phone mode never pushes anything new: a `push: false` is respected, and her own turns are never pushed instead of spoken); saying anything when a game starts or stops; holding Master's own reminders and schedules; muting music, Windows' notifications, or the avatar; routine steps on the PC or a `powers_pc` plug (refused at load and at run time, as always); running `on_stop` for a game closed while `waifu run` was off; game mode in `chat`; a dropped notice told again later; expiring held notices other than the rain alert, the hot-GPU notice, and the gacha reminders about a game's state; telling which game a held notice is about from the games played; a new tool, tool parameter, or dependency.

Config `game_mode`: `announcements` (`normal` (default: today's behaviour), `hold`, or `phone`), `on_start` (none: no routine), `on_stop` (none). A routine name that is not in `routines` is a ConfigError (`game_mode.on_start names the routine 'redup', which is not in routines`); a game mode other than the default (another `announcements`, or a routine) with `play_time.enabled: false` is a ConfigError (`game_mode needs play_time.enabled: true: it knows a game is running from the play time watcher`). A routine with a PC step is refused by the v1.7 rules already.

When a game is running (`play_watch.py`):
- `PlayTimeWatcher(..., on_poll: Callable[[bool, bool], None] | None = None)`: after each poll that worked, once its lock is released, `on_poll(playing, resumed)`: `playing` whether a tracked game has an open session (one missed at a single poll still has it: the two-poll rule, so alt-tab, which leaves the process list as it is, and a game or launcher restart within a poll change nothing; `HYP.exe` never counts), `resumed` True only at the run's first poll that worked when a running game went on with a session from before the restart (`resume_games`: last seen at most `RESUME_GAP`, 5 minutes, before; a first poll that fails after following the games, its write or the bedtime nudge's, keeps that for the next poll that works, so `on_start` never runs again for it). A sleep of the PC starts fresh sessions for the games still open, so `playing` stays True. Never for a failed poll, never from `stop()`, never after it; a failing `on_poll` is logged with its traceback and the watcher goes on. The bedtime nudge is as before.

`game_mode.py` — `GameMode(config, *, registry, routines, schedule=None, poll: timedelta, now=datetime.now)`, one per `run`, shared by the play time watcher's thread (which tells it) and the voice loop (which asks it at every frame):
- `observe(playing, resumed)`: remembers the observation and, once its routine (if any) has run, its time on `now`, replaced whole (read without a lock); while the routine runs the observation is never stale, so lamps out of reach that make a routine outlast three polls never let the hold lapse (a routine that raises still records the time, so a failing listing can end the hold after it). A change from not playing to playing runs `on_start`, from playing to not playing `on_stop`; the first observation of the run runs `on_start` only when a game is playing and not `resumed` (a restart mid-game finds the room set already), never `on_stop`. So each start and stop runs its routine at most once. A routine runs on the watcher's thread through `routines.run_routine` with the run's registry, schedule (its delayed steps go into the schedule), its name, and `now`; nothing is said or pushed; a failed step is logged at WARNING with its message, an exception with its traceback, and the watcher goes on. Starts and stops are logged at DEBUG only (play times are Master's own, as the watcher's sessions).
- `playing()`: the last observation saw a game and is at most `SLEEP_POLLS × poll` (3 polls) old, counted from the end of its routine, `playing_now`'s rule: a process listing that keeps failing ends the hold instead of holding forever, and the next good poll brings it back.
- `holding()`: `announcements` is `hold` or `phone`, and `playing()`. `diverting()`: `announcements` is `phone`, and `playing()`.

Holding, through the v1.30 quiet (no parallel mechanism):
- The Assistant's and the avatar's `quiet` become `focus.in_block() or game_mode.holding()` (only `holding` without the focus timer; exactly `focus.in_block`, or none, with `announcements: normal`, so focus behaves as before). While it holds, `run` says only what `remind` queued; plain and patient announcements and `ask_aloud` requests wait in order and come out once neither holds, merged into one announcement with the reminders queued then (patient ones only with the follow-up window closed), the requests one at a time after them, each request still dropped past its expiry (v1.31.1).
- The Assistant also takes `game_holding` (game mode's `holding`; None without a game mode). Each quiet frame in which a game holds takes the queued announcements out of their queues, in order, and keeps them aside marked `held_by_game` (v1.34.1: per announcement, so one queued after the game has closed while a focus block goes on stays unmarked); the announcement that releases them says `GAME_HELD` ("Tadi selama main ada kabar.") after the reminders queued with it, then the ones a game held, then the rest; not when every one a game held expired, and never for a focus block alone (its end line frames it). In every announcement a text queued more than once is said once.
- The automatic notices are queued through one announcer in `cli.py`: with `phone` while a game runs (`diverting`), a notice that also went to the phone is not queued, logged at INFO as "Not saying a notice while a game runs: it went to the phone" (without the text). Pushed are the gacha, redeem code, PC, and free offers notices and the budget notice whenever notifications are on, the rain alert and the version notice only with their `push` as well. Since every watcher pushes on its own, phone mode needs no push of its own and never pushes twice.
- The bedtime nudge is due only while a game is open, so holding it until the game closes would make it pointless: with `announcements` other than `normal` it is queued with `remind`, like Master's reminders (he set the bedtime himself): said while the game runs, also in an open follow-up window and during a focus block. Still never pushed. With `normal`, a patient announcement as before.

Staleness of held items:
- `app.py`: a queued announcement is `_Announcement(text, emotion, expires_at, game_expires_at, held_by_game=False)`; `announce(text, emotion=NEUTRAL, *, wait_for_followup=False, expires_at=None, game_expires_at=None)`. When she would say it (`_say_announcements`), one whose `expires_at` has passed on the Assistant's `now` is dropped, logged at INFO as "Not saying an announcement held past <expires_at>: it is out of date" (without the text); so is one whose `game_expires_at` has passed when a game held it (marked `held_by_game`: a frame saw it queued while a game held), logged as "Not saying an announcement a game held past <game_expires_at>: it is out of date"; the rest are said as before (nothing said when every one expired). Without a game hold (`announcements: normal`, no game mode, or a focus block with no game open) `game_expires_at` is never counted, so a focus block behaves exactly as in v1.30 (v1.34.1: v1.34 dropped the gacha reminders and the hot-GPU notice after a focus block too). A game opened during a focus block counts as holding what is queued then; what is queued after it closed is held by the block alone and keeps its game expiry uncounted. Compared with v1.31.1's `_expired` (a naive time read as the PC's local time). `remind` never expires. The protocol `Announce` (`rain_watch.py`) takes both, keyword-only, default None; `cli._announcer` passes both on.
- `rain_watch.py`: the alert is announced with `expires_at` = the start of its rain (`RainWindow.start`, the forecast's local time, read as the PC's local time: the place is the owner's own, in WIB); its `announce` takes `expires_at` (protocol `Announce`). A rain alert held through a game, a focus block, or a long talk past the start of its rain is dropped: this also ends the v1.30 known limit. The push is sent at once, as before.
- `gacha/watcher.py`: the reminders about a game's state (stamina, daily, weekly, expeditions) are announced with `game_expires_at` = the poll's time + `poll_minutes`, the next poll: held longer by a game they tell a state the game just played has likely changed (resin spent, dailies done), so they are dropped. Without notifications that also drops one about a game not played; it is not told again. A focus block alone keeps them until it ends. The problems and the check-in never expire.
- Every other notice is kept until it is said, in memory only (see the known limits for a stop of `waifu run`): they are told once (version, budget, offers, the redeem codes, a nearly full drive, the gacha problems and check-in), and without notifications they would be lost. A game left open all night holds them all night, and they come out merged when it closes.
- `pc_watch.py`: the hot-GPU notice is announced with `game_expires_at` an hour after it was told, when the next could be told: of a long hot game at most the last notice, if still under an hour old, is said after it. A focus block alone keeps it until it ends.

CLI:
- `run` with play time on and a game mode other than the default builds one `GameMode` inside the schedule block (the run's registry, schedule, routines, `poll` = `play_time.poll_seconds`, the PC's local clock as the scheduler's) before the Assistant, gives `game_mode.observe` to the play time watcher as `on_poll`, and passes the quiet above. With the default, no `GameMode`, and everything is as in v1.33. `chat` has none.
- `check`, right after the focus line: `✓ Mode game: pengumuman ditahan selama main` (`hold`), `✓ Mode game: pengumuman ke HP selama main` (`phone` with notifications on), `! Mode game: ke HP, tapi notify mati: pengumuman ditahan sampai game ditutup`, `✓ Mode game: pengumuman tetap diucapkan saat main` (`normal` with a routine), each with `; rutinitas redup saat game dibuka, terang saat game ditutup` for the routines set, or `! Mode game mati` for the default.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Mode game" after "Teman fokus" (the three modes, what waits and what still speaks, the routines, debounce and restart, the check row, privacy) with a contents entry, checklist items, and a Privasi bullet; "Teman fokus" no longer says a held rain alert may come after the rain began; `config.example.yaml` documents `game_mode`; CHANGELOG v1.34.

Known limits: the hold starts at the first poll that sees a game (up to `poll_seconds`, 60 s, after it opens), and the hold, `on_stop`, and the notices end at the second poll that misses it (1-2 polls after it closes); a game seen at one poll only (a crash at launch) runs `on_start` and then `on_stop`, so the room is left as `on_stop` sets it, not restored (nothing saves the state from before playing: a `brightness` step switches a lamp that was off on, so `terang` leaves it lit); a game closed while `waifu run` was off never runs `on_stop` (the lamp stays dimmed); a game minimized all evening holds her notices all evening; after the PC wakes with a game open, held notices may come out before the first poll after the wake; with `phone`, a push that fails loses the spoken copy too (each watcher pushes on its own); held announcements live only in the Assistant's memory while their watchers have already recorded them as told, so any still held when `waifu run` stops (also a shutdown in the 1-2 polls after the game closes, before the hold lifts) is never said and, without notifications, never told (as the journal question still queued at stop); with `normal`, a game changes nothing (v1.33).

Privacy: nothing new is stored or sent: game mode keeps whether a game runs in memory only, logs starts and stops at DEBUG, and its routines run on the owner's own devices like any routine (their failures logged at WARNING with the step).

Safety: tests never list the real processes, run real devices (`MockDevice` registries and fake watchers), sleep, speak, or push; every clock is a fake; `test_cli.py` keeps its fake `PlayTimeWatcher` and every schedule under tmp_path or a dry run's folder.

### perbaikan fokus dan waifu mic (v1.34.1)

A bug found in the final check of v1.34, and a microphone the owner could not get heard.

Focus: v1.34's expiries of the gacha reminders about a game's state (the next poll) and of the hot-GPU notice (an hour) applied to every hold, so a v1.30 focus block dropped them too: a 30-minute block with `game_mode` unset lost "Trailblaze Power ... 295 dari 300" and "Daily Training belum selesai", which v1.33 said at the break. The fix: `announce(..., game_expires_at=None)` is counted only for an announcement a game held, marked one by one (see mode game, Staleness of held items; a first version marked the whole queue once a game had held anything, so a reminder queued after a game closed during a block was still dropped at the break); the gacha and PC watchers pass their expiries there, and the rain alert keeps `expires_at`. Without a `game_mode` section nothing differs from v1.33 but the rain alert and a repeated text said once.

Mic: the owner said the mic is not detected. On his PC `audio.input_device` was unset, so Cyrene opened the Windows default recording device, "Microphone (High Definition Audio Device)" (the onboard jack), whose level was only the noise floor (about -51 dB RMS, peak 0.006) while the VAD needs speech at -45 dB or more and `margin_db` (12 dB) above the mic's own noise, about -39 dB for that jack; a Bluetooth headset, "Headset (Polaris-BT)", was connected, and Windows allowed desktop apps the mic.

User stories:

1. `waifu mic` lists every input once, marking the one Cyrene uses and the Windows default; while Master says "halo Cyrene" it listens to each for a few seconds, prints each one's peak and RMS in dB with a bar and whether the VAD would take it for speech, and ends with the exact `audio.input_device` line for the mic that heard him best, or says the mic in use already does.
2. `waifu check` says which mic Cyrene uses and whether it is the Windows default.

Non-goals (v1.34.1): saving or playing audio; writing the config for him; switching a Bluetooth headset's profile; measuring through WDM-KS; taking the run lock (its streams are shared, so it runs beside `waifu run`); recording in `check`; a config key, tool, or dependency.

`voice/mic_check.py` (sounddevice passed in):
- `input_devices(sd) -> list[InputDevice]` (`index`, `name`, `host_api`, `sample_rate`): the input entries (`max_input_channels > 0`) but the default's aliases ("Microsoft Sound Mapper - Input", "Primary Sound Capture Driver") and the host API "Windows WDM-KS" (kernel streaming opens a device exclusively, so it would take the mic from a running Cyrene, and it lists paired headsets that are not connected); the host API of `sd.default.device[0]` first (MME on Windows), then PortAudio's order; one entry per name: `same_device_name(a, b)` when equal, or when the shorter has 31 characters (MME's cut) and starts the longer. `InputDevice.config_value` is `"<name>, <host API>"`, which sounddevice resolves to that entry alone as an exact match of its full string (the bare name matches every host API's entry, and a WDM-KS hands-free name too: "Multiple input devices"); `config_line` is `input_device: "<config_value>"` (JSON-quoted, valid YAML).
- `windows_default_input(sd) -> str | None`: the name at `sd.default.device[0]` (PortAudio's default input, the Windows default recording device); None when that is -1.
- `measure(sd, device, seconds, audio, *, clock=time.monotonic) -> Levels`: listens as Cyrene does, through `MicStream(audio.sample_rate, vad.frame_ms, device.index, sd_module=sd, clock=clock)` (a shared callback stream at the device's own rate, so it opens beside a running `waifu run`, resampled to the VAD's rate: the frames the VAD gets), for `seconds` in whole frames (at least one), then closes it. A device that opens but stops delivering raises `MicrophoneError` at MicStream's 2 s stall check instead of waiting forever (a first version made one blocking `read`, which a headset dropping its hands-free link could hang). The samples live in memory for their levels only.
- `levels(audio, rate, vad) -> Levels(peak_db, rms_db, speech_db, speech_bar_db, heard_speech)`, in dBFS as `segmenter.frame_db`, over frames of `vad.frame_ms`: `speech_bar_db = max(vad.threshold_db, 10th percentile of the frames + vad.margin_db)` (the quiet frames stand in for the noise floor the VAD learns); `speech_db` the loudest level held through the VAD's onset (the quietest frame of the loudest run of `ceil(ONSET_MS / frame_ms)` frames, so a click does not raise it); `heard_speech` when `EnergySegmenter`'s rules would keep an utterance of the frames at or above the bar: it starts with ONSET_MS of them in a row, ends after `silence_ms` without, and is kept with `min_speech_ms` of them in it (one still going at the end counts), so a click, a cough, or a plosive alone is not heard (a first version took a single frame at the bar for speech); `silent` when the peak is at most -80 dB (below any real microphone's own noise: muted, blocked, or a Bluetooth headset still switching to hands-free, which on the owner's PC gave a -90 dB peak in its first two seconds). `level_bar(levels)`: 30 cells from -80 to 0 dB, `#` up to `speech_db`, `|` at the bar.

CLI:
- `waifu mic [--seconds 3]` (more than 0, else argparse's exit 2) loads the config and imports sounddevice (missing: the usual package message, exit 1). Without any input: `✗ Windows tidak menemukan mikrofon. Sambungkan mic atau headset, lalu cek Windows Settings, Privacy & security, Microphone.` Then the mic in use: unset `input_device`, "Cyrene memakai mic default Windows, sekarang <default> (audio.input_device kosong). Default yang berubah baru dipakai setelah 'waifu run' dijalankan ulang." (no default: `✗ audio.input_device kosong, dan Windows tidak punya mic default.`); set, "Cyrene memakai audio.input_device '<value>': <name>." or `✗ audio.input_device '<value>' tidak bisa dipakai: <why>`. "Mikrofon di PC ini:" lists `  N. <config_value>`, with `  <- dipakai Cyrene, default Windows` as they apply (by name). After "Sebut 'halo Cyrene' beberapa kali selama N detik di tiap mikrofon, dengan suara biasa. Rekamannya tidak disimpan.", each device: `[i/n] <config_value>: mendengarkan...`, then `  puncak P dB, RMS R dB [bar] ✓ suara terdengar`, `... ✗ suara tidak terdengar (butuh B dB)`, `  puncak P dB, RMS R dB [bar] ✗ hampir tanpa suara: mic di-mute atau diblokir Windows, atau headset Bluetooth belum siap (coba --seconds 5)` (its levels too: digital zero, -200 dB, is muted or blocked, while a headset not yet in hands-free gives about -90 dB), `  ✗ Mikrofon terputus atau berhenti mengirim suara. Periksa sambungan mikrofon, lalu jalankan ulang.` (`MicrophoneError`), or `  ✗ Tidak bisa dibuka: <why>`, and on to the next. The verdict goes to the mic that heard speech whose `speech_db` stood furthest above its bar: the one in use, `✓ Suaramu paling jelas di mic yang dipakai Cyrene: <name>.`; another, `✓ Suaramu paling jelas di <name>. Supaya Cyrene memakainya, isi audio.input_device di config.yaml (baris ini di bawah audio:):`, `    input_device: "<config_value>"`, and "Lalu jalankan ulang 'waifu run', dan daftarkan ulang suaramu dengan 'waifu enroll' lewat mic itu."; none, `✗ Tidak ada mic yang mendengar suara sekeras yang dibutuhkan Cyrene.` with what to try. Always exit 0.
- `check`'s mic line: `✓ Mikrofon: <name>, default Windows (audio.input_device kosong)`, `✓ Mikrofon: <name> dari audio.input_device, sama dengan default Windows`, or `✓ Mikrofon: <name> dari audio.input_device (default Windows: <default>)` (`(Windows tanpa mic default)`); `✗ Mikrofon tidak ditemukan: <why>` as before. Nothing is recorded.

Known limits: the bar approximates the VAD, which learns the noise floor as it listens; a Bluetooth headset may take a moment to switch to hands-free, so speak throughout its turn; the list and the config line show MME's 31-character names; two identical mics under one host API share a config value, which sounddevice refuses (`waifu mics`' number then); without `input_device` a running `waifu run` keeps the default it started with.

Docs: README "Pemecahan masalah", "Mic tidak terdeteksi atau tidak mau dibuka.", leads with `waifu mic`, says that the default counts at the start of `waifu run` and that an onboard jack without a mic hears only noise, and adds the Bluetooth note (Windows switches a headset to a lower quality hands-free mode while its mic is open, as Cyrene's mic for as long as `waifu run` runs); the commands table, "Mendaftarkan suara Master", the config table, and the hardware checklist name `waifu mic`; "Teman fokus", "Mode game", and the gacha and PC sections say that only a game drops the stale notices; CHANGELOG v1.34.1.

Privacy: nothing is saved or sent; a measurement's samples live in memory for its levels only.

Safety: no test opens a stream or plays audio: a fake sounddevice everywhere, and one test runs the installed sounddevice's own name matching over a made-up listing (skipped without it), querying and opening no device; every clock in the focus tests is a fake.

### tombol cepat di HP (v1.35)

From the phone the owner mostly wants one thing done: the lamp off, the AC to 24, "mode tidur". Typing it to her costs a brain turn (seconds, and API money with Claude) for a one-word job. The phone page (v1.14) gains a panel of quick buttons that run his devices and routines directly through the device registry, as `waifu device` and `waifu routine run` do, without the brain.

User stories:

1. On the phone page, a lamp button left of the text box opens "Tombol cepat": a group per device (a lamp: Nyala, Mati, Nyala/mati, 10%, 30%, 60%, 100%; the AC: Nyala, Mati, and the learned temperatures of 18°C, 20°C, ... 28°C; a plug: Nyala, Mati, Nyala/mati) and a group "Rutinitas" with a button per routine ("Mode tidur"). A tap runs it at once, and a short Indonesian toast tells the result ("Lampu kamar dinyalakan.", "Mode tidur: 3 dari 3 langkah berhasil. Dijadwalkan jam 01.00 dini hari nanti.").
2. The PC is never a button: no shutdown, restart, sleep, or wake, and no button at all for the plug that feeds the PC (`powers_pc`), not even "on". Those stay voice and chat commands with the confirmation in code (v1.9).
3. Optionally, `phone.quick_actions` picks and orders the devices and routines that show; without it every device that can have buttons and every routine shows.

Non-goals (v1.35): a button for the PC or a `powers_pc` plug behind a second confirmation step (decision: left out, which is simpler and safer than a confirm step with a short-lived server token; README says so); media keys and apps (not on/off switches: `play_pause` and `mute` toggle without showing the state, and opening an app takes the focus from a game); other IR buttons (volume, input, navigation); AC +/- (an IR device's state is only the last code this process sent, a change made with the remote is invisible, so +/- would guess; a preset names its temperature); live device state on the buttons; changing routines or buttons from the phone; telling the brain about a button (her conversation may still say the lamp is on until she reads the live state with `list_devices`); buttons in `chat` or without `phone.enabled`; a brain tool (the strict-tool budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters).

The buttons (`phone/actions.py`), built afresh from the registry and the config at every request, so a newly learned IR code shows at the next load of the page:
- Devices of kind `light`, `plug`, `ir`, and `mock` (a dry run's mocks keep the real device's kind), in config order, leaving out every device for which `registry.powers_pc` is true (the PC and its plug, with every command); kinds `pc`, `media`, and `apps` never.
- Per device, of the commands it has, in this order: `on` ("Nyala"), `off` ("Mati"), `toggle` ("Nyala/mati"), `power` ("Tombol power", an IR remote's power button); for a light with `brightness`, brightness 10, 30, 60, and 100 ("10%"); for an IR device, `temp_N` for each N of 18, 20, 22, 24, 26, and 28 that is learned ("24°C"). A device with none of these has no group. A device whose commands cannot be read (an IR code file that is not valid JSON) is left out and logged at WARNING, and the rest still show.
- Routines: every routine, in config order, in one group "Rutinitas", each labelled by its description, else its name.
- An action's id: `dev.<device>.<command>`, `dev.<device>.brightness.<value>`, or `rt.<routine>` (device ids, IR command names, and routine names are lowercase slugs, so an id is plain ASCII: `ACTION_ID`).
- `QuickActions(registry, routines, *, schedule=None, chosen=None, now=datetime.now)` (`chosen`, `phone.quick_actions`; None shows all): `groups() -> list[QuickGroup(name, actions)]`, each `QuickAction(id, label, device, command, value, routine)`; `find(id) -> QuickAction | None`, which looks the id up in a list built then (nothing of the device, the command, or the value comes from the phone); `run(action) -> QuickResult(ok, message)`, one at a time under a lock of its own (the devices have no locks; the scheduler, the brain, and game mode still call the registry from their own threads). A device action is refused first with `routines.pc_power_refusal(registry, device, "tombol cepat HP")`, as defence in depth (such a device is never listed, so `find` never returns one), then runs through `registry.execute`, its message as it is ("Lampu kamar dinyalakan.", or the registry's Indonesian failure). A routine runs through `run_routine(registry, routine, name=..., schedule=..., now=...)` with the run's schedule (a delayed step is scheduled, and a new run replaces the pending steps of the one before, as by voice): `"<label>: <n> dari <m> langkah berhasil."`, then the first failed step's message, then each different message of a scheduled step ("Dijadwalkan jam 01.00 dini hari nanti."); ok when every step is.

Config `phone.quick_actions` (default: every device and routine above): `devices` and `routines`, each a list that shows those in that order (empty: none of them), or left out (all of them). A ConfigError at load for an unknown device or routine, a device of type `pc`, `media`, or `apps`, a `powers_pc` plug (`phone.quick_actions may not name 'colokan_pc': it powers the PC, which is never a quick button`), and a name given twice.

Phone server (`PhoneServer(..., actions: QuickActions | None = None)`):
- `GET /actions` behind the device cookie (401 otherwise, as `/config.json`), never cached: `{"groups": [{"name", "actions": [{"id", "label"}]}]}`; `{"groups": []}` without `actions`.
- `POST /action {"id"}` is a POST route like `/chat`, so it needs one `Origin` of the page's own (403) and the device cookie (401); then at most `ACTION_RATE_LIMIT` (10) actions per device in any `ACTION_RATE_WINDOW_S` (60 s), counted before the body is read (429 with `Retry-After: 60`; the avatar server's `RollingLimit`, made public, per `Device.id`, since every request comes from 127.0.0.1 through `tailscale serve`); the body of at most 1 KB, strict JSON with only `id` (411, 413, 400, or 415 as for `/pair`); an id shaped as `ACTION_ID` (400); an id in the current list (404, "Tombol itu sudah tidak ada. Muat ulang halaman."); one action at a time per device (409 "Perintah tadi masih jalan. Tunggu sebentar.", without `Retry-After`: not the limit's 429, so the page tells a press that waits for the last one, from another tab or after a reload, from too many presses; apart from the chat's one at a time, so a button works while she answers a chat); then 200 `{"ok", "message"}`. A device's failure is a 200 with `ok: false`, so its text reaches the toast; anything raised is a 500 with `FAILED_TEXT`, logged with its traceback.
- `GET /action` and `POST /actions` are 405 (`Allow: POST`, `Allow: GET`).
- Logs: INFO `Phone device d1 ran quick action dev.lampu_kamar.on: ok` (or `failed`), the message at DEBUG; refusals at INFO with the device id, and the action id only once it is shaped as one. Never the cookie or the token.

Page (`avatar/web/phone/`):
- `index.html`: `#quick-toggle`, a round 44 px button left of the text box (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls="quick"`, label "Tombol cepat"), hidden until `/actions` lists a group, and by `phone.css` while the owner types (the text box focused and not empty) with the panel closed, so the text box keeps the room it had before v1.35 (168 px instead of 116 at 360 px with the camera button; `phone.js` fits its height again when it gains or loses the focus, as its width changes then); `<section id="quick" aria-label="Tombol cepat" hidden>` between the notice and the text box: `#chat` is anchored at the bottom of the screen, so the toast above the panel pushes up only the chat list, never the buttons under the owner's finger (nor drops them back 6 s later).
- `actions.js` (pure, under `node --test`): `parseActionGroups(data)` (malformed groups and actions dropped, empty groups too; null when the answer has no list), `actionRequest(id)` (`{body, contentType}`), `actionOutcome(data)` (`{ok, text}`, or null for an answer it cannot read).
- `phone.js`: once the chat shows it reads `/actions` (a problem leaves the button hidden, without a notice). The toggle shows and hides the panel, built with `createElement`: an `<h2>` and a `role="group"` per group, `type="button"` buttons; opening it puts the focus on its first button (the panel comes before the toggle in the page, so Tab or a screen reader's next from the toggle would skip it); Escape in the panel or on the toggle closes it and puts the focus on the toggle; opening it closes the viewfinder, and opening the viewfinder closes it (one tray at a time). A tap posts the id; until the answer the button is `aria-busy` and every button `aria-disabled` (not `disabled`, so the focus stays), and taps are ignored. The answer's message, or the problem, shows in `#notice` for 6 s (`role="status"`); 401 shows the pairing form. Chat and talk stay usable meanwhile.
- `audio.js`: problem kinds `action_unknown` (404 on `/action`), `action_limit` (429 on `/action`), and `action_busy` (409 on `/action`, the server's text).
- `phone.css`: buttons at least 44 px high in a grid of `minmax(96px, 1fr)` (three a row at 360-412 px), the panel at most 36dvh with its own scroll, the chat list smaller while it is open, styles for `aria-busy` and `aria-disabled`; no new animation.
- `sw.js` keeps `/phone/actions.js` with the shell; `/actions` and `/action` pass to the network.

CLI: `run` hands the phone server `QuickActions(registry, config.routines, schedule=<the run's schedule>, chosen=config.phone.quick_actions)`, with the run's own registry (the mocks under `--dry-run`).

Guest mode (v1.36) leaves the quick buttons working: they are device control.

Docs: README "Tombol cepat di HP" in "Cyrene di HP" (what shows, the PC is never a button, the toast, the limits, `phone.quick_actions`), checklist items; `config.example.yaml` documents `quick_actions`; CHANGELOG v1.35.

Privacy: nothing new is stored or sent; action ids and their outcome are logged as above; the brain never sees a button.

Safety: tests use `MockDevice` registries, never a real device, 127.0.0.1 port 0, fake clocks, and tmp schedules; no test sends a PC power command: the PC kinds are refused at load, never listed, and refused again at run time.

### mode tamu (v1.36)

Friends come over and sit in the room while Master talks to her. Until now the only way to keep his money, journal, tasks, and the rest out of earshot was to turn features off in `config.yaml` and restart (`tepira.enabled: false`, v1.24). Guest mode does it by voice, for a few hours, without touching the config.

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, mode tamu" (or "ada tamu nih") → she turns guest mode on and says so in one short sentence ("Mode tamu aktif sampai jam sebelas malam"). "Cyrene, mode tamu selesai" turns it off; "mode tamu masih aktif?" asks. Also typed in `waifu chat` and in the phone chat.
2. While it is on, "pengeluaranku bulan ini berapa?", "jurnal kemarin apa?", "tugasku apa aja?", "pity HSR-ku berapa?", "ingat ya, aku suka kopi" → every tool that reads or writes Master's own records or accounts refuses in code, and she says briefly that it stays closed while guests are here. The lamp, the AC, routines, songs, the weather, and plain chat work as always, the phone's quick buttons (v1.35) too.
3. She stops drawing on what she remembers about Master: from the turn after the switch, a new conversation starts without the remembered facts in her prompt and without the earlier turns (which may hold a finance or journal answer); the same when it ends.
4. Her morning greeting in guest mode tells only the weather and the device steps scheduled today, without tasks, reminder texts, games, or memory. Her nightly journal question and the weekly game report are skipped.
5. Guest mode ends by itself `guest_mode.max_hours` (6) after it was turned on, or at 05:00, whichever comes first, so a forgotten guest mode never lasts into the next day. A restart of `waifu run` ends it too.
6. `waifu check` says how guest mode is turned on and when it ends.

Non-goals (v1.36): a `waifu tamu on|off|status` command (decision: the CLI runs in its own process and cannot reach the running `waifu run`, whose guest mode lives in memory; a command that changed only its own process would mislead); keeping guest mode across a restart; a spoken notice when it ends by itself; a schedule for it ("mode tamu jam 7"); asking the skipped journal question or weekly report later; holding them until guest mode ends; changing the self-started prompts (the own-turn refusal tables key on their exact text); hiding the fixed-text announcements (Master's own reminders, the focus lines, the gacha, code, PC, rain, version, offers, budget, and bedtime notices); guest mode in the terminal commands (`waifu todo`, `waifu jurnal`, `waifu tepira`, ...: the terminal is Master's own) or on the phone's quick buttons (device control); per-guest voice profiles; removing tools from the request (the tools stay byte-identical and refuse at run time, so the prompt cache stays warm); a new dependency; a strict tool (`guest_mode` has no required argument: the budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters).

The private tools (refused while guest mode is on): a tool that reads or writes Master's own records or accounts.
- Money (tepira): `finance_summary`, `wallet_balances`, `savings_goals`, `recent_transactions`, `record_expense`, `undo_expense`.
- Journal: `journal_write`, `journal_read`.
- Memory: `remember`, `forget` (a guest's fact must not be saved as Master's, `forget` would delete Master's notes, and its result repeats the note; the notes reach the model only through the prompt, see below).
- To-do list: `add_task`, `list_tasks`, `complete_task`, `reopen_task`, `remove_task`, `edit_task`.
- Play time: `play_time`.
- Game accounts: `game_status`, `gacha_history`, `gacha_income`, `game_characters`, `game_endgame`, `game_events` (not a public calendar: it is read from Master's HoYoLAB account and carries his progress in each running event, the Polychrome he earned from it, and his stars in each endgame window), `weekly_report`, `redeem_codes`, `redeem_codes_list` (the list holds the outcomes on Master's account).
- Schedule list: `list_schedules` (its reminder texts), `cancel_schedule` (would cancel Master's entries).
- API cost: `api_usage`.
- Screen: `look_at_screen` (the PC screen may show private things).

Still allowed: `control_device` (the PC's power still with Master's yes, v1.9) and `list_devices`, `run_routine`, `schedule` (adding an entry), `play_youtube`, `pc_health` (CPU, GPU, disk), `free_offers`, `send_offer_link`, `focus_timer` (a control like the devices: a block started before the guests came must still stop on Master's word, or its lines go on in front of them; its status and stop name the time focused today and this week, which is low-stakes), `japanese_lesson` (v1.39: beginner words and how many of them Master knows are low-stakes, like the focus totals), Claude's `web_search`, `guest_mode`, `daily_briefing` (reduced, below), and plain chat. A test lists every tool of a request with every feature on and fails for a tool on neither list, so a new tool must be sorted.

Config `guest_mode`: `max_hours` (6, 1-24). Nothing else: guest mode is always available.

`guest.py` — `GuestMode(*, max_hours=6, now=datetime.now)`, one per process (`run` and `chat`), shared by the brain, its tools, the briefing tool, and the Assistant (as `TurnImages` is), its state under a lock and in memory only:
- `PRIVATE_TOOLS` (the list above, as literal names: `guest.py` is a leaf, so no brain module is imported; a test checks them against each module's constant) and `GUEST_REFUSAL` ("Mode tamu sedang aktif: data pribadi Master tidak dibuka dulu.").
- `turn_on() -> Toggle(changed, until)`: `until` = the earlier of `now + max_hours` and the next 05:00 strictly after `now`, on the PC's local clock (naive `datetime.now`, as the briefing and the schedule use; the owner lives in WIB). Turning it on while it is on renews `until` from now by the same rule (`changed` False). INFO "Guest mode on until 2026-10-24 23:00" (or "Guest mode renewed until ...").
- `turn_off() -> Toggle(changed, None)`: `changed` False when it was off. INFO "Guest mode off".
- `is_on()`: whether it is on; the first call at or after `until` ends it (lazy: checked by a turn, a tool call, a request, or the briefing), logged at INFO "Guest mode ended by itself at <until>". `status() -> GuestStatus(on, until)`.
- `generation() -> int`: goes up whenever guest mode turns on (from off), off, or ends by itself (after the lazy check), never on a renewal; the brains' session marker.
- `refusal(name) -> str | None`: `GUEST_REFUSAL` for a name in `PRIVATE_TOOLS` while it is on, else None.

The refusal, in the shared dispatcher (no provider gets its own check):
- `brain/tools.run_tool(..., refusal: Callable[[str], str | None] | None = None)`: before anything else, a call `refusal` names is answered `{"ok": false, "message": <refusal>}` as an error and runs nothing (so it comes before the confirmation gate: `record_expense` never asks its question). INFO "Refused tool '<name>' before running it".
- `ClaudeBrain` and `OllamaBrain(..., tool_refusal=None, session_marker=None)` pass `tool_refusal` to every `run_tool`.

A new session on entering and leaving:
- `session_marker: Callable[[], object] | None` (guest mode's `generation`): read when a session's system prompt is read (before it, so a change in between ends the session at the next turn), and compared at the start of every turn with history. When it differs, the session ends before the turn, logged at INFO, also while a question awaits Master's yes: that question is forgotten (`forget_question`), so a later "ya" runs nothing. The turn that turns guest mode on or off stays in the old session: its reply may still draw on that session (the persona tells her not to), the refusals apply from the switch on.
- `brain/factory.build_brain(..., guest_mode: GuestMode | None = None)` passes `tool_refusal=guest_mode.refusal` and `session_marker=guest_mode.generation`, and makes the system prompt callable (read at each session start) whenever `guest_mode` or `memory_section` is given: while guest mode is on, the base prompt, a blank line, and `GUEST_SECTION`, a static text (no live state, so one cache write per guest session and the persona and device prefix still cached) saying guest mode is on, her memory of Master is hidden, she never mentions Master's private things however asked, the private tools refuse, devices, routines, music, weather, and chat work, the morning greeting tells only the weather and the device steps of the day, and `guest_mode` with on false ends it when Master says so; otherwise the base prompt with the memory section as before (memory on), or the base prompt alone (memory off). The remembered facts are never read in guest mode.

`brain/guest_tools.py` — `GuestTools(guest_mode)`, a `ToolProvider` with `guest_mode {on?: boolean}`, non-strict (nothing required), its description static English: turn guest mode on when Master says guests are here or asks for it, off when Master says it is over, leave `on` out to check; what it hides, that the next turn starts a new conversation, that it ends by itself after a few hours or at 05:00, and only at Master's own request. Results `{"on", "until"` ("HH:MM", null when off)`, "message"}`: "Mode tamu aktif sampai jam 23:00.", "Mode tamu sudah aktif, sekarang sampai jam 23:00.", "Mode tamu selesai.", "Mode tamu memang tidak aktif.", status "Mode tamu aktif sampai jam 23:00." or "Mode tamu tidak aktif."; bad arguments (not an object, an unknown key, `on` not a boolean) are an error result. It is always added, first among the providers.

Self-started turns:
- Morning greeting: still asked and said (also pushed with `briefing.push`), reduced. `BriefingTools(..., guest_mode: Callable[[], bool] | None = None)`: while it is on, `daily_briefing` leaves out `tasks` and `tasks_problem` and the schedule's reminder entries (their text is Master's), keeping the weather and the device and routine entries. `BRIEFING_PROMPT` is unchanged; `game_status` refuses, and the prompt holds no memory. `waifu briefing` passes none.
- `Assistant.ask_aloud(..., private=False)` and `Assistant(..., guest_mode: Callable[[], bool] | None = None)`: a `private` request that comes to be answered while guest mode is on is dropped before its turn (no new session for nothing), logged at INFO "Not saying a private request while guest mode is on" (never the prompt), never thought up, said, or passed to `on_reply`; every dropped request at the head goes in the same pass, as expired ones do. The nightly journal question and the weekly game report are private; their timers saved the day before asking, so they are not asked again that day or week (`waifu gacha report` still shows the week). Both turns would also fail their `required_tool` (refused), so neither could be said anyway.
- Fixed-text announcements are unchanged: Master's reminders (`remind`) still speak with their text, his own choice when he set them; so do the focus lines and the gacha, code, PC, rain, version, offers, budget (it names the API spend), and bedtime notices. README's privacy note says so.

The phone: a phone chat is a turn of the same brain under the same lock (`reply_for_phone`), so guest mode applies to it, and "mode tamu selesai" typed on the phone ends it for the PC too. The quick buttons never reach the brain and keep working.

CLI:
- `run` builds one `GuestMode(max_hours=config.guest_mode.max_hours)` before the brain and hands it to `_companion_brain` (`GuestTools`, `build_brain(guest_mode=...)`, the briefing tool's `guest_mode=guest_mode.is_on`) and to the Assistant (`guest_mode=guest_mode.is_on`); the journal and weekly report requests pass `private=True`. `chat` builds its own for its brain (no timers).
- `check`, right after the game mode line: `✓ Mode tamu: "mode tamu" dan "mode tamu selesai" lewat suara, chat, atau HP; berakhir sendiri setelah 6 jam atau jam 05:00`, or with `speaker.enabled: false` `! Mode tamu: ...; verifikasi suara mati, jadi suara tamu juga bisa mengakhirinya`.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Mode tamu" after "Keuangan dari tepira" (turning it on and off, what is closed and what works, the new conversation, the greeting and the skipped turns, the end, the check row, privacy) with a contents entry, checklist items, and a Privasi bullet; the tepira advice to turn `tepira.enabled` off with guests points to it; `config.example.yaml` documents `guest_mode`; both personas get one identical "Mode tamu:" section before "Keuangan Master:" (which stays last); CHANGELOG v1.36.

Known limits: the turn that switches guest mode on still runs in the old session, so its reply could echo what that session held; tool results already in the history go only when the session resets at the next turn; with speaker verification off (`--no-speaker-check`, `speaker.enabled: false`) any voice can end it, while with it on guests are not heard at all and guest mode guards only what she says aloud; a restart or crash ends it without a word, and the automatic end is lazy and silent; a skipped journal question or weekly report is not asked again; the model may still say something private it inferred from the turn that switched (the refusals and the prompt are the guarantees, the persona line a guide).

Privacy: nothing new is stored or sent: guest mode lives in memory only; the tool call goes to Anthropic like any other with the Claude backend; INFO logs on, off, the automatic end, refusals (the tool's name only), and dropped requests (never the prompt).

Safety: tests use fake clocks and fake brains or SDK-shaped fake clients, never a real device, push, or speech.

### volume suaraku (v1.37)

Her voice comes out of the PC at whatever level the TTS and the anime voice give it, and the only knob so far is the Windows volume (the `media` device, v1.8), which also moves the music and the game. Master wants her own voice softer or louder by itself, and softer at night, without touching the PC's volume.

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, kecilkan suaramu" / "suaramu kekerasan" → her own voice gets one step (20 percent) softer, and she says so in one short sentence, on the PC already heard at the new level ("Segini ya, delapan puluh persen."). "Keraskan suaramu", "suara kamu kurang keras" → one step louder. "Suaramu 60 persen" sets it; "suaramu berapa?" tells it. Also typed in `waifu chat` and in the phone chat (the voice on the PC changes; the phone plays its own reply as before).
2. "Kecilkan volume", "volume 30", "kecilkan musiknya" stay the media device's Windows volume keys (v1.8), which move everything the PC plays, her voice included. The tool's description and the media device's line tell the two apart, and the possessive decides: "suaramu" / "suara kamu" / "volume suaramu" is hers even with the word volume, "volume" alone, "musik", and "PC" are the media device.
3. "Cyrene, nyalakan mode bisik" → every night from `voice.night.from` (22:00) until `voice.night.until` (06:00) her voice is at most `voice.night.level` (50) percent, until "matikan mode bisik". The morning greeting at or after `until` is at her normal level again.
4. The level and whisper mode survive a restart of `waifu run`; `waifu say` speaks at the current level, and `--volume 60` tries one without saving it; `waifu check` tells the level and whisper mode.

Non-goals (v1.37): the Windows master volume, per-app volume in the Windows mixer, or the music's volume (the media device's job); the phone's playback (a phone reply is played by the phone, at the phone's volume); edge-tts's `volume` setting (static, edge-tts only, and it would change the phone's voice too); loudness matching between voices or clips; a whispering voice style (only the level changes); switching whisper mode by the hour on its own or from `config.yaml` (it is switched by voice; the config holds only its window and level); fades; a `waifu volume` command (voice, chat, and the phone change it; `say --volume` tries a level); scaling the avatar's mouth with the level; a new dependency; a strict tool (`voice_volume` requires nothing: the budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters).

The gain:
- Linear amplitude: a level of 50 halves every sample (-6 dB), 150 multiplies it by 1.5. The boring choice; the steps of 20 are even in amplitude, not in loudness.
- Applied after synthesis and the anime voice (RVC), right before playback, never before RVC: the clip goes to the RVC server as 16-bit PCM, which hard-clips anything beyond full scale, and RVC may re-level it anyway.
- At 100 the clip is played untouched (the same array). Below 100 it is only scaled. Above 100 it is scaled, then every sample goes through a soft limiter: linear up to 0.8 of full scale, above it `sign(x) * (0.8 + 0.18 * tanh((|x| - 0.8) / 0.18))`, continuous and smooth at the knee and always below 0.98, so no peak reaches full scale and clips, whatever level the voice came at (the RVC output level is not controlled in this repo). The output stays float32. Silence stays silence.
- The avatar's lip-sync gets the clip before the gain, so the mouth does not shrink at 10 percent or in whisper mode: the Assistant and `cli._speak` hand the avatar the clip before `play`, and the gain is in the player.

`voice/volume.py`:
- `LEVELS = (10, 150)`, `STEP = 20`, `NORMAL = 100`; `apply_volume(audio, level) -> np.ndarray` as above.
- `VolumePlayer(player, level: Callable[[], int])`, an `AudioPlayer`: `play(audio, sample_rate)` plays `apply_volume(audio, level())` through `player`, the level read at every clip; `stop()` delegates (cutting her off, v1.11). It makes no PortAudio call itself, so `Player`'s one-thread rule holds.

`voice_level.py` — `VoiceLevel(path, night: VoiceNightConfig, *, now=datetime.now)`, the level and whisper mode:
- The file `data/voice.json` (`voice.state_path`): `{"version": 1, "level": 100, "night": false}`, exactly these keys, `level` a whole number 10–150 (not a boolean), `night` a boolean. Re-read at every call, replaced atomically (`write_json_atomic`), never created by reading (missing: level 100, whisper mode off), and a file that is not such a state is moved aside as `<name>.corrupt-YYYYmmdd-HHMMSS[-n]` (ERROR log) and read as missing; a failed move raises `OSError`. One lock serialises the calls of a process. `run`, `chat`, and `say` share the file, so a change made in one is heard in the others at their next clip.
- `state() -> VoiceState(level, night)`; `set_level(level)`, `change("louder" | "softer")` (one step to the next multiple of `STEP` in that direction, held in `LEVELS`: 75 softer is 60, 150 softer is 140, 10 louder is 20, so after either end the steps still reach 100; softer steps from `level_for(state)`, the level heard now, so while whisper mode holds her below her level the next clip is softer at once and her level drops to it, 120 at 23:00 softer is 40; louder steps from her level, which whisper mode may still hold back), and `set_night(on)` each return `VoiceChange(before, after)` and write nothing when nothing changes (ValueError for a level outside `LEVELS` or another direction). INFO logs "Voice level 80 (was 100)", "Whisper mode on", "Whisper mode off".
- `whispering(state) -> bool`: whisper mode on and the PC's local time (naive `datetime.now`, as the briefing, the rain alert, and guest mode use) inside the window: `from <= t < until` when `from` is before `until`, else, across midnight, `t >= from or t < until`; `from` counts, `until` does not.
