- `level_for(state) -> int`: the level a clip is played at now with `state`, `min(level, night.level)` while whispering, else `level`. `level_now() -> int`: `level_for` the file's state, read at every clip at play time, so a reply said at or after `until` (the morning greeting among them) is at the normal level, and a change is heard from her very next clip, the reply confirming it included. It never raises: when the file cannot be read (an `OSError`, or a broken file that cannot be moved aside) it logs once until the file reads again (one WARNING, after one ERROR for a broken file that cannot be moved aside), never once per clip, and uses the last state it read, or level 100 with whisper mode off. She never goes silent over her volume.

Config `voice`: `state_path` (`data/voice.json`) and `night`: `from` (22:00), `until` (06:00), `level` (50, 10–100). `from` and `until` are clock times HH:MM (a bare YAML `22:00` too, which YAML reads as base 60), never with a time zone (pydantic reads 1440 and more as seconds in UTC), and never equal (an empty window); `until` before `from` wraps past midnight. The field is `from_` with the alias `from` (a Python keyword); only `from` is accepted in the file. `VoiceConfig` is taken (the TTS voice union), so the models are `VoiceVolumeConfig` and `VoiceNightConfig`.

`brain/voice_volume_tools.py` — `VoiceVolumeTools(level: VoiceLevel)`, a `ToolProvider` with `voice_volume {level?: integer, change?: enum[louder, softer], night?: boolean}`, non-strict (nothing required), without numeric bounds in the schema (the ranges are in the description and checked in code). Its description is static English: only for HER own voice ("suaramu", "suara kamu", "kecilkan volume suaramu", "jangan keras-keras ngomongnya", "mode bisik"; not "pelan-pelan", which mostly means slowly), the possessive deciding: "suaramu", "suara kamu", or "volume suaramu" are hers even with the word volume; "volume" alone, "musik", "PC", and the Windows volume are `control_device` on the media device, which changes everything the PC plays, her voice included; speaking slower or faster is not this tool; `level` 10 to 150 percent (100 normal), `change` one step of 20 percent, `night` whisper mode on or off (at most the night level inside its window every night), nothing to tell the state; phone replies play on the phone and are not affected; only at Master's request; on the PC her answer is already heard at `now`, which whisper mode may keep below the level just set (never "at the new level" without condition, so she never claims a voice that is not heard); one short sentence with the percentage spoken naturally; after an error result, say its message briefly and do not call again in this turn.
- Arguments (null counts as left out): `level` a whole number 10–150, never a boolean; `change` one of the two; not `level` and `change` together; `night` a boolean, alone or with either; none: the state. Not an object, an unknown key, or a bad value is an error result.
- Results: `{"level", "night", "night_from", "night_until", "night_level", "now", "whispering", "message"}` (times `HH:MM`; `now` the level a clip is played at now), with `"changed"` after a change. Messages: "Suaraku sekarang 80 persen.", "Suaraku memang sudah 80 persen.", "Suaraku sudah paling keras, 150 persen.", "Suaraku sudah paling pelan, 10 persen.", "Mode bisik aktif: dari jam 22:00 sampai 06:00 suaraku paling keras 50 persen.", "Mode bisik memang sudah aktif.", "Mode bisik mati.", "Mode bisik memang mati."; with both a level and `night`, the night message, a space, then the level message. The state: "Suaraku 100 persen; mode bisik mati." or "Suaraku 100 persen; mode bisik aktif, jam 22:00 sampai 06:00 paling keras 50 persen.". While whispering with the level above the night level, a level message and the state end ", tapi mode bisik menahannya di 50 persen sampai jam 06:00." instead, so she never claims a louder voice that is not heard.
- `begin_turn` refuses the tool in her own turns (`BRIEFING_PROMPT`, `WEEKLY_REPORT_PROMPT`, `JOURNAL_PROMPT`): her volume is Master's to change. `run` never raises: a file that cannot be read or written is a logged error result ("Volume suaraku tidak bisa dibaca atau disimpan saat ini.").
- Guest mode (v1.36) leaves it open: a control, nothing private.

The media device's line (in `control_device` and the persona's device list) ends "; volume di sini volume Windows untuk semua suara PC, termasuk suaramu: untuk suaramu sendiri pakai voice_volume", so "kecilkan suaramu" goes to her tool. No persona line (decision): the tool's description and the device line carry the difference, and both personas stay as they are.

The phone: `reply_for_phone` synthesises for the phone and never plays through the PC's player, so phone replies are not affected; a change typed on the phone is heard on the PC.

CLI:
- `run` builds one `VoiceLevel(config.voice.state_path, config.voice.night)`, gives the brain `VoiceVolumeTools` (always, right after `GuestTools`) and the Assistant `VolumePlayer(Player(config.audio.output_device), voice_level.level_now)`. `chat` builds its own (the same file); with `--speak` its player is wrapped too.
- `waifu say [--volume N]`: plays at `level_now()` (the file read, never created), or at `N` (10–150; anything else is argparse's error "harus bilangan bulat 10 sampai 150, bukan '…'"), saving nothing. `_speak` takes any `AudioPlayer`.
- `check`, right after the guest mode line: `✓ Volume suaraku: 100%, mode bisik mati (22:00-06:00 paling keras 50%)`, `✓ Volume suaraku: 100%, mode bisik aktif (22:00-06:00 paling keras 50%)` (with `, sekarang 50%` while it holds), `! …; file volume yang rusak dipindah ke …`, or `✗ File volume suara <path> tidak bisa dibaca: …`.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Volume suaraku" after "Musik, volume, dan aplikasi PC" (examples, the difference from the Windows volume, whisper mode, the phone, `say --volume`, the check row), linked from that section, with a contents entry and checklist items; `config.example.yaml` documents `voice`; CHANGELOG v1.37.

Known limits: a clip is played at one level, so a long reply begun at 21:59 is said whole at the day level; a morning greeting set before `night.until` (`briefing.time` earlier than `until`) is whispered, and so is every reminder and schedule announcement inside the window, since everything she says on the PC goes through the one player (the README points to the phone notifications for a reminder that must wake Master); the Windows volume still scales everything, her voice included; at 150 a voice that already peaks near full scale is limited more than amplified; levels off the steps of 20 are reached only with `level`, and a step from one lands back on the grid.

Privacy: nothing new is sent; the file holds the level and the switch only.

Safety: tests never play audio: `VolumePlayer` wraps fakes, `tests/test_cli.py` replaces `Player`, state files live under tmp_path, and clocks are fake.

### ulangi (v1.38)

Master misses what she said (the AC was loud, he was not listening) and has to ask the brain again, which costs a turn and tokens and may not give the same words. A plain repeat request is answered on the PC by saying her last words again, at once, without the brain.

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, ulangi" (or "ulang lagi", "bisa diulang?", "tadi bilang apa?", "apa tadi?", "sekali lagi", "maaf, apa?", "hah?") right after she said something → she says the same text again, word for word, with the same emotion on the avatar, without asking the brain (instant, no tokens). Inside the follow-up window without her name too.
2. "Ulangi lagunya", "putar sekali lagi", "apa tadi lampunya sudah mati?", "tadi aku bilang apa?" → these hold other words, so they go to the brain as always.
3. Before she has said anything (or once guest mode has switched since) → "Belum ada yang bisa kuulangi, Master."
4. She asked "PC mau kumatikan sekarang, Master? Jawab iya kalau jadi." → "ulangi" says the question again; "iya" after it still turns the PC off. The repeat itself never answers the question.

Non-goals (v1.38): repeating on the phone (decision below); a replay button on the phone page; repeating something older than the last thing ("yang sebelumnya"); repeating slower or only a part ("ulangi pelan-pelan", "ulangi angkanya": other words, so the brain); repeating Master's own words; redoing an action ("sekali lagi" after "Sudah, Master." says those words again, it never calls a tool again); keeping it across a restart; a fuzzy name match (the words of her name, wake words, and owner call are left out only as configured, like `is_yes`); a Japanese phrase; `waifu chat` (no voice loop); a tool, a config key, a check line, a persona line, or a dependency (the strict budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters).

`repeat.py` — `is_repeat_request(text, names=()) -> bool`, pure:
- The words as `is_yes` splits them (NFKC, lowercase, runs of letters and digits), leaving out "master", the words of `names` (her name, wake words such as "hai cyrene", and what she calls the owner), and the softeners `dong`, `deh`, `ya`, `coba`, `tolong`. A question mark is allowed ("hah?").
- What is left must be exactly one of `REPEAT_PHRASES`: `ulangi`, `ulangi lagi`, `ulang lagi`, `diulang`, `diulangi`, `bisa diulang`, `bisa diulangi`, `tadi bilang apa`, `tadi kamu bilang apa`, `kamu tadi bilang apa`, `kamu bilang apa tadi`, `bilang apa tadi`, `apa tadi`, `sekali lagi`, `hah`; each of them after `maaf`; and `maaf apa`. Nothing else: no other word, no repeat of a word ("ulangi ulangi"), not empty, and not a bare "apa?".
- "Sekali lagi" with `coba` or `tolong` anywhere in the utterance ("coba sekali lagi", "tolong sekali lagi dong") is not a repeat request: it asks her to try again (after a failure her own lines say "Coba lagi sebentar lagi"), so it goes to the brain. The two soften only the other phrases ("coba ulangi").

What she remembers: `Assistant._last_said`, a frozen `_Said(text, emotion, marker)` in memory only, written only on `run`'s thread (so no lock), right before the words are spoken (`_say_remembered`), so a reply that was cut off (v1.11) or could not be voiced can still be asked for again, whole:
- Her reply to Master's voice turn (`handle_utterance`), the text as spoken (without its tag) and its emotion.
- What she says by herself in one go (`_say_announcements`): the reminders, the game's lead-in, and the announcements, joined as spoken, with the first one's emotion.
- Her spoken answer to an `ask_aloud` request (the morning greeting, the journal question), only when it is said (not when its `required_tool` failed).
- Never: the acknowledgement of a bare wake word, the avatar's reactions and the double-click's listen line (`speak_reaction`), the startup line (`speak` from the CLI), anything said on the phone, the "nothing to repeat" line, or the repeat itself. `speak` itself records nothing (it runs on other threads too).
- `marker` is guest mode's generation read before the turn that thought the words up (for the announcements, as they are said): `Assistant(..., guest_marker: Callable[[], object] | None = None)`, guest mode's `generation` in `run`. When the marker has moved since (guest mode turned on, off, or ended by itself, also by the phone or with nothing said on the PC), the memory is cleared and treated as empty: what she said before guests came is never repeated in front of them, nor what they heard after they left. So a reply whose turn switched guest mode (or during which it switched) is never repeated either: guest mode refuses per tool call, so in "berapa saldoku, terus nyalakan mode tamu" the balance was fetched before the switch, and the brains drop that turn with their new session. Without `guest_marker` the marker is None. Decision: kept across a new brain session (the morning greeting's `new_session`, the idle reset, the turn limit), since the words were said aloud on the PC either way.

The repeat, in `handle_utterance`: once the wake gate accepts an utterance whose command is not empty and `is_repeat_request(command, [name, *wake_words, owner_call])` holds (in the follow-up window the command still holds her name, which the gate keeps), she says `_last_said` again through `speak` like a reply (the avatar mouths it with its emotion, the follow-up window opens, her voice level of v1.37 applies, the level of now). No brain turn: no turn lock, no `begin_turn` of the confirmation or screen gates, no history, no tokens. INFO "Repeating what she said last" (never the text: reply text stays out of the INFO log). With nothing to repeat: `NOTHING_TO_REPEAT` "Belum ada yang bisa kuulangi, {owner}." with NEUTRAL, the follow-up window opened, INFO "Nothing to repeat". `handle_utterance` returns the text said.

The confirmation gate (v1.9): since the repeat is no turn, a question waiting for Master's yes stays waiting: Master's next real turn answers it, so "iya" after "ulangi" confirms (inside the follow-up window the repeat opened, without the name), and the repeat itself is never a yes or a no. The 60-second answer window still counts from the refusal: a repeat does not renew it.

Focus and game quiet (v1.30, v1.34): quiet holds only what she says by herself, so a repeat is answered as always. It leaves the queues alone: announcements held by quiet were never said, so they are never what she repeats, and they are not released by it; its follow-up window holds the patient announcements and the requests as a reply's does.

The phone: not intercepted (decision): the phone page already shows her words, so a typed or spoken "ulangi" on the phone is a brain turn like any message (it counts for the gates and goes into the history). Phone replies are never remembered as the PC's last words, and a repeat on the PC never says a phone reply.

CLI: `run` passes `guest_marker=guest_mode.generation` to the Assistant. Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Ulangi" after "Mode tamu" (the phrases, what she repeats and what not, the confirmation question, quiet, guest mode, the phone, "sekali lagi"), with a contents entry, an example in "Pemakaian sehari-hari", and checklist items; CHANGELOG v1.38.

Known limits: "sekali lagi" after an action repeats her words and does not redo it ("nyalakan lagi" or "putar lagi lagunya" go to the brain); a name Whisper misspells and the config does not list, inside the follow-up window ("Sairin, ulangi"), sends the request to the brain (a turn, safe); the brain's history never learns Master asked her to repeat; with speaker verification off any voice can ask her to repeat what she said since guest mode last switched; a long reply is repeated whole.

Privacy: nothing is stored or sent: the last words live in memory only, and the repeat never writes them to the log (it logs only "Repeating what she said last"). Her replies are not logged at INFO either, but the announcements and reminders were already logged as `Announcing: ...` when she first said them.

Safety: tests use fake brains, speech, players, and clocks; nothing is played.

### belajar bahasa jepang (v1.39)

Master already sprinkles Japanese into his days with her (her persona speaks it in kana and kanji) and wants to learn a little more, a word a day, without an app or a lesson: she gives him one beginner word each morning, quizzes him on the words he has met when he asks, and keeps track of what he knows. Everything is offline: the words ship with the package. Off by default.

User stories:

1. With `japanese.enabled: true`, her morning greeting ends with one "kata hari ini": the word in kana, then its Indonesian meaning ("Kata hari ini: たべる, artinya makan."). A new word each day, the same word all day, none repeated before every word of the list has come once.
2. "Kata Jepang hari ini apa?" → today's word with a short example sentence and its meaning, at any hour; also typed in `waifu chat` and in the phone chat.
3. "Kuis bahasa Jepang dong" → she asks the meaning of one learned word that is due ("たべる artinya apa?") and waits; Master answers "makan" → she judges the answer against the meaning, records it, praises him warmly (or gently gives the meaning when he was wrong), then asks the next due word, until none is due or Master stops. One word at a time, no lecture.
4. "Aku sudah belajar berapa kata?" → how many words he learned, how many he mastered, and how many are due today.
5. `waifu jepang` shows today's word and `waifu jepang progres` the progress, without the brain and without recording anything; `waifu check` tells the progress.

Non-goals (v1.39): romaji anywhere (her voice reads kana and kanji; the list holds none); grammar lessons, kanji writing, or listening drills; quizzes the other way round (Indonesian to Japanese) or multiple choice; levels beyond N5 or a word list Master edits (the list is part of the package; see known limits for a list changed by a later version); a quiz she starts herself (only when Master asks: never in the greeting, the weekly report, or the journal question); a push of its own (the greeting's push, `briefing.push`, carries the word as part of the greeting); a CLI to quiz, grade, or reset (`waifu jepang` only reads; deleting the file starts over); a new dependency; a strict tool (`japanese_lesson` requires nothing: the budget stays 18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters).

The word list `src/waifu/japanese_words.json`, package data (not in a folder named `data`, which `.gitignore` ignores anywhere in the tree, and apart from the owner's `data/`):
- `{"version": 1, "words": [...]}`: 300 common words and phrases at JLPT N5 level, compiled for this project from general knowledge (no copied list). Each `{"id": "w001", "kana": "たべる", "kanji": "食べる", "meaning": "makan", "example": "...", "example_meaning": "..."}`: `id` a `w` and three digits; `kana` the reading in hiragana or katakana only; `kanji` the usual written form with at least one kanji, or null when the word is usually written in kana; `meaning` and `example_meaning` Indonesian; `example` one short sentence in kana and kanji with Japanese punctuation only (。、！？), so no romaji, digits, spaces, or 々 (which the language split would hand to the Indonesian voice).
- A test validates it: exactly these keys, at least 300 words, unique ids, words, and examples, no empty field, the reading kana only, the kanji form and the example each one Japanese run of `split_by_language` (so the Japanese voice reads them whole), and the Indonesian fields Indonesian runs only and left whole by `clean_for_speech` (no parentheses, which her voice would drop).

`japanese.py`:
- `WORDS_FILE = Path(__file__).with_name("japanese_words.json")` (the package-file pattern of `voice/windows_voices.py`); `load_words(path=WORDS_FILE) -> tuple[Word, ...]` with `Word(id, kana, kanji, meaning, example, example_meaning)`; ValueError for a file that is not such a list.
- `word_of_the_day(words, day) -> Word`: the words in the order of the SHA-256 of their ids (a fixed shuffle), then the one at `(day - EPOCH).days % len(words)` (`EPOCH` 2026-01-01). Deterministic per date and kept in no file; consecutive days take consecutive words, so none repeats before all have come.
- Leitner boxes 1 to 5, `MASTERED_BOX = 5`. `review(progress, correct, today) -> WordProgress`: right moves the word to the next box (5 stays 5), wrong back to box 1; it is due `2 ** (box - 1)` days after `today` (1, 2, 4, 8, or 16; wrong: tomorrow). `next_quiz(progress, today) -> str | None`: of the words due (`due <= today`), the lowest box, then the earliest due, then the id; None when none is due.
- `JapaneseStore(path, *, now=wib_now)`, the file `data/japanese.json` (`japanese.path`): `{"version": 1, "words": {"w001": {"box": 1, "due": "2026-10-24", "learned": "2026-10-24"}}}`, exactly these keys, `box` a whole number 1 to 5 (not a boolean), the dates `YYYY-MM-DD`, `due` not before `learned`. The rules of `todo.py`: re-read at every call, one lock serialises the calls of a process, written atomically (`write_json_atomic`), never created by reading (missing: nothing learned), and a file that is not such a file is moved aside as `<name>.corrupt-YYYYmmdd-HHMMSS[-n]` (ERROR log) and read as empty; a failed move raises `OSError`. The clock (aware WIB, else ValueError) only names a moved file; the days are passed in.
- `progress() -> dict[str, WordProgress(box, due, learned)]`. `learn(word_id, today) -> bool`: a word not learned yet goes into box 1, due `today` (so Master can be quizzed on it the same day), learned `today`, and True; a word already learned changes nothing and writes nothing. `grade(word_id, correct, today) -> WordProgress | None`: `review` written; None, writing nothing, for a word never learned.
- `learn_word_of_the_day(words, store, day) -> tuple[Word, bool]`: today's word, learned once (the bool: whether it was new).

When a word counts as learned (decision): when she introduces it, the first time her greeting's `daily_briefing` or the tool's `today` gives it to her. `waifu jepang` only reads. A day nobody asks and no greeting is said leaves that day's word for the next round of the list.

`brain/japanese_tools.py` — `JapaneseTools(words, store, *, now=wib_now)`, a `ToolProvider` with `japanese_lesson {action?: enum[today, quiz, grade, progress], word_id?: string, correct?: boolean}`, non-strict (nothing required), the spec built once (byte-identical for the brain's lifetime). Its description is static English: Master's Japanese lessons from a built-in list of beginner words; `today` (the default) gives today's word, one a day, with its kana, its kanji form when it has one, the Indonesian meaning, and an example sentence, and counts it as learned; `quiz` gives the next learned word due for review, or `quiz` null when none is due (then `next_due_in_days`); `grade` records whether Master's answer was right, with the quiz's `word_id` and `correct`, which she judges herself from Master's answer against the meaning (a synonym or a close meaning counts; Master hears only the kana, so the meaning of a common word that sounds the same, はな heard as 鼻 "hidung", counts too, or she gives a hint and asks again); `progress` the counts. In a quiz: one word at a time, the word said in kana and its meaning asked, never the meaning first; wait for Master's answer, then grade, praise warmly when right or gently say the meaning when wrong, then the next word if he wants. Never grade without Master's answer, never quiz or grade in a turn she starts herself. Japanese only in kana or kanji, never romaji, and no parentheses (her voice drops them): the word once, then its Indonesian meaning. No grammar lecture. After an error result, say its message briefly and do not call again in this turn.
- Arguments (null counts as left out): `action` defaults to `today`; `word_id` (a string) and `correct` (a boolean) both with `grade`, and only with it. Not an object, an unknown key, or a bad value is an error result ("Argumen japanese_lesson tidak valid: ...").
- Results: `today` `{"word": {"word_id", "kana", "kanji", "meaning", "example", "example_meaning"}, "new"}`; `quiz` `{"quiz": {"word_id", "kana", "kanji", "meaning"}, "due_today"}` or `{"quiz": null, "learned", "next_due_in_days"}` (null with nothing learned); `grade` `{"word_id", "correct", "box", "mastered", "next_due_in_days", "due_today"}` (what is left due today); `progress` `{"learned", "mastered", "due_today", "total"}`. Words in the file but not in the list (a later list) are left out of the counts and the quiz.
- Errors: an id not in the list "Kata 'w999' tidak ada di daftar kata."; a word never learned "Kata itu belum dipelajari, jadi belum bisa dinilai."; a word not due yet (`due` after today) "Kata itu belum waktunya diulang, jadi tidak dinilai lagi.", writing nothing: every grade moves `due` past today, so a turn retried after an API error (its tool calls are not rolled back) never moves a word up twice, and only a word the quiz could offer is graded; a file that cannot be read or written (logged) "Catatan belajar bahasa Jepang tidak bisa dibaca atau disimpan saat ini.". `run` never raises.
- `begin_turn` refuses `quiz` and `grade` in her own turns (`BRIEFING_PROMPT`, `WEEKLY_REPORT_PROMPT`, `JOURNAL_PROMPT`): "Kuis bahasa Jepang hanya untuk permintaan Master, bukan untuk sapaan pagi." (or "laporan game mingguan", "pertanyaan jurnal malam"), so a grade comes only from Master's answer. `today` and `progress` stay open.
- Guest mode (v1.36) leaves it open (decision): beginner words and how many of them Master knows are low-stakes, like the focus timer's totals, and learning a word with friends around is fine; the tool reads none of his money, journal, tasks, games, or memory. Not in `PRIVATE_TOOLS`; the every-tool test turns the feature on so the tool is sorted.

The morning briefing:
- `BriefingTools(..., japanese: Callable[[date], Word] | None = None)`: with it, `daily_briefing` adds `word_of_the_day` `{"kana", "kanji", "meaning"}` (today's word for the briefing's date, counted as learned) in her morning greeting's turn only (`begin_turn` hears `BRIEFING_PROMPT`), in guest mode too; a failure is logged and leaves it null. Master's own turns ("cuaca hari ini gimana?" at any hour, a summary of the day) get no such field and count nothing learned: the word belongs to the greeting, and asked for, it comes from `japanese_lesson`. Without `japanese` the field is never there. The description gains one static sentence, the same with the feature on or off (prompt cache): when `word_of_the_day` is set, end with it in one short sentence, the word in kana and then its Indonesian meaning, without romaji or parentheses.
- `BRIEFING_PROMPT` adds "kata Jepang hari ini kalau ada" to what she sums up (no yes, look word, or play verb). `waifu briefing` passes none (it records nothing).

Persona: both personas get one identical "Belajar bahasa Jepang:" section between "Jurnal harian:" and "Mode tamu:": `japanese_lesson` with `today` for today's word, a quiz only when Master asks, one word at a time in kana with its meaning asked, waiting for his answer, judging it and calling `grade` (the meaning of a common homophone counts as right, or she gives a hint and asks again), warm praise or the meaning given gently, then the next word; `progress` for how many; never a grade without his answer; no grammar lecture, romaji, or parentheses.

Config `japanese`: `enabled` (false), `path` (`data/japanese.json`).

CLI:
- `run` and `chat`: with `japanese.enabled`, `_companion_brain` loads the list once and builds one `JapaneseStore`, giving the brain `JapaneseTools` (after the focus timer) and the briefing tool `japanese=` today's word learned once. Both commands write the file, as both write the journal.
- `waifu jepang [hari-ini|progres]` (default `hari-ini`): today's word (`Kata hari ini, Sabtu 24-10-2026: ちいさい (小さい), artinya kecil`, then `Contoh: ...` and `Artinya: ...`, then a note that she counts it as learned when she says it), or the progress (`Dipelajari: 12 dari 300 kata, 2 dikuasai`, `Perlu diulang hari ini: 3 kata`, or `Belum ada kata yang dipelajari (0 dari 300).`); it only reads (the file is never created); first `! Belajar bahasa Jepang mati (japanese.enabled: false).` while off; `✗ File belajar bahasa Jepang <path> tidak bisa dibaca: …` and exit 1.
- `check`, right after the journal line: `! Belajar bahasa Jepang mati`, `✓ Belajar bahasa Jepang: 12 dari 300 kata dipelajari, 2 dikuasai`, `! …; file belajar bahasa Jepang yang rusak dipindah ke … (lihat README, bagian Belajar bahasa Jepang)`, or `✗ File belajar bahasa Jepang <path> tidak bisa dibaca: …`.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Belajar bahasa Jepang" after "Ulangi" (turning it on, the greeting's word, today's word, the quiz and its schedule, the progress, the commands, the check row, guest mode, privacy) with a contents entry, mentions in the briefing section and "Mode tamu", checklist items, and a Privasi bullet; `config.example.yaml` documents `japanese`; CHANGELOG v1.39.

Known limits: the model judges Master's answer, so another model may be stricter or more lenient; Whisper writes what it hears, so a Japanese answer may be misheard (Indonesian meanings are the expected answer); a word list changed by a later version reshuffles the order, so a word already learned may come again as the word of the day (`new` false); `waifu run` and `waifu chat` both write the file and a write in one may lose a change the other made at the same moment (the journal's limit too); the greeting's day is the PC's local date and the tool's is WIB, the same on the owner's PC; in guest mode `GUEST_SECTION` keeps the greeting to the weather and the device steps, so she may leave the word out of it although the data holds it (asking still gives it).

Privacy: the file holds word ids, boxes, and dates only, on this PC; with the Claude backend the words and Master's answers go to Anthropic like any turn; nothing is pushed apart from the greeting's own `briefing.push`.

Safety: tests use tmp files and fake clocks; the word list is read from the package; nothing is spoken or pushed.

### pengingat istirahat (v1.40)

Master sits at this PC for hours (work, study, and games late into the night) and asked her to remind him to rest his eyes: after every hour of continuous activity at the PC she says one short, gentle line, then waits another hour, and a real break starts the count again. Off by default.

User stories:

1. With `breaks.enabled: true`, after `every_minutes` (60) of continuous keyboard or mouse activity at the PC she says once, like her other notices (between utterances, once the follow-up window has closed): "Sayang, sudah satu jam di depan layar. Istirahatkan matamu sebentar dan minum air, ya." Still active another `every_minutes` later: "Sayang, sudah dua jam di depan layar. ...", and so on.
2. A pause without keyboard or mouse input of at least `idle_reset_minutes` (5) is a break: the count starts again at his next input. A sleep of the PC is a break too.
3. Never during a focus block (v1.30): the focus timer has its own breaks, so a block resets the count, which starts afresh after it.
4. While game mode (v1.34) holds her announcements, the line waits like her other notices (and is dropped when still held after `idle_reset_minutes`); with `breaks.during_games: true` it is said while he plays, like his own reminders.
5. Optional active hours (`active_from`, `active_until`, across midnight allowed): outside them nothing is counted or said.
6. No push unless `breaks.push` and notifications are on. `waifu check` says whether it is on and how it is set.

Non-goals (v1.40): looking at the screen, the webcam, or which window is in front; counting gamepad (XInput) input, a running game, or a playing video as activity (see known limits); a break timer, or checking that he took the break; telling how long he has been at the PC when asked (no tool); a count that survives a restart of `waifu run`; the line in `chat`; a stronger second line; a CLI command; a new tool, tool parameter, persona line, or dependency (18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters stay).

Reading the activity (`input_idle.py`):
- `InputIdle(tick_ms, idle_ms)` (frozen): Windows' tick count (`GetTickCount64`, milliseconds since Windows started, counting through sleep) and the milliseconds since the last keyboard or mouse input of the session (`GetLastInputInfo`, user32).
- `read_input_idle(*, last_input=<GetLastInputInfo>, tick_count=<GetTickCount64>) -> InputIdle`: reads the last input first, then the tick count; `idle_ms = (tick - dwTime) mod 2**32` (`dwTime` is the 32-bit tick count, which wraps every 49.7 days of uptime), and a difference of `2**31` or more (a last input a moment newer than the tick count read after it) is 0. A failed `GetLastInputInfo` (0) is an `OSError` with Windows' last error. user32 and kernel32 come through ctypes with Microsoft's documented signatures (`LASTINPUTINFO {cbSize: UINT, dwTime: DWORD}`, `cbSize` filled in), loaded at every call, never at import. Read-only: nothing is hooked, injected, or sent, and which key or where the mouse is never reaches it.
- `tests/conftest.py` refuses `user32.GetLastInputInfo` like the process snapshot, recording it so that a test whose code caught the refusal still fails; `GetTickCount64` stays allowed (`pc_health` needs it).

The rule (`break_watch.py`, pure):
- `Activity(since, due, last_tick, last_wall)` (frozen; ticks in ms; `last_wall` the poll's local time). `follow_activity(activity, reading, now, *, every, reset) -> tuple[Activity | None, int | None]`, the activity after a poll (None: not counting) and the minutes to tell when a line is due:
  - `idle_ms >= reset`: a break, None.
  - Counting, and this poll comes more than `reset` after the last one by the tick count or by the wall clock (the PC slept, or the watcher could not look): the count starts afresh at this poll.
  - Not counting: it starts at this poll (`since` its tick, `due` = `since + every`).
  - The last input (`tick_ms - idle_ms`) at or after `due`: the line is due with `minutes = (due - since) / 60000` (a multiple of `every_minutes`), and the next is due `every` later, on the same grid, so the second says "dua jam", never "dua jam satu menit". The last input, not the poll, decides: a line due while he is away waits for his next input, and a break of `reset` resets it instead, so she never speaks to an empty chair.
- A break is always seen when it lasts at least `idle_reset_minutes` plus one poll (30 s); a shorter one only when a poll falls in it after `idle_reset_minutes`. Each poll is at most `reset` after the last (else the count starts afresh), and `reset` is shorter than `every`, so one poll gives at most one line.

`BreakWatcher(read_activity, config, *, announce, notifier, owner_call, title="Cyrene", in_focus_block=None, now=datetime.now, clock=time.monotonic, wait=threading.Event.wait)`:
- `poll_once() -> list[str]` (what she said): during a focus block (`in_focus_block()`) or outside the active hours (`now`, the PC's local time, `voice_level.in_window`), nothing is read and the count resets; else it reads the activity and follows it. A failed read resets the count too: the first of a run is logged at WARNING (with its traceback unless it is an `OSError`), the rest at DEBUG, the recovery at INFO. A due line, `"{owner}, sudah {spoken_duration(minutes)} di depan layar. Istirahatkan matamu sebentar dan minum air, ya."` (the owner word at sentence start; `focus_timer.spoken_duration`: "satu jam", "satu jam tiga puluh menit"), is announced with emotion `relaxed` and `expires_at` = `now` + `idle_reset_minutes` (one still unsaid then is out of date: held by a long talk, a game, or a block that began after it), then pushed with the `coffee` tag when the watcher has a notifier; a failure of either is logged, never raised. The count and the line are logged at DEBUG only: when he is at the PC is his own (`run` logs `Announcing: ...` as it says the line).
- `start()`: a daemon thread "break-watcher" polling at once, then every `POLL_S` (30 s) from the start of the previous poll; a second `start()` is a no-op. `stop()` wakes and joins it (5 s). The count lives in memory only: a restart starts from zero, and activity before the first poll is unknown.

Config `breaks`: `enabled` (false), `every_minutes` (60, 20–180), `idle_reset_minutes` (5, 2–15, so always shorter than `every_minutes`), `during_games` (false), `push` (false), `active_from` and `active_until` (none: all day; both or neither, not equal, clock times without a time zone, across midnight when `active_until` comes first, as `08:00`–`02:00`).

CLI:
- `run` with `breaks.enabled` starts the watcher inside the watchers' block, right after the focus session, with `read_input_idle`, the focus timer's `in_block` (none without the focus companion), and the notifier only with `breaks.push` and notifications on; stopped on exit. Its announce is the rain alert's (`_announcer(..., patient=True, pushed=...)`: held while game mode holds, left unsaid while a game runs in `phone` mode when it was pushed), but with `breaks.during_games`, while game mode holds her announcements, the line goes to `assistant.remind` (said while he plays, also in an open follow-up window). The watcher's own focus check keeps `remind` out of focus blocks. `chat` never starts it. Guest mode changes nothing: the line holds nothing private.
- `check`, right after the voice volume line: `! Pengingat istirahat mati`, or `✓ Pengingat istirahat: tiap 60 menit di depan layar, jeda 5 menit dihitung istirahat`, with `, jam 08:00-02:00` for the active hours, `, juga saat main` with `during_games`, and `, juga ke HP` with `push` and notifications on. From the config only: nothing is read.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Pengingat istirahat" after "Belajar bahasa Jepang" (turning it on, the line, what counts as activity and as a break, focus blocks, game mode, the active hours, the check row, known limits, privacy) with a contents entry, mentions in "Teman fokus", "Mode game", and "Mode tamu", checklist items, and a Privasi bullet; `config.example.yaml` documents `breaks`; CHANGELOG v1.40.

Decisions: a focus block resets the count (focus has its own breaks, and a count carried through a session would nag right after it); outside the active hours the count resets, so it starts at `active_from`; a line expires `idle_reset_minutes` after the poll that gives it (not after the time it fell due: a pause shorter than a break across that time can delay the poll by up to `idle_reset_minutes` plus one poll); `during_games` uses `remind` only while game mode holds (with `normal`, or no game mode, nothing holds the plain announcement).

Known limits: gamepad (XInput) input never reaches `GetLastInputInfo`, so playing with a controller, like watching a video without touching anything, looks idle and resets the count; any input counts, also Cyrene's own media keys (`SendInput`), a mouse jiggler, remote play (Parsec, Steam Remote Play), or a bumped mouse; she counts from `waifu run`'s first poll; a break barely longer than `idle_reset_minutes` can fall between two polls; a line held longer than `idle_reset_minutes` (a long talk, a game holding it) is dropped, and the next comes `every_minutes` later.

Privacy: nothing is stored: the count lives in memory, only the time since the last input is read, and the count and the line are logged at DEBUG; with `breaks.push` and notifications on, the line goes to ntfy.sh.

Safety: tests never read the real input (refused in `tests/conftest.py`), sleep, speak, or push; every clock and reader is a fake; `test_cli.py` keeps a fake `BreakWatcher`.

### waktu sholat (v1.41)

Master asked her to know the five daily prayer times where he lives and, when he wants, to tell him as each one comes in. Off by default, and nothing about prayer exists while it is off: no tool, no persona line, no reminder, and nothing in her greeting. The wording is neutral and respectful; she tells the time and never comments on whether or how Master prays.

User stories:

1. With `prayer.enabled: true`, "jadwal sholat hari ini?", "Maghrib jam berapa?", or "jadwal sholat besok" gets the times from her `prayer_times` tool: imsak, subuh, terbit, dzuhur, ashar, maghrib, and isya for the place, computed on this PC with the Kemenag RI method.
2. At the time of each prayer in `prayer.remind` (all five by default) she says once, through Master's own reminder queue: "Sayang, sudah masuk waktu Dzuhur." With `minutes_before` above 0 she says it that long before instead: "Sayang, sepuluh menit lagi masuk waktu Dzuhur." He asked for these, so they are said during a focus block and while game mode holds her announcements too, like the reminders he sets himself. Never twice for the same prayer and day, also across a restart of `waifu run` that day.
3. `waifu sholat [YYYY-MM-DD]` prints the day's times; `waifu check` says whether it is on, for which place, and how the reminders are set.
4. No push unless `prayer.push` and notifications are on.

Non-goals (v1.41): the adzan or any sound of its own, the qibla, the Hijri calendar, Ramadan or fasting features, anything for Jumat, other methods (MWL, ISNA, the Hanafi Ashar) or angles, an elevation correction, a time zone other than WIB, fetching a schedule online, checking or asking whether Master prayed, prayer in the morning greeting, the weekly report, or the journal question, the reminders in `chat`, a persona line, a strict tool, or a dependency (18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters stay).

The math (`prayer_times.py`, pure, offline):
- Times are WIB (UTC+7, fixed), whose meridian is 105°E. The sun comes from the USNO low-precision formulas, good to about a minute of arc from 1950 to 2050: with `D` the days since J2000.0 (Julian day minus 2451545), mean anomaly `g = 357.529 + 0.98560028 D`, mean longitude `q = 280.459 + 0.98564736 D`, ecliptic longitude `L = q + 1.915 sin g + 0.020 sin 2g`, obliquity `ε = 23.439 - 0.00000036 D`; declination `δ = asin(sin ε sin L)`, right ascension `RA = atan2(cos ε sin L, cos L)`, and the equation of time `q/15 - RA` (hours, into -12..12).
- `solar_noon(day, longitude) -> float` (hours after the day's midnight, WIB): `12 + (105 - longitude)/15 - EoT`. `sun_at_altitude(day, latitude, longitude, altitude, *, rising) -> float | None`: the hour angle from `cos H = (sin a - sin φ sin δ)/(cos φ cos δ)` before (`rising`) or after noon, None when the right side is outside -1..1 (the sun never reaches that altitude that day); `sun_altitude(day, hours, latitude, longitude) -> float`, the altitude of the sun's centre then (no refraction). Each time is found three times over, the sun taken at the time found before (first at 06:00 or 18:00, or noon), so the sun is at the asked altitude at the time found.
- Kemenag RI: Subuh at the sun's altitude -20°, terbit and Maghrib at -0.8333° (refraction and the sun's half width; no elevation correction), Dzuhur at the solar noon, Ashar when a shadow is its object's length plus the noon shadow (Syafi'i: `a = acot(1 + tan|φ - δ|)`, none when `|φ - δ|` reaches 90°, the sun below the horizon at noon), and Isya at -18°.
- `prayer_times(day, latitude, longitude, *, ihtiyath_minutes=2) -> PrayerTimes`: the ihtiyath (a safety margin) is added to Subuh, Dzuhur, Ashar, Maghrib, and Isya, each then rounded up to the next whole minute; terbit, the end of Subuh's time, is the sunrise minus the ihtiyath rounded down, so both err on the safe side; imsak is Subuh minus 10 minutes. `PrayerTimes(day, imsak, subuh, terbit, dzuhur, ashar, maghrib, isya)` holds aware WIB datetimes, None where the sun does not reach the angle that day (far from the equator: no Subuh around the summer solstice from about 46.6° on and no Isya from about 48.6° on, no terbit, Ashar, or Maghrib in a polar night); Dzuhur always has a time. A time may fall on the next day (a late Isya far north).
- `PRAYERS = ("subuh", "dzuhur", "ashar", "maghrib", "isya")` and `PRAYER_LABELS`, their names as written ("Subuh", "Dzuhur", "Ashar", "Maghrib", "Isya").

The place (`prayer.py`):
- `WeatherClient.location() -> Location` (the geocoded place, made public from `_Location`): coordinates without a request, else the city geocoded within `TIME_LIMIT_S` and kept for the client's life; a `WeatherError` when it fails.
- `PrayerPlace(locate, *, named=True)`: `locate()` gives the `Location` or a `PrayerError` whose Indonesian message is safe to say; no `locate` (no place configured): "Lokasi waktu sholat belum diatur. Isi briefing.city, atau prayer.latitude dan prayer.longitude, di config.yaml."; a `WeatherError` becomes a `PrayerError` with its message. A lock keeps the tool and the watcher from looking it up at the same moment.
- Which place: `prayer.latitude`/`longitude`, else `briefing.latitude`/`longitude`, else `briefing.city` geocoded by Open-Meteo once per run (one client, shared by the tool and the watcher; a lookup that failed is tried again at the next use), else none. Coordinates are named by `briefing.city` when it is set, as the weather names them; without it their name is `<lat>, <lon>` and the place is not `named`, so the tool never tells them (they may be the house: she never says them, and they never reach the model), while `waifu sholat` prints them. Nothing is looked up at startup: the first use is the watcher's first poll or the tool.

The tool (`brain/prayer_tools.py`):
- `prayer_times {date?}` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, today in WIB when left out or null; within 366 days of today), non-strict, its spec static English. The result: `{"place" (null when the place is not `named`), "date", "day" (Indonesian weekday), "timezone": "WIB", "imsak", "subuh", "terbit", "dzuhur", "ashar", "maghrib", "isya" ("HH:MM", null where it cannot be computed there that day), "method": "Kemenag RI", "ihtiyath_minutes"}`. A place that cannot be found is an error with the `PrayerError` message.
- The description: the times only from this tool, never from memory; a null `place` means she names no place; only the times asked for, and for the schedule the five prayers, imsak or terbit only when Master asks for them (imsak and terbit are for display); said briefly and respectfully; never urging Master to pray or commenting on it; a null time means the sun does not reach its angle there that day.
- In her own turns (the morning greeting, the weekly game report, the nightly journal question) it refuses: "Waktu sholat hanya untuk permintaan Master, bukan untuk ...". Guest mode leaves it open: the times and the city hold nothing private (the weather tells the city too).

The reminders (`prayer_watch.py`):
- `PrayerWatcher(place, config, *, remind, notifier, owner_call, title="Cyrene", now=wib_now, clock=time.monotonic, wait=threading.Event.wait)`; `remind(text, emotion)` is `Assistant.remind`.
- `poll_once() -> list[str]` (what she said): looks the place up (a failure is never said; the first of a run is logged at WARNING, the rest at DEBUG, the recovery at INFO, and after a failure the place is asked again only `RETRY_S`, 15 minutes, later), computes today's times (WIB), and finds the prayers of `remind` that are due: from `minutes_before` before the prayer's time until `LATE` (30 minutes) after it, so a PC woken or a `waifu run` started later than that says nothing for it. The line at or after the time: `"{owner}, sudah masuk waktu {Label}."`; before it: `"{owner}, {spoken_duration(m)} lagi masuk waktu {Label}."` with `m` the whole minutes left, rounded up (the owner word at sentence start). Each due prayer not yet told is marked told, then said with emotion `neutral` and pushed with the `mosque` tag when the watcher has a notifier; a failure of either is logged, never raised.
- Told prayers are kept in memory and in `prayer.state_path` (`data/prayer.json`): `{"version": 1, "date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "told": [...]}`, the day's told prayers in their order. The file is read only when a prayer is due and not told in memory, written atomically before she says it, and never created by reading; a date other than today counts as nothing told. A file that is not such a state (bad JSON or UTF-8, other keys, version, date, or names, a name twice) is moved aside as `<name>.corrupt-YYYYmmdd-HHMMSS[-n]` with an ERROR log and counts as nothing told. A file that cannot be read or moved is logged at WARNING and left alone (not written that day); one that cannot be written is logged at WARNING; either way she says the prayer and remembers it in memory, so only a restart within its window could say it again.
- `start()`: a daemon thread "prayer-watcher" polling at once, then every `POLL_S` (30 s) from the start of the previous poll; a second `start()` is a no-op. `stop()` wakes and joins it (5 s).

Config `prayer`: `enabled` (false), `latitude` and `longitude` (none; both or neither, -90..90 and -180..180), `ihtiyath_minutes` (2, 0-5), `remind` (all five; each listed once; `[]` gives the times on request only), `minutes_before` (0, 0-30), `push` (false), `state_path` (`data/prayer.json`).

CLI:
- `run` with `prayer.enabled` builds one `PrayerPlace` (nothing looked up), gives it to the brain's tool and, with a non-empty `remind`, to the watcher, started together with the scheduler (Master's own reminders, one item of the watchers' block, which is at Python's limit of nested blocks) with `assistant.remind` and the notifier only with `prayer.push` and notifications on; stopped on exit. `remind` is never held by quiet or diverted by game mode's `phone` mode. `chat` offers the tool (with its own place) and starts no watcher. Guest mode changes nothing: the line holds nothing private.
- `waifu sholat [YYYY-MM-DD]` (today in WIB by default): `Waktu sholat di <place>, <Hari DD-MM-YYYY> (WIB):`, a row per time (`  Subuh    04:10`, `--` where it cannot be computed, with a closing note), then `Metode Kemenag RI: Subuh -20°, Isya -18°, Ashar bayangan 1x, ihtiyath 2 menit.`; first `! Waktu sholat mati (prayer.enabled: false).` while off (it works all the same); `✗ <message>` and exit 1 without a place or when the lookup fails. The date must be `YYYY-MM-DD`.
- `check`, right after the break reminder line: `! Waktu sholat mati`, `! Waktu sholat: lokasi belum diatur (isi briefing.city, atau prayer.latitude dan prayer.longitude)`, or `✓ Waktu sholat: untuk <place>, ihtiyath 2 menit, pengingat subuh, dzuhur, ashar, maghrib, isya` (`<place>` as the weather's check lines write it: `<city>`, `<lat>, <lon>`, or `<city> (<lat>, <lon>)`) with ` 10 menit sebelumnya` for `minutes_before`, `, tanpa pengingat` for an empty `remind`, and `, juga ke HP` with `push` and notifications on (and reminders); `! …; file pengingat sholat yang rusak dipindah ke … (lihat README, bagian Waktu sholat)` after a moved state file. From the config only: nothing is looked up.
- Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Waktu sholat" after "Pengingat istirahat" (turning it on, the place, asking, the reminders, `waifu sholat`, the check row, the method and its limits, guest mode, privacy) with a contents entry, mentions in "Teman fokus", "Mode game", and "Mode tamu", checklist items, and a Privasi bullet; `config.example.yaml` documents `prayer`; CHANGELOG v1.41.

Decisions: computed offline (no account, no schedule site, works without internet once the place is known); the reminders use Master's reminder queue because he turned them on, so a focus block or a game never holds them; the told prayers survive a restart in a state file, since a restart within a prayer's 30-minute window must not say it twice; a lateness window of 30 minutes (shorter than the gap between any two prayers in Indonesia), after which a missed reminder is not said; terbit minus the ihtiyath, rounded down, like Kemenag's tables; one line per prayer, before its time with `minutes_before` or at it; no persona line, so nothing about prayer reaches the prompt while it is off; the tool open to guests.

Known limits: a time can differ by a minute or two from Kemenag's published table (their coordinates for a city, their elevation correction, and their ephemeris); a geocoded city is its centre, not the house (coordinates are more exact); a place outside WIB (WITA, WIT, abroad) still gets WIB times; far from the equator a prayer may have no time and gets no reminder that day; the watcher looks every 30 s, so a line comes up to 30 s after its time, and later when she is talking; `chat` has no reminders.

Privacy: the times are computed on this PC; only the city name goes to Open-Meteo, as for the weather; the state file holds the date and which reminders she said, never whether Master prayed; with the Claude backend the tool's result (the city's name when there is one, never coordinates, and the times) goes to Anthropic like any turn; with `prayer.push` and notifications on, the line goes to ntfy.sh; `run` logs each line as `Announcing: ...` like all she says.

Safety: tests use tmp files, fake clocks, and a fake place; geocoding is faked (no network); nothing is spoken or pushed; `test_cli.py` keeps a fake `PrayerWatcher`.

### tombol cepat di PC (v1.42)

At the PC Master has the same one-tap jobs as on the phone: the lamp off, the AC to 24, "mode tidur", without saying it to her or typing `waifu device`. The phone's quick buttons (v1.35) come to the PC as a page in its own browser, served on 127.0.0.1 by `waifu tombol`.

User stories:

1. `waifu tombol` opens a page "Tombol cepat" in the default browser with the same groups and buttons as the phone (a group per device, then "Rutinitas"). A click runs the button at once and a toast tells the result, as on the phone. The console shows the page's address and stays open; Ctrl+C closes the page ("Selesai.").
2. The PC is never a button here either, and nothing but Master's own page can press one: not another website open in the same browser, not a page on another port of 127.0.0.1, not another computer.
3. `waifu tombol --dry-run` presses the simulated devices; `waifu check` says how many buttons the page has.

Non-goals (v1.42): the page inside `waifu run` (decision below); a bookmarkable address (a new port and token every time); buttons in the avatar window or the system tray; a button for the PC or a `powers_pc` plug; live device state; settings of its own (the page shows the phone's buttons); the brain or guest mode knowing of a press; a brain tool (18 of 20 strict tools and 17 of 24 optional parameters stay); a dependency.

The buttons: `QuickActions` as in v1.35, with `chosen=config.phone.quick_actions` (one list for the phone and the PC, read also with `phone.enabled: false`) and the new keyword `where` (default "tombol cepat HP"), the place `pc_power_refusal`'s sentence names: "tombol cepat PC" here. `ACTION_MAX_BYTES`, `ACTION_RATE_LIMIT`, `ACTION_RATE_WINDOW_S`, `ACTION_UNKNOWN_TEXT`, `ACTION_LIMIT_TEXT`, `ACTION_BUSY_TEXT`, and `group_json(group)` (a group as GET /actions lists it) move to `phone/actions.py` for both servers. The phone server's request handling that is not about the phone (a body read within a limit, strict JSON fields, refusals that read or drain the unread body first, JSON and file replies, `PROTECTING_HEADERS` on every response) becomes `JsonRequestHandler` in `phone/server.py`, which the phone's handler and the PC page's handler extend. Nothing the phone does changes.

Server (`phone/desk.py`): `DeskServer(actions, *, owner_call, web_root=WEB_ROOT, host=LOOPBACK, port=0, clock=time.monotonic)`, a `ThreadingHTTPServer` with daemon threads (no SO_REUSEADDR on Windows), `start()`, `stop()` (safe to repeat), and a context manager like the phone server's; a `ValueError` for a host that is not IPv4 loopback.
- A token of `secrets.token_urlsafe(24)` per server, in the path: `url` is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/t/<token>/`. Every route is under that prefix, the token compared with `hmac.compare_digest`; any other path (no prefix, a wrong token) is a 404 `{"error": "Tidak ditemukan."}` for every method before anything else of the request is looked at.
- Every request: HTTP/0.9 is dropped; the Host must be exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>` (DNS rebinding), else 403 "Tombol cepat di PC hanya melayani alamat 127.0.0.1 atau localhost." before any route. Every response, refusals included, carries `PROTECTING_HEADERS` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`.
- Under the prefix: GET of the prefix itself is `web/tombol/index.html` (no-store); GET `tombol/<file>` a file of `web/tombol/`; GET `phone/actions.js` the phone page's pure helpers (files as the phone server sends its own); GET `actions` is `{"groups": [group_json(...)]}`, never cached; POST `action` runs a button. GET `action` is 405 (`Allow: POST`), a POST elsewhere under the prefix 405 (`Allow: GET`), and so are HEAD, PUT, DELETE, PATCH, OPTIONS, CONNECT, and TRACE; any other path under the prefix is a 404. No response ever has an `Access-Control-*` header, so another origin's JSON request fails its preflight (OPTIONS, 405).
- POST `action {"id"}`: exactly one `Origin` of the page's own, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>` or `http://localhost:<port>` (403 `FOREIGN_ORIGIN_TEXT` for none, `null`, another port, `https://`, two); then at most `ACTION_RATE_LIMIT` (10) presses in any `ACTION_RATE_WINDOW_S` (60 s) for the page, every tab together, counted before the body is read (429 with `Retry-After: 60`); the body as the phone's (at most 1 KB, strict JSON with only `id`: 411, 413, 400, or 415); an id shaped as `ACTION_ID` (400); an id of the buttons of that moment (404 `ACTION_UNKNOWN_TEXT`); one press at a time (409 `ACTION_BUSY_TEXT`, without `Retry-After`); then 200 `{"ok", "message"}`, a device's failure included; anything raised is a 500 with `FAILED_TEXT` (`owner_call`), logged with its traceback.
- Logs: INFO `PC page ran quick action dev.lampu_kamar.on: ok` (or `failed`), the message at DEBUG; refusals at INFO, the action id only once it is shaped as one; the request line at DEBUG with the token replaced by `<token>`. The token itself is never logged.

Page (`avatar/web/tombol/`, every URL relative, so it keeps the token's prefix):
- `index.html` (lang `id`): a Content-Security-Policy meta before any script, `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'` (`frame-ancestors 'none'` comes from the server); no inline script or style; `tombol/tombol.css` and the module `tombol/tombol.js`, and no icon (`data:,`, which no route would serve); `<h1>Tombol cepat</h1>`, `#message` (`role="alert"`, "Memuat tombol…"), `<section id="groups" aria-label="Tombol cepat" hidden>`, and `#notice` (`role="status"`, hidden), a toast fixed over the bottom of the window, so it never moves the buttons. The modules it loads are only routes the server serves (`tombol/tombol.js`, `tombol/desk.js`, `phone/actions.js`), which a test checks.
- `desk.js` (pure, under `node --test`): `deskProblem(route, status)` (404 `action_unknown` for `action` and `closed` for `actions`, since another `waifu tombol` on the same port has another token; 429 `action_limit`, 409 `action_busy`, 403 `forbidden`, 400, 411, 413, and 415 `bad_request`, anything else `server`); `requestDesk(fetchFn, route, {body, contentType, timeoutMs})`, a GET or JSON POST with `mode: "cors"` and `referrerPolicy: "same-origin"` (with the server's `no-referrer`, a POST in any other mode carries `Origin: null` under the Fetch spec, as in Firefox, and gets a 403; the relative URL keeps it same-origin), `credentials: "omit"`, and `cache: "no-store"`, resolving to `{data}` or `{problem}` (`deskProblem`, `closed` when the server does not answer, `timeout` after 60 s, `bad_reply` for an answer that is not JSON), never rejecting; `deskText(kind)`, the page's Indonesian texts (`closed`: "Tombol cepat di PC sudah ditutup. Jalankan waifu tombol lagi, lalu pakai halaman yang dibukanya."; the refusals of a press as on the phone), an unknown kind as `server`.
- `tombol.js`: reads `actions` (`parseActionGroups` of `../phone/actions.js`); a problem, or an answer it cannot read, shows its text in `#message`, no buttons "Belum ada tombol cepat di PC."; otherwise `#message` is hidden and the groups show as on the phone (an `<h2>` and a `role="group"` labelled by it per group, `type="button"` buttons, made with `createElement`), always open. A click posts `actionRequest(id)` to `action`; until the answer that button is `aria-busy` and every button `aria-disabled` (not `disabled`, so the focus stays), and clicks are ignored; the answer's message (`actionOutcome`), or the problem's text, shows in `#notice` for 6 s.
- `tombol.css`: the phone page's colours (and a dark variant for `prefers-color-scheme: dark`); the groups as cards filling a desktop window, buttons at least 44 px high in a grid of `minmax(120px, 1fr)`, the `aria-busy` and `aria-disabled` styles of the phone; a narrow window gets one column.

CLI:
- `waifu tombol` (shared options `--config`, `--dry-run`): builds the registry (the mocks with `--dry-run`) and the session schedule as `waifu routine run` does (a delayed step goes into the schedule for `waifu run`; a dry run's into a temporary one), then `QuickActions(registry, config.routines, schedule=..., chosen=config.phone.quick_actions, where="tombol cepat PC")`. Without buttons: `! Belum ada tombol cepat: tambahkan lampu, colokan, perangkat IR, atau rutinitas di config.yaml, atau periksa phone.quick_actions.`, exit 1, nothing served or opened. Otherwise a `DeskServer` on 127.0.0.1 port 0 and `open_in_browser(server.url)` (`os.startfile`, looked up at each call, injected in tests); a browser that cannot be opened (OSError) gives `! Browser tidak bisa dibuka otomatis (<error>). Buka alamat di bawah ini di browser.` and the page is served all the same. Then `Tombol cepat di PC: <url>. Tekan Ctrl+C untuk menutup.` (with ` (perangkat simulasi)` before the period on a dry run). When a routine button has a step with `after_minutes`, the next line says who runs it, since this page runs without `waifu run`: `DRY_RUN_DELAYED_STEPS` on a dry run; `DELAYED_STEPS_IN_RUN` (as `waifu routine run` prints) while the run lock is held; else `! 'waifu run' tidak sedang berjalan: langkah rutinitas yang ditunda tetap masuk jadwal, tapi hanya dijalankan kalau 'waifu run' menyala pada jamnya. Jalankan 'waifu run' dan biarkan menyala sampai saat itu, atau batalkan dengan 'waifu schedule cancel <id>'.` Then a wait of `sleep(AVATAR_WAIT_S)` at a time, and on Ctrl+C `Selesai.`, exit 0, the server stopped.
- `waifu check`, right after the routine lines: `✓ Tombol cepat di PC: <n> tombol, buka dengan 'waifu tombol'`, or `! Tombol cepat di PC: belum ada tombol (tambahkan lampu, colokan, perangkat IR, atau rutinitas di config.yaml, atau periksa phone.quick_actions)`; from the real registry, which connects to nothing.

Guest mode (v1.36) lives in `waifu run`; the page is another process, so its buttons keep working, as the phone's do (device control, nothing private).

Docs: README (Indonesian) section "Tombol cepat di PC" after "Waktu sholat" (opening it, what shows, the console, the limits, who can press, privacy) with a contents entry, a pointer from "Tombol cepat di HP", a mention in "Mode tamu", a checklist item, and a Privasi bullet; CHANGELOG v1.42.

Decisions:
- `waifu tombol` serves its own page in the foreground instead of a route of `waifu run`'s avatar server, which runs only with `avatar.enabled` and a model: it works with or without the avatar and `waifu run`, needs no new port or setting, and its token is made in the process that opens the browser, never handed over in a file. The cost: the console stays open while the page is used, and its `QuickActions` is another than the phone's, so a press on the PC and one on the phone may run at the same moment, as `waifu device` beside `waifu run` already may (Tuya opens a connection per command; Broadlink authenticates again).
- The token is in the path, not in a cookie: browsers share cookies across the ports of 127.0.0.1 and count every port as one site, so any local page could plant or shadow one; a path is known only to who was given it, survives a reload, and needs no script. Not in a fragment either, which would need the page's script and storage to survive a reload.
- Why the checks stop a website in the same browser (CSRF), the real threat: its POST carries its own `Origin` (403), it cannot send JSON across origins without a preflight that gets a 405 and no CORS header, and it cannot read the token (no CORS, CORP same-origin, no `Referer`); a name re-pointed at 127.0.0.1 (DNS rebinding) carries its own Host (403) and still does not know the token. Other programs of the same Windows user are out of scope (they can run `waifu device`), and the LAN never reaches 127.0.0.1.
- One rate limit for the page, not per tab: tabs are not devices with an identity, and ten presses a minute is the phone's bar.
- The phone's buttons and `phone.quick_actions`, not a second list: one place to pick them.
- A browser that cannot be opened keeps the page served, since the printed address is then the way in.

Known limits: the page lives as long as the console; a new address each time, so a bookmark or an old tab is dead (an old tab says the page is closed); `waifu run` and her conversation do not know of a press (ask her again for the live state, as on the phone); the phone's and the PC's buttons may run at the same moment.

Privacy: nothing is stored or sent off the PC; the token is in the address the browser opens (its history keeps it, dead once the server stops) and in the console, never in a log; logs hold the action ids and whether they worked; the brain never sees a press.

Safety: tests use `MockDevice` registries, 127.0.0.1 port 0, fake clocks, and tmp schedules; the conftest guard refuses `os.startfile`, and `test_cli.py` swaps the opener for a fake; the PC and its plug are never listed, refused by `find`, refused again at run time, and refused by the config.

### jadwal mingguan, bulanan, tahunan (v1.43)

Master wants birthdays, bills, and weekly chores in her schedule: "ingatkan ulang tahun Ibu tiap 5 Mei", "tiap tanggal 20 ingatkan bayar listrik", "tiap Senin jam 7 ingatkan buang sampah". Until now an entry repeated only daily, on weekdays, or on weekends.

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, ingatkan ulang tahun Ibu tiap 5 Mei." → a yearly reminder on 5 May at 08:00 (or the time Master says). That morning she says "Master, hari ini ulang tahun Ibu!" and pushes it like any reminder, and the entry moves to 5 May next year. Asked after 5 May, it starts next year.
2. "Cyrene, tiap tanggal 20 ingatkan bayar listrik." → a monthly reminder on the 20th at 08:00 (or the time Master says). One of the 31st fires on the last day of a month without a 31st (30 November, 28 or 29 February) and on the 31st again after it; 29 February fires on 28 February in a common year.
3. "Cyrene, tiap Senin jam 7 ingatkan buang sampah." → a weekly reminder on the weekday of its first time. Device commands and routines repeat the same way ("matikan lampu teras tiap Minggu jam 6 pagi").
4. "Ulang tahun siapa bulan ini?" / "Jadwal bulan ini apa?" → she reads `list_schedules` and picks the entries herself.
5. `waifu schedule list` shows `tiap Senin`, `tiap tanggal 20`, `tiap 5 Mei`.
6. A monthly or yearly reminder missed while the PC or `waifu run` was off is told when she is back, like a one-shot reminder, not only pushed: a birthday would otherwise come back only next year.

Non-goals (v1.43): other rules ("Senin pertama tiap bulan", "tiap 2 minggu", "hari kerja terakhir"), an end date or a count, a yearly entry that starts in a later year ("mulai tahun depan"), the Hijri or Chinese calendar (Lebaran, Imlek), a reminder some days before ("3 hari sebelum ulang tahun Ibu": a second yearly entry does it), a birthday list or contacts of their own, a filter on `list_schedules`, a new tool or tool parameter (18 of 20 strict tools, 17 of 24 optional parameters, and 0 of 16 unions stay; only the `repeat` enum grows), a `FILE_VERSION` bump, a dependency.

`schedule.py`:
- `Repeat` and `REPEATS` gain `"weekly"`, `"monthly"`, and `"yearly"`, after the four older ones. The refusal of an unknown repeat lists `REPEATS`: "Pengulangannya harus salah satu dari: none, daily, weekdays, weekends, weekly, monthly, yearly."
- `Entry.day: int | None = None`, the last field: the day of the month (1-31) a monthly or yearly entry repeats on; None for every other repeat. `at` alone cannot tell it once clamped: an entry of the 31st at 30 November must go back to the 31st in December.
- `next_occurrence(at, repeat, after, *, day=None) -> datetime | None` (`day` defaults to `at.day`; the other repeats ignore it), still a pure function of naive local times at `at`'s clock time, later than `after`: `weekly`, the first day on `at`'s weekday; `monthly`, the day `min(day, days in the month)` of `after`'s month, or of the next month when that is not later than `after`; `yearly`, `at`'s month on `min(day, days in that month)` of `after`'s year, or of the next year when that is not later than `after` (29 February is 28 February in a common year and 29 February again in a leap year). Whole calendar days, so no daylight saving change ever shifts one (WIB has none).
- `add(at, repeat, action)`: a `weekly`, `monthly`, or `yearly` entry also accepts an `at` before the current minute, which starts at its first occurrence from the current minute on, with the same weekday or day: "tiap tanggal 20" passed as 2026-10-20 08:00 on 24 October starts on 20 November. A yearly entry ignores the year of `at` and always starts at its first occurrence from the current minute on, so 2027-05-05 and 2026-05-05 both give 5 May 2027 in October 2026, and 29 February 2028 gives 28 February 2027 instead of "terlalu jauh". A weekly or monthly `at` more than 366 days ahead is still refused, and the four older repeats still refuse a past time. A monthly or yearly entry gets `day` = the day of the `at` passed (so 2028-02-29 keeps 29).
- `fired` and `skipped` move an entry with its `day`.
- The file: a monthly or yearly entry gains the key `day` (an integer), and no other entry has it, so a file without the new repeats is written exactly as before and `FILE_VERSION` stays 1. Reading requires `day` on a monthly or yearly entry and refuses it on any other; it must be an integer (never a boolean) from 1 to 31 that `at` agrees with (`at.day == min(day, days in at's month)`), and a yearly entry's must exist in `at`'s month in a leap year (29 only in February). Anything else is not a schedule: the file is set aside as before, and the error names the entry and `day`, never its text.
- A Cyrene older than v1.43 reads a file with a weekly, monthly, or yearly entry as not a schedule: it renames the whole file to `schedule.json.corrupt-<stamp>` (never overwritten) and starts an empty schedule, so every entry is gone from it. The CHANGELOG says to cancel those entries before going back, as v1.32 did.

`scheduler.py`: a monthly or yearly reminder missed by more than the grace is told like a one-shot one (in the tick's one `sad` announcement, with its date when it was not today's) and pushed (`Terlewat (...)`); daily, weekdays, weekends, and weekly reminders are still only pushed, since they come again within a week. The entry moves on as before (`skipped`).

`brain/schedule_tools.py`:
- `schedule`: the `repeat` enum is `REPEATS`, so it gains the three values (strict, no new parameter). Its static description adds: weekly repeats on `at`'s weekday, monthly on `at`'s day of the month (in a month without that day, on its last day), and yearly on `at`'s month and day (29 February on 28 February in other years); for these three an `at` that has passed is fine (the entry starts at its next time) and a yearly one ignores the year; for a day the current month lacks ("tiap tanggal 31" said in November) pass the next month that has it, never the month's last day, which would become the day it repeats on; for 29 February pass a leap year's date such as 2028-02-29 (the year is ignored), never 28 February, since `parse_local_time` refuses 29 February of a common year and 28 February would become the day it repeats on; birthdays and anniversaries are yearly reminders with a reminder text like "'Master, hari ini ulang tahun Ibu!'" (the owner's call word), and bills monthly ("bayar listrik tiap tanggal 20", with "'Master, hari ini bayar listrik!'"), both at 08:00 unless Master says a time (the default the README and CHANGELOG promise). The literal guidance tests check ("weekdays (Monday-Friday)", "weekends (Saturday-Sunday)", ...) stays. The result of a monthly or yearly entry has `day` after `repeat`: `{"ok": true, "id": "j3", "at": "2027-05-05 08:00", "repeat": "yearly", "day": 5}`; a weekly entry's has `weekday` after `repeat`, the Indonesian weekday of `at` (`WEEKDAYS_ID`, as `journal_write` and `prayer_times` name theirs): `{"ok": true, "id": "j4", "at": "2026-10-26 07:00", "repeat": "weekly", "weekday": "Senin"}`, so she never works the weekday out from the date and a date miscounted for "tiap Senin" shows as "Selasa" in her confirmation (the description says to say the result's `weekday` for a weekly entry); the others are unchanged.
- `list_schedules {}` stays non-strict and without parameters. A monthly or yearly entry has `day` after `repeat`, a weekly one `weekday`, the others are unchanged. The description adds that for "jadwal bulan ini apa?" or "ulang tahun siapa bulan ini?" she picks from the list the entries that fall in that period herself, counting the repeating ones (a weekly one every week on its `weekday`); with at most `schedule.max_entries` (50) entries, the list fits a turn.

CLI: `waifu schedule list` and `waifu schedule cancel` say how an entry repeats from the entry: `sekali`, `tiap hari`, `tiap hari kerja`, `tiap akhir pekan` as before, `tiap Senin` (the weekday of `at`, `WEEKDAYS_ID`), `tiap tanggal 31` (`day`), `tiap 5 Mei` (`day` and the Indonesian month of `at`, `MONTHS_ID`: `tiap 29 Februari`).

Unchanged: `due`; the morning briefing (it lists the entries whose `at` is today, so a yearly reminder shows on its day when the greeting comes before it); routines (they only cancel their own one-shot steps); every older entry and its file format.

Docs: README "Jadwal, pengingat, dan rutinitas": examples for weekly, monthly, and yearly entries (birthdays, bills), the month-end and 29 February rules, the missed rule, and the `waifu schedule list` example; CHANGELOG v1.43 with the downgrade note.

Decisions:
- The day is stored, not derived: once clamped, `at` no longer tells an entry of the 30th from one of the 31st. Weekly needs nothing stored: the weekday of `at` never changes.
- A past `at` is accepted for the three new repeats: "tiap 5 Mei" said in October is a date that has passed this year, and refusing it would make the model guess a year. The older repeats keep refusing one (unchanged; a past daily time is more likely a wrong date).
- A yearly entry ignores the year of `at`: the model may pass this year's, next year's, or a leap year's date, and the next occurrence is always meant.
- A missed monthly or yearly reminder is told aloud: a daily or weekly one comes again within a week, a bill a month later, a birthday a year later, and without ntfy (off by default) a push reaches nobody. Device commands and routines missed stay silent, as before.
- No filter on `list_schedules`: the list holds at most 50 entries, each with its next time, repeat, and day, which is enough for the model to answer about a month, and a filter would have to decide what a daily entry means for "this month".

Known limits: "tiap tanggal 31" said in a month without a 31st relies on the model passing the next month that has one; given the month's last day (2026-11-30), the entry repeats on the 30th (cancel it and ask again). A yearly entry cannot start in a later year. Birthdays and bills count toward `schedule.max_entries` (50 by default, up to 200). An older version sets the file aside (above). A reminder still fires only while `waifu run` is on and the PC awake; a missed one is told once, when she is back.

Safety: tests use stores under tmp_path and fake clocks, never the repository's `data/schedule.json`, never a real device or an LLM; nothing is spoken or pushed.

### cuaca beberapa hari (v1.44)

Master asks about the weather ahead: "besok cuaca gimana?", "akhir pekan hujan nggak?", "nanti sore hujan?", "Jumat cuacanya gimana?". Until now she knew the weather now, today's range, and tomorrow at 07:00 (the briefing), and the rain alerts warn only shortly before a rain.

User stories:

1. "Cyrene, besok cuaca gimana?" → from her `weather_forecast` tool, in a sentence or two: "Besok di Cianjur pagi cerah, sorenya kemungkinan hujan, 19 sampai 28 derajat."
2. "Nanti sore hujan nggak?" → today's evening (sore, 15:00-19:00): its condition, rain chance, amount, and temperatures; "nanti malam?" → tonight (19:00-24:00); "nanti hujan nggak?" → the rest of today. A part of today that has passed ("nanti sore" asked at 20:00) is said to have passed.
3. "Akhir pekan hujan nggak?" → the coming Saturday and Sunday (on a Saturday the rest of today and Sunday, on a Sunday the rest of today).
4. "Jumat cuacanya gimana?" → any day from today to six days on; a later day is out of reach, and she says how far the forecast goes.
5. `waifu cuaca [hari-ini|besok|akhir-pekan|YYYY-MM-DD]` prints the same per day, with its parts.
6. Guest mode leaves it open: the weather of the city she already names holds nothing private.

Non-goals (v1.44): more than seven days; the exact hour of a rain (the parts and the rain alerts tell it; `waifu rain` prints it); a place other than the briefing's ("cuaca di Jakarta besok?"); wind, humidity, UV, sunrise and sunset; a change to the morning greeting, `daily_briefing`, `FORECAST_QUERY`, `waifu briefing`, or `waifu rain`; a cache between questions; the forecast in a turn she starts herself; a config switch of its own; a strict tool or a new strict parameter (18 of 20 strict tools, 17 of 24 optional parameters, and 0 of 16 unions stay); a dependency.

`weather.py` (extended minimally):
- `OUTLOOK_QUERY`: `current=weather_code` (asked only for the reply's `current.time`, the place's local time now), `daily=weather_code,temperature_2m_max,temperature_2m_min,precipitation_probability_max,precipitation_sum`, `hourly=weather_code,temperature_2m,precipitation_probability,precipitation`, `timezone=auto`, `forecast_days=7` (today and six more days). `FORECAST_QUERY` stays as it is, so the briefing and the rain watcher (every 15 minutes) ask exactly what they asked before.
- `Hour` gains `temperature_c: float | None = None`, its last field (None in `forecast()`, which does not ask for it).
- Values (frozen dataclasses): `DayOutlook(day: date, code: int | None, min_c: float | None, max_c: float | None, rain_chance: int | None, rain_mm: float | None)` and `Outlook(place: str, now: datetime, days: tuple[DayOutlook, ...], hours: tuple[Hour, ...])`: `now` is the reply's `current.time`, `days` every daily entry in order, `hours` every hourly entry with its temperature. A null value is None (Open-Meteo may leave a later day's probability null): unknown, never zero.
- `WeatherClient.outlook() -> Outlook`: one forecast request with `OUTLOOK_QUERY` (and the city lookup the first time per client, kept for the client's life), the same 8 s limit, `failure_level`, and errors as `today()` and `forecast()`. A reply without what it reads (a missing block or column, columns of different lengths, a date or time that is not ISO, a value that is neither a number nor null, no daily entry, no current time) is the unreadable `WeatherError`.
- `condition_of(code) -> str` (was `_condition`): a WMO code in Indonesian, the words the briefing uses.

The days and parts (`brain/weather_tools.py`, pure):
- Parts, with the hours `spoken_hour` gives their words: `morning` pagi 04:00-11:00, `afternoon` siang 11:00-15:00, `evening` sore 15:00-19:00, `night` malam 19:00-24:00 (dini hari, 00:00-04:00, is no part; the whole day covers it). Open-Meteo's hourly entry at T holds the hour before T, so the part from a to b reads the entries after a up to b, and night reads the next day's 00:00 entry.
- `Period(day: date, part: str | None, condition: str | None, min_c: float | None, max_c: float | None, rain_chance: int | None, rain_mm: float | None)` (frozen): `part` is the part's Indonesian word, `REST_OF_TODAY` ("sisa hari ini"), or None for a whole day.
- `period_of(outlook, day, part) -> Period | None`: the dates are the reply's, and `outlook.now`'s date is today (a place in WITA or WIT keeps its own).
